*Locked:* `adapter.laws.DstTimeZoneDiffTest` (instant diffs across both transitions, ambiguous→standard, gap formal error + suppression, chained-Add instant identity, UTC control, extraction control) + `adapter.laws.WallDayArithmeticDiffTest` (time-of-day-aware day counts incl. reverse truncation + mixed operands, time-preserving `AddDays`, both gap-landing directions) — kernel differentials, red-first (10 arms kernel-true/interpreter-false before the fix) — + multiplatform `interpreter … ModelTimeZoneTest` (loader mapping incl. the loud refusal, and the full Berlin semantics kernel-free, JVM + JS).

*Perf (2026-07-18, historical state at this finding):* `berlinOffsetSeconds` (`DateMath`) is on the hot path for every DATE/DATE_TIME conversion under a non-UTC model, yet it originally derived the year and DST boundaries via string round-trips. Replacing those with pure-integer civil-calendar math cut an ad-hoc Berlin/UTC probe from ~2.1× to ~1.26×; the helpers are equivalence-locked across the four-digit-year civil-calendar domain by `DateMathCivilArithmeticTest` on JVM + JS. At the IF128 landing this remained probe-only and the perf harness still dropped the model zone. IF131 subsequently fixed complete `DocumentConfig` propagation, IF132 added the committed Berlin + UTC scale scenarios and guards, and IF172 supplied the structured fair generated-Java evidence lane. Owner policy settled 2026-07-21: no candidate ratio is promoted now.

### IF129 — `computeField(fieldPath, doc): List<Computed>`, the single-computation library primitive (SHIPPED 2026-07-18, graduated from IG74) <a id="if129"></a>

`DmInterpreter` now exposes the single-*computation* twin of `testRule` — "run this computed field like a function" — closing the named module goal ("single-rule / **single-computation** eval", `interpreter/CLAUDE.md`). Before, computing one field meant `compute(doc).first { it.path == target }`: correct but it evaluated *every* sibling computation.

- **The mechanism is prune-and-reuse, not new semantics.** `computeField` runs the SAME cascade engine as `compute` (the extracted `ComputationEngine.computeOver(doc, comps)` — overlay / poison / cleared, the flattened first-selected scan IF87, the parallel join IF96) over a **pruned** computation list: the target's transitive computed-dependency **closure**, in dependency order. The closure comes from the shared topo-DFS `orderedFrom(roots)` (roots = every target → the whole-model order; root = one field → its upstream closure); `computeField` then projects `outcomeByCell` to the target field's own instances.
- **The governing invariant: `computeField(t, doc) == compute(doc).filter { it.path == t }`** — same instances, order, per-row indices, outcome bucket. Pruning to the UPSTREAM closure is behaviour-preserving because an unrelated computation cannot feed the target (downstream dependents are irrelevant to the target's value; a non-computed operand is just read). This is the cheap, kernel-free regression net; the kernel agreement rides on the whole-document `compute()` differential (already both-strategies-locked), projected per field.
- **Return shape corrected the ledger.** IG74 wrote `: Computed` (singular); the shipped signature is **`List<Computed>`** — a computed field in a **repeatable** group has one outcome per row, exactly why `compute()` returns a list, so the singular could not represent the common per-row case (owner-decided). A non-computed `fieldPath` (no computation targets it) throws `IllegalArgumentException` — a non-computed field has no outcome, mirroring `testRule`'s "no such rule" fail-fast.
- **Library-only.** A CLI surface (a verb / `--field` option) trips the earns-a-verb + conformance-matrix discipline (CLI-SPEC §6) and is scoped separately; no dmtool change. The kernel has no single-computation entry either (`IDocumentRtService.compute` is whole-document) — irrelevant since kernel eval retired (Set V) and the capability lives in `:interpreter`.

*Locked:* multiplatform `interpreter … ComputeFieldInvariantTest` (JVM + JS — the `computeField == compute`-projected invariant across a repeated target, a depth-2 cascade, poison propagation, empty-operand clearing, and the non-computed-field throw) + `adapter.laws.ComputeFieldDiffTest` (the compute differential: `computeField` == the real kernel's `compute()` projected per field, over clean / poison / empty scenarios).

### IF130 — low startup, high performance, and low RELATIVE footprint are separate regimes; bounded caches are an intended optimization (DECIDED 2026-07-18; numerical status corrected 2026-07-19) <a id="if130"></a>

The durable decision separates three regimes that earlier performance prose mixed together: **first-answer latency** (fresh process, engine construction and lazy initialization included), **warm throughput/scaling** (prepared engines, correctness checked before timing, with generated Java as the fair relative baseline), and **relative footprint** (dependency/artifact size, cache ownership and plateau, retained-memory/allocation growth, and native-vs-JVM delivery). [INTERPRETER-SPEC.md](INTERPRETER-SPEC.md) owns the product contract and [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) the method/evidence. The exact numbers introduced with this finding were not backed by an earlier owner decision or protocol-compliant series; they are informative candidate budgets until per-number certified (IF137), not settled acceptance bars.

**“Low footprint” does not mean minimum memory or an arbitrary small heap.** Parsed-AST caches, document indices, hash maps, row-context memoization, and other bounded memory-intensive structures are allowed and advised when they reduce repeated work, improve the complexity class, or materially improve startup/throughput. The invariant is that cached state has a clear owner/invalidation boundary, reaches a stable warm plateau, grows in its declared space class, and earns regressions against the previous release or equivalent kernel-backed evaluator. The proposed 10%/20% review triggers belong to the numerical decision (IF137), not this timeless principle. The invalidation premise is now upheld (IF142 closed the one violation). Exact kernel parity remains prior to every NFR.

The historical timings make several candidates plausible but certify none: warm growth used best minima rather than the later ratified median estimator, startup samples excluded required work, and the 33/119 MB delivery pair predates equivalent interpreter capability. The numerical/product decision is ratified (IF137); IF172 supplies the executable performance evidence lane and IF173 the same-collector delivery comparison. No candidate may be reported as a green release gate without its six-point certification and owner ratification.

### IF131 — `DocumentConfig`: the evaluator configuration is ONE immutable value, so every `Document` reconstruction preserves it wholesale (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG75) <a id="if131"></a>

The 14 evaluator-configuration inputs a `Document` carries (kinds / reps / formats / scale / enum / precision / base-year / constraint / **time-zone**) are now a single immutable `DocumentConfig`. `Document`'s only constructors are `Document(DocumentConfig)` and a kinds-only convenience `Document(kinds)`; the former 14-argument positional constructor is **removed**. This makes the IG75 class of bug unrepresentable: a copy passes the one `config` object, so it cannot drop a slot.

- **The defect it fixes.** `Document.emptyCopy()` (behind `withComputed` → `applyComputations`) rebuilt positionally through only 13 of 14 arguments and silently dropped `timeZone` → `UTC`. So a `Europe/Berlin` document became UTC after compute→apply.
- **Reachable impact is narrow (the corrected scope, IG75 review 2026-07-19).** Ordinary evaluation and the emitted formal-error diagnostics read `EvalConfig.timeZone` / `AutoChecks.formalErrors` — both from the (unaffected) `ModelDef` — so `validate` is bit-identical before/after `applyComputations` (probe-verified: the spring-forward `DifferenceInHours` and the spring-gap `datumFormatFalsch` both agree). The Document's `timeZone` feeds only `Document.formallyInvalid`/`cellState` (the not-check-relevant gate + the IF85 compute-poison trigger), so the only reachable divergence is datetime VALUE-resolution inside that gate on an applied document — an aggregate/fill over a spring-gap DATE_TIME, narrow and not exhibited by a simple validate. The **perf-harness sibling was the more material defect**: `InterpreterPerfEngine` (and `scenario.interpModel`) built a `Document` from six inputs, dropping constraints/zone/etc., so "real Document" perf scenarios ran semantically incomplete — now they build via `newDocument()`.
- **The migration.** ~85 call sites moved off the positional constructor: `Document(def.kinds, def.declaredReps, …)` → `def.newDocument()` (the blessed full-config path), and the handful of ad-hoc maps → `DocumentConfig(…)` with named arguments (conspicuous, never a silent positional drop). Partial construction is now impossible or explicit — the standing "make illegal states unrepresentable" rule applied to `Document`.

*Locked:* `interpreter … DocumentConfigPreservationTest` (JVM + JS—every slot preserved through `withComputed` by data-class equality, plus an explicit pin that a Europe/Berlin document stays Berlin); the full `:adapter` differential estate stays green across the migrated call sites. At this landing it unblocked the Document side of the then-open IG80; IF132 subsequently completed the model/perf side.

### IF132 — the Europe/Berlin DATE_TIME instant path is perf-gated: a committed scenario exercises `berlinOffsetSeconds` at scale (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG80) <a id="if132"></a>

The datetime-instant hot path — `berlinOffsetSeconds` (the civil-calendar offset `epochDayOfCivil`/`yearOfEpochDay`, IF128/IF131) run once per DATE/DATE_TIME conversion under a non-UTC model — is now exercised by a **committed** benchmark, not just a discarded hand-probe. Before this the whole `PerfCatalog` carried no DATE_TIME field, so the zone axis was inert and the IF128 optimization's win was free to silently regress.

- **The scenario.** `PerfCatalog.berlinDateTime()` — a per-row `DifferenceInHours(StartedAt, EndedAt) > 0` rule over `Europe/Berlin` DATE_TIME fields on a large repetition list — plus `utcDateTimeControl()` (the same shape with the zone inert). Values are mid-June (constant CEST, **non-DST-straddling**), so Berlin and UTC agree on the difference (interp == kernel holds bit-for-bit) while the offset path still runs per conversion.
- **Two committed guards.** `PerfGateTest.berlinDateTimeStaysSubQuadratic` is the interpreter-only declared-linear catastrophe guard under the shared paired-median estimator and loose 9× trip line; it is not a certified NFR-P1 result. The B9-berlin case in `PerfBenchTest.gatesEveryInventoryScenario` (derived from `PerfInventory`) is the Groovy comparison: interp == kernel, with the historical pre-correction run recording ratio ≈0.29 at N=3000 and Berlin/UTC delta ≈1.37×.
- **The performance handoff.** At the IF132 landing, the routine bench was Groovy-only and no fair kernel-Java B9 result existed. IF158 subsequently put the B9 Berlin/UTC pair in the closed declared inventory with its generated-Java leg, and IF172 added the pinned-runner NFR-P2 reporting lane. The candidate ratio remains informative; owner policy currently promotes no candidate number.

*Locked:* `adapter.perf.PerfGateTest.berlinDateTimeStaysSubQuadratic` (declared-linear growth) + the B9-berlin case in `adapter.perf.PerfBenchTest.gatesEveryInventoryScenario` (interp == kernel + ratio + Berlin/UTC delta; the case is registered in `PerfInventory`); the two civil-calendar helpers' correctness is separately locked by `interpreter … DateMathCivilArithmeticTest` (JVM + JS).

### IF133 — the perf comments describe the current architecture: parse-once, IF20 constraint parity, IF116-memoized indexed reads (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG78; follow-up cleanup closed by IF141) <a id="if133"></a>

The `:adapter/perf` comments had drifted behind three shipped changes; they now match the code:

- **Parse-once, not per-call re-parse.** `ParsedModel` parses each rule's condition (and each computation) to an AST **exactly once, cached** at `DmInterpreter` construction (`ruleCondAst` = `Map<RuleDef, Lazy<Cond>>`) — the "#31 per-call re-parse" cost is retired. The kernel's edge in the many-rules / batch regimes is now stated correctly: its compile-once codegen (JIT-compiled rules) vs the interpreter's per-call **tree-walk of the cached ASTs** (`PerfHarness`, `PerfCatalog` B6/`manyRules`, `KernelEdgeTest`).
- **IF20 constraint parity.** The interpreter auto-validates the same declared field-type constraints as the kernel (range/length/pattern/enum-domain — IF20, locked by `FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest`), so `PerfBounds.CAVEAT` dropped its stale "kernel also validates field-type constraints" fairness clause (the ratio's residual unfairness is only the excluded codegen + the Groovy tax).
- **IF116-memoized indexed reads.** `PerfCatalog.semanticIndexPerRow`'s comment framed the raw indexed read as *present-tense* O(rows²); IF116 memoizes the indexed-read column per (column, scope) at the document level, so it is O(rows) now — reworded to the historical + guarded framing (`PerfGateTest.semanticIndexStaysSubQuadratic` locks it).

**Three of IG78's cited items were verified NOT stale** (so they were left unchanged, recorded here to prevent re-investigation): `PerfScalingTest` already says the interpreter pays "a lazy index build + first parse" (one-time, correct); `EvalProfileTest` attributes cost to eval / `firedType` / `iterationContexts` / allocations (not re-parse or codegen); and `DmInterpreter.validatePart`'s KDoc has two distinct paragraphs, not a duplicated sentence. Comments-only—no behavior changed. A separate lifecycle defect—six Kotlin comment references that still named deleted IG80 rather than IF132—was subsequently closed by IF141.

### IF134 — the 2026-07-18 NFR commit was a useful regime split but not a ratified or certified numerical contract (DOCUMENTATION AUDIT 2026-07-19) <a id="if134"></a>

Commit `6eba3f46` (`docs(interpreter): define realistic non-functional requirements`, 2026-07-18 23:33 +0200) is the only commit that introduced or changed the NFR contract in `INTERPRETER-SPEC.md`; `git log -S` confirms every exact latency, ratio, footprint, exponent, and review-trigger number first appeared there. Its qualitative separation is sound: first-answer latency, warm throughput/scaling, and relative footprint are distinct; generated Java is the fair warm comparator; bounded caches are intended. Its evidence labels were not sound enough to make the numbers release requirements.

- **Product contract vs measurement at this audit.** No earlier owner decision or SLO supported the exact numbers, and the then-current absolute cases did not identify a versioned model, operation, value/firing regime, output cardinality, locale, or zone. Their warm-growth evidence used best minima rather than the proposed paired medians. IF137 subsequently ratified the posture/estimator/promotion gate, IF156–IF159 shipped prepared eligibility, the paired estimator, content-bound identities/declared inventory, and the comparator guard, IF172 completed the ordinary executable performance lane, and IF173 completed the functional delivery-artifact comparison lane. Owner policy settled 2026-07-21: promote no candidate number now; keep `scripts/perf.sh gate` isolated in nightly/manual use and the complete IF172 collector manual.
- **Footprint provenance.** The 33 MB native / 119 MB JVM pair was measured 2026-06-15, before interpreter-backed native evaluation existed. It is cross-revision and not an established equal-capability pair, so it remains historical plausibility only. IF161/IF162 now own deterministic/forked runtime evidence and IF173 rebuilds current/previous functional delivery bases with one collector. Corrected 2026-07-26: R3.3 added the real packed npm and staged Maven bases after their external consumer gates; the earlier absence remains true only for archived pre-package revisions and is represented explicitly by protocol v2.
- **Architecture truth.** The desired rule is that all document-derived caches share the document's mutation invalidation boundary. The live implementation violated it: `rowContextsCache` and `correlationBucketCache` survived `Document.add`; fixed by IF142 (one invalidation method clears all three document-derived caches; locked by `DocumentMutationCacheInvalidationTest`).
- **Certificate truth at the end of this NFR audit.** IF128 closed the exercised modern-Berlin regime, not the kernel's full accepted timezone domain (IG83). G1′ was a loud-refused included-reference class and is now fully closed by the reference-aware move-remap and tri-engine include lock (IF146); the commonMain assembler residual later completed as IF224. The follow-on IF135 audit found further source/evidence gaps; the verdict is historical green-or-documented-equivalent on the exercised surface, never “fully GREEN” over every legal input.
- **Perf/doc state at this audit.** IF132 added B9 and raised the then-current interpreter-only growth-guard count to thirteen; its fair generated-Java lane had not landed yet (IF158 subsequently added it). Validation's logical walk is strict while computation preconditions short-circuit under IF85. The two-phase/dispatch/unboxing/vectorization ideas are historical or profile-earned candidates, not open gaps. Six closed-IG80 references were deliberately outside this Markdown-only pass and were subsequently closed by IF141.
- **Distribution truth.** At this audit, dependency hygiene made the KMP module publication-eligible but not published. Corrected 2026-07-26: R3.1–R3.3 stage Maven/npm artifacts and lock their ABI/content/delivery boundaries. Corrected 2026-07-27: Node and system Chrome are qualified hosts under their distinct gates (IF224/IF225), and IF226 completes anonymous public-GitHub consumption.

This audit reviewed all ten documents touched by `6eba3f46` and corrected only documentation. It changed no evaluator, harness, build, or script behavior.

### IF135 — a source-first certificate sweep exposed the dimensions that operator-count and prepared-corpus evidence do not cover (DOCUMENTATION AUDIT 2026-07-19) <a id="if135"></a>

The follow-on audit checked the live common/JVM/JS implementation, CLI preparation paths, differential/corpus machinery, performance harness, and certificate claims rather than inferring completeness from `110/110`. It established five durable boundaries:

- **Handled operator inventory is not legal-input coverage.** `CatalogParityTest` derives callable dispatch/fuzz classification, but modifiers, boolean placement, computation metadata, model configuration values, platform dialects, assembly forms, mutation sequences, and dynamic/generated-Java evidence are separate axes. The original certificate's per-test name sweep was useful but manual; IF140 now owns the derived capability × strategy × coverage-tier matrix.
- **The common loader consumes a prepared representation, and now ENFORCES it.** `ModelLoader` requires name-path ids and does not assemble includes/imported typedefs; it no longer degrades unresolved residue silently — an unexpanded include, an unresolved `TypeDefType`, or an opaque id is rejected loud (IF145, `requirePrepared`). **Updated 2026-07-26:** every dmtool CLI runtime consumer, including `model seed`, now collects and assembles through commonMain `ModelWorkspace`, then crosses the strict expanded-input gate; the corpus preparer's separate test-only transformation pipeline remains non-production evidence.
- **Common Kotlin source does not erase host semantic differences.** Legal combined charset sequences were flattened (fixed — atomic trie matching, IF151); decimal and regex required explicit host-semantics work (completed by IF163/IF164); and the timezone implementation covers UTC/GMT plus the full pinned Berlin history under the enforced Route-B contract (IF155/IF165), while the kernel's wider accepted id domain remains owner-deferred IG83 Route A.
- **Modeled mechanism is not always exact observable behavior.** Custom-field rejection suppression existed before its project code (IF150), observable validator context, one-call cardinality, and message-template semantics were complete (IF152). Legal `RepetitionNotUnique` under `Or`/nested boolean placement degraded unsupported (fixed — RNU is now a 3VL leaf composed by the boolean walk, IF149); computation `toleranceRangeOp` was dropped (fixed — the implicit validation now uses the tolerance band, IF148); and the messages-only `validateFull` convenience discarded unsupported reports (fixed — it now fails loud, IF139). A bare-group compute quantifier's ordered poison behavior was a source-predicted discriminator; probed, confirmed, and fixed as the declaration-order early-stop scan (IF147).
- **Performance evidence has both confirmed and unproven hard cases.** Compute overlays formerly rebuilt semantic-index columns and equality-correlation buckets per row (confirmed worst-case O(rows²)); now cached per computation step (IF143, was IG60). Equality and single-comparator outer correlations have indexed plans and sub-quadratic guards (IF47/IF159); arbitrary compound filters retain an explicit general fallback. Multi-star equality correlation uses the bind-depth-aware hash join, including explicit bind-depth 0/2 locks (IF144). IF164 compiles distinct model-static patterns once and guards pattern-heavy N/4N growth; IF171 locks the other declared preparation classes. Any newly suspected model-static work still requires profile evidence rather than speculative implementation.

The audit also broadened the then-open IG82 from six stale IG80 references to the source-comment lifecycle class and corrected the kernel regex/timezone canonical docs. IF141 subsequently closed that comment-drift class. This audit itself changed Markdown only—no evaluator, harness, build, or script behavior.

### IF136 — the ratified oracle on a legal kernel-strategy split: Groovy-dynamic is normative, a static-Java split is recorded and never adopted (OWNER DECISION 2026-07-19, graduated from IG76) <a id="if136"></a>

The module carried a contradiction about which kernel strategy is authoritative when the two diverge on a **legal** input: `interpreter/CLAUDE.md` said the interpreter matches "production codegen" while [CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC.md](CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC.md) §11 anchored `expected` on the Groovy-dynamic runtime and recorded a static strategy as a *divergence, never adopted*. Nothing forced a resolution because the tri-engine estate agrees everywhere (0 corpus `divergences` cases; 0 java-static splits in the corpus sweep). The owner ratified the anchor and corrected the framing.

- **The decision.** The normative anchor is **Groovy-dynamic** (the kernel's dynamic md-runtime service — the production-normative strategy). On a legal-input divergence the interpreter matches Groovy-dynamic; the **static-Java** codegen result is recorded as a characterized divergence (`divergences[kernel-java-static]`) and **never adopted**.
- **The framing correction (owner).** The split axis is **dynamic-Groovy vs static-Java**, *not* "runtime vs codegen." The old "production codegen wins" wording was wrong on both counts — wrong winner and wrong axis label.
- **Why this is ratifying reality, not new churn.** The differential estate already gates the interpreter against the Groovy-dynamic runtime (`RuntimeLaws`/`KernelAdapter`), and the published corpus already anchors `expected` on Groovy-dynamic. Choosing dynamic aligns the *prose* with what the *code and corpus already enforce*; it edits sentences, not the estate.
- **The enforcement machinery (already complete, now locked).** `InterpreterReplay` always asserts the top-level anchor and ignores `divergences`; `CorpusReplay`'s java-static leg applies a `divergences[kernel-java-static]` override when present (`case.overrideFor(JAVA_STATIC) ?: anchor`). The pure selection `overrideFor` was lifted into `commonMain` next to the `Divergence` type so it is engine-neutral and multiplatform-testable. No real split exists to capture (the certificate's 0 java-static splits), so the lock is a machinery lock with a synthetic split; the first real captured divergence inherits the same enforcement.
- **Locks.** `interpreter … conformance.ConformanceDivergenceEnforcementTest` (JVM + JS): the interpreter matches the Groovy-dynamic anchor even with a static-Java divergence recorded, a divergence override never becomes the interpreter's expectation, and `overrideFor` hands the override only to a static-Java replay while never overriding the anchor. `CaseJsonTest` already round-trips a `divergences` entry (serialization); this adds the enforcement it lacked.

Aligned in the same change: `interpreter/CLAUDE.md` #1 rules, `CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC.md` §11, `TESTING-SPEC.md` codegen-tri-differential row, the certificate's fuzz-net note, and the NFR-oracle references in [INTERPRETER-SPEC.md](INTERPRETER-SPEC.md)/[INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) (the perf preflight now names the ratified anchor, not "the IG76 decision").

### IF137 — the numerical NFR contract: informative until per-number certified; `PerfBounds` is the only ratified NFR/release floor (OWNER DECISION 2026-07-19, graduated from IG84) <a id="if137"></a>

The exact-number family (JVM `200/400/1,500 ms`, Node `350 ms`, native `750 ms`, warm `1,000 ms`, `6×` growth, generated-Java `1.25`, closure `50%`, native/JVM `40%`, space-exponent `1.25`, and the sampling/fork/job values) entered in one commit (`6eba3f46`) with **no prior owner SLO** (`git log -S` finds none) and was supported by protocol-non-compliant measurements (best minima, one-shot startup, pre-interpreter footprint). IG84 asked whether these are release bars. The owner ratified the **posture**, not the numbers.

- **Posture: informative until per-number certified.** Every NFR-S/P/F number is an **informative candidate**, not a release-blocking bar. This preserves regression protection without presenting unmeasured candidates as certified SLOs.
- **Ratified NFR/release floor: `PerfBounds` only.** Its sub-quadratic growth guards (`<9×`) + reduced against-kernel correctness canaries (`<5 s`/`<5×`) form the ratified catastrophe floor. Other executable implementation-regression assertions remain in common/default/tagged lanes; they are diagnostics/backstops, not certified NFR bars. **Owner correction:** the fair generated-Java `≤1.25×` ratio is not an executable gate (it is a portable warm-performance signal, informative alongside the other candidate numbers). A green floor is never NFR certification.
- **The per-number promotion gate (all six).** A number becomes a release bar independently, only after: (1) its scenario and estimator are fixed; (2) the runner/runtime is pinned; (3) the required p95/median sampling is performed; (4) semantic preflight passes — `unsupported.isEmpty()` + tri-engine agreement with the ratified Groovy-dynamic anchor (IF136); (5) the measurement is reproducible; (6) the owner ratifies it as a release bar.
- **The ratified method.** The paired growth estimator is **interleaved N/4N samples with the median of within-pair ratios**. Every tighter design target is **informative** (not gating). The recommended sampling defaults (≥50 fresh processes for a p95, ≥5 forks for footprint, the job/miss policy) are **not globally normative** — they bind a number only at its promotion. The reference runner + "equivalent capability" comparator are pinned alongside the first executable certification.
- **What this decision did NOT do.** The IF137 decision itself bound no scenario, calibrated no number, and built no gate; it fixed the contract posture. IF156–IF159 later added the performance eligibility/estimator/identity/inventory/correlation machinery, IF172 completed the ordinary executable performance lane, and IF173 completed the functional delivery-artifact comparison lane. Owner policy now promotes no candidate number, keeps `scripts/perf.sh gate` isolated in nightly/manual use, and leaves the complete collector manual. No lock was added by IF137 itself because its deliverable was a documented owner decision, not code behavior.

Aligned in the same change: [INTERPRETER-SPEC.md](INTERPRETER-SPEC.md) Non-functional contract (posture + the six-point gate + the informative-ratio note), [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) protocol/gate-mechanics/staging, `interpreter/CLAUDE.md`, `TESTING-SPEC.md`, `NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM.md`, IF130/IF134's audit prose, and the IG60/IG77/IG79/IG98 ledger rows (all now cite the ratified estimator/contract, not "IG84").

### IF138 — Node was the first supported JS runtime; the browser lane required an actual consumer and independent evidence (OWNER DECISION 2026-07-19; activated 2026-07-26 and completed by IF224/IF225) <a id="if138"></a>

`:interpreter` compiles Kotlin/JS and the shared suite runs under `js { nodejs() }` — strong Node evidence, not browser evidence: there is no `browser()` target, browser smoke/conformance run, bundled consumer fixture, or browser first-answer measurement. Pure `commonMain` makes browser support *plausible*, not *proven* (module format, npm `big.js` loading, bundling, and host `RegExp`/decimal are all unverified). The owner decided the scope rather than building a speculative lane.

- **The decision.** **Node is the SUPPORTED JS runtime.** A **browser host is an unverified future capability, deferred** — not a claim the project makes today.
- **Why defer, not build.** There is no browser consumer to justify a `browser()` target + bundling + conformance-replay + a scoped browser perf bar; building it now would be speculative. Decimal and regex host semantics are complete under IF163/IF164, and IF164's translator stays within the browser-safe ES2018 design constraint.
- **The lock.** The source-of-truth over-claim (`interpreter/build.gradle` said the module "runs … in the browser") is corrected to name Node as the supported runtime and browser as deferred; every doc that treated Node as browser proof now says so. The deferred residual is tracked in [INTERPRETER-GAPS.md](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md) §2, not §1 — it is an owner-accepted capability residual, not a silent mismatch.
- **Re-open trigger.** A real browser consumer materializes, or the publication wave promises browser support. Then: add a `browser()` target + a bundled consumer smoke, replay the portable conformance subset in a real browser, and give browser first-answer its own explicitly scoped bar (Node may remain a separate CI proxy only after equivalence is demonstrated).

Corrected 2026-07-27: the re-open trigger fired and the entire evidence gate completed. R4.1 registers the browser environment and executes the real packed tarball through a Vite bundle in system Chrome under the repository-relative Pages base. R4.2 replays all 33 portable Kernel-grounded corpus cases, rejects every unsupported result, prepares the exact four-source raw include/typedef workspace through finite and asynchronous collection, and locks decimal, Java-pattern/grapheme, and Europe/Berlin DST host behavior in the same real browser. IF225 adds the owner-ratified median ≤250 ms bar under an adaptive 10→50 fresh-process protocol on the pinned Apple M1/Chrome runner. The historical deferral remains accurate for its date; current browser support is qualified by IF224/IF225.

Aligned in the same change: `interpreter/build.gradle`, `interpreter/CLAUDE.md`, [INTERPRETER-SPEC.md](INTERPRETER-SPEC.md), [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md), [INTERPRETER-ARCHITECTURE.md](INTERPRETER-ARCHITECTURE.md), IF134's distribution note, the IG82 row (its browser over-claim item), and the OPERATOR-API proposal cross-ref.

### IF139 — `validateFull` fails loudly on unsupported rules, so the messages-only convenience can't produce a false-clean result (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG97) <a id="if139"></a>

`DmInterpreter.validate(doc)` returns `ValidationOutcome(messages, unsupported)`; the `validateFull(doc)` convenience is the messages-only kernel twin. It used to return `.messages` and silently drop `.unsupported` — so a legal-but-unmodeled construct or a missing external SPI (a `CustomCondition` with no registered factory) could look like a CLEAN validation to a library caller. The kernel can never reach that state (it supports every construct), so this false-clean was interpreter-specific and invisible to a message-only differential.

- **The fix.** `validateFull` now **throws** (`IllegalStateException`, via `check`) when `unsupported` is non-empty, naming the unsupported rules + their reasons and pointing the caller to `validate(doc)` (the outcome-bearing API) or `validate(doc).messages` (an explicit, on-purpose messages-only projection). The empty-unsupported path — every real kernel-equivalent case — is unchanged, so it stays the ergonomic kernel twin.
- **Blast radius at IF139 landing.** All ~25 then-current `validateFull` callers (interpreter commonTest + the perf harness) used supported-only models, so `unsupported` was empty and they did not throw; the full `:interpreter:check` (JVM+JS) and the then-current against-kernel perf canary stayed green. RV-2026-07-20-03 later showed that a projected `interp == kernel` assertion was not a sufficient eligibility invariant because the projection could discard unsupported outcomes; IF156 now requires exact outcome agreement before timing and refuses an unsupported projection.
- **Why fail-loud, not deprecate.** `validateFull` is the natural, ergonomic kernel twin and is correct on every input the kernel could handle; deprecating it would force `validate(doc).messages` everywhere, including the safe case. Fail-loud closes the safety hole precisely where the kernel can't help (the interpreter-only unsupported state) and matches `testRule`'s loud contract.
- **Lock.** `interpreter … ValidateFullUnsupportedSafetyTest` (JVM + JS) covers both siblings: a legal-but-unmodeled composed `RepetitionNotUnique` placement and a missing `CustomCondition` registration make `validateFull` throw a corrective error naming the rule + pointing to `validate`, while tolerant `validate` still surfaces each report and a supported-only model returns messages unchanged. `adapter … ValidateFullUnsupportedSafetyDiffTest` first sends the composed-rule discriminator through the real `RuleValidator`, then proves it reaches the interpreter's unsupported/fail-loud path; this prevents an interpreter-only fixture from being mistaken for legal kernel input.
- **Feeds IF140.** A message-only differential cannot prove that nothing was skipped, so this unsupported-safety lock is an explicit excluded-and-locked cell in IF140's derived capability × strategy evidence matrix.

### IF140 — the capability × strategy evidence matrix: derived, compile-checked, tiered, and certificate-generating (SHIPPED 2026-07-19; graduated from IG94) <a id="if140"></a>

`CatalogParityTest` makes operator dispatch coverage a derived guard, but the certificate's "every construct has a differential against both strategies" rested on a manual test-name sweep. That could neither account for non-operator axes nor prove that each cited test really exercised Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, and the interpreter. IF140 replaces that claim with a mechanical accounting.

- **The complete matrix.** `adapter.laws.CapabilityEvidenceMatrixTest` derives **110 operator cells** from the live catalog and checks them through `CatalogOperatorStrategyDiffTest` or the exact specialist differential. Its independent non-operator universe covers formal, computation, configuration, customization, platform, and assembly: **18 tri-engine evidence cells plus 6 explicit reasoned exclusions**, for **134 accounted cells** total. Exclusions never inflate evidence counts; IF139's unsupported-result safety is an excluded kernel-inapplicable cell with its actual fail-loud lock. IF146's included-reference preparation has its own `IncludeAssemblyStrategyDiffTest` cell rather than borrowing the unrelated prepared typedef/id cell.
- **Truthful evidence.** Every evidence cell names its real test by `KClass`, so a rename or deletion breaks compilation. The 13 formerly missing static-Java checks were added to their dedicated differentials. Cross-root computation points at `MultiRootComputationPointerDiffTest`; ordinary operators are evaluated as one live-catalog-derived model by the interpreter, Groovy-dynamic, and generated Java rather than inferred from the partial fuzz net.
- **Omission and tier locks.** The non-operator `CapabilityId` universe is declared independently from the matrix; removing a row, adding a new universe entry without classifying it, duplicating a row, or leaving an axis empty fails. Coverage tiers name always-on JVM strategy differentials, always-on JVM+JS portable replay, and the opt-in JVM real-corpus sweep.
- **Generated certificate evidence.** `certificateSummaryIsGeneratedFromTheMatrix` writes `adapter/build/reports/capability-evidence/certificate-summary.md` from the live cells and mechanically checks the live-operator and total-cell counts. The durable certificate summarizes those derived results rather than maintaining a hand-counted competing inventory.
- **Locks.** `CapabilityEvidenceMatrixTest`, `CatalogOperatorStrategyDiffTest`, the 13 dedicated tri-engine differentials, IF139's JVM+JS safety lock and kernel-legality bridge, and the existing portable/corpus tier tests. IG94 is fully drained and its ledger row is deleted.

### IF141 — the deleted-gap reference guard and bounded executable-comment sweep (SHIPPED 2026-07-19; graduated from IG82) <a id="if141"></a>

Executable comments outlive the gaps they cite: an `IGn` that graduated to an `IFn` keeps reading as an *open* gap to every maintainer who meets the comment, and only an expensive manual audit catches it. The gap's hard constraint is that most `IGn` references in code are **legitimate provenance** — the codebase carries ~250 bare origin-labels ("the IG44 drill", "the IG56 E3 hunt", "IG13 residual") that must NOT be touched. A naive "reject every deleted-`IGn`" guard fired on all 250 (empirically confirmed): it would ban exactly the history the gap protects.

- **The mechanical signal (the crux).** Current-state vs. history is distinguished by **present-tense-OPEN framing**, not by an IF link. A deleted gap cited as `pending IGn` / `awaits IGn` / `future work … IGn` / `not yet … IGn` / `TODO … IGn` is a **current-state claim that misleads**; a bare descriptive cite is history. Classification is per reference inside a logical block or consecutive-line comment, so a historical token cannot exempt another open token on the same line and wrapped wording remains visible.
- **The guard.** `cli.StaleGapReferenceGuardTest` and its focused `StaleGapReferenceClassifierTest` run in the dedicated `staleGapGuardTest` task. The source/build universe and Gradle task inputs are derived across the root and every current module, including the shared kit modules; an interpreter contract in `core` is no longer invisible. A violation requires an `IGn` absent from the live ledger, a nearest open marker in the same clause, and no nearer historical marker. Focused canaries lock mixed references, wrapped comments, capitalization and `does not (yet)`, live/deleted IDs, explicit history, and the positive detection path. The old arbitrary minimum-live-gap count is replaced by ledger title/section/row-shape/duplicate validation, so legitimate graduations cannot break the guard.
- **What it caught + the sweep.** The original increment caught `FuzzDiagnostics` "excluded **pending IG20**" (IF42 closed that boundary), removed its vestigial `ignoreCodes`/`filterCodes` mechanism, and repointed the six `IG80` datetime-perf cites to `IF132`. The expanded source universe then caught both `ModelReader` comments that still described the A4 refusal boundary as IG67; they were repointed at executed IF122 (the residual reference shapes later shipped via the move-remap, IF146).
- **The scan universe is CODE, and prose resists the same mechanism (measured 2026-07-29).** `isScannable` admits `.kt`/`.java`/`.gradle` only, and the classifier reads *logical comments* (`/* */` blocks, `//` runs) — markdown has neither, so pointing the guard at `docs/` scans nothing, and `docs/*.md` carries 386 `//` occurrences (mostly `https://`) that would each open a bogus comment run. Simulating a markdown mode — paragraph-as-clause-container, plus the `stays|remains open` wording the vocabulary lacks (no code site pairs it with an `IG` ref, so extending it is code-safe) — found **4 real wording defects, all corrected** — `IG44 stays open`, `IG89 remains open`, a `Recorded as IG61 pending …` inside the very finding IG61 graduated into, and (caught by an independent cold audit, not by the probe) `pending relativization (IG67)` in `KERNEL-DECOUPLING-PROPOSAL`, whose row had graduated as IF122. **No exhaustive residual was established, and the attempt to state one was itself the lesson:** the candidate set is *container*-sensitive. Splitting a paragraph into punctuation-delimited clauses — the shape the classifier uses for comments — yields 5 candidates and **misses** the IG67 site, because `(IG67)` is a parenthetical that the split severs from its `pending` marker; taking the whole line as the container yields 12, of which most are false (the open-marker on that line governs something else entirely). So a prose-capable guard needs a container decision *plus* carve-outs for quoted spans and resolved/archived sections, each a place it silently under-checks — which is why the wording was fixed by hand instead. Two durable lessons: the mechanical signal works on terse comments because a comment *is* a clause, whereas prose freely quotes and narrates its own history; and honest past-tense framing still trips a clause scanner when it keeps an open-vocabulary token (`was still open` contains `still open`), so historical wording should drop the token, not merely re-tense around it. *(Corrected 2026-07-29: this bullet first claimed 5 candidates with "the residual is exactly 2, both legitimate" — an exhaustiveness claim one container could not support, and it named as untouchable a site the same commit had already rewritten.)*
- **Final sweep and closure.** The bounded non-mechanical residuals were corrected in the closing pass: IG21 citations now point to IF44; the Unicode-length and unspecified-interpolation locks point to KERNEL-FINDINGS §7 and IF121; five parser-test comments describe `Dec` value equality under IF41; `TestRuleDiffTest` points SUPPRESSED coverage to `SuppressionDiffTest`; `Model.kt` names the shipped `CustomFieldType` checksum SPI; and the three named performance comments describe the current lazy-polarity walk, equality hash join, and interpreter-only startup clock. IG82 is fully drained and its row is deleted. The later IF153 review separately reopened IG89; that is a decimal-contract finding, not a regression of this comment-lifecycle closure.

### IF142 — a `Document` mutation invalidates EVERY document-derived cache, not just the lookup index (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG81) <a id="if142"></a>

`Document.add` cleared `cachedIndex` but left two document-level caches warm: **`rowContextsCache`** (a `Having` filter's per-row `EvalContext`s) and **`correlationBucketCache`** (a `$` equality-correlation's hash buckets). Both are keyed by structure, not just the index, and both survived mutation. The internal eval path never mutates after querying (its comment even asserted "the document is immutable during eval"), so the differential never hit it — but the `Document` API is **public**, and a library consumer's query → mutate → query reused stale row sets / `$`-correlation counts. This broke the architecture + NFR-F2 invariant that document-derived caches are invalidated with their owner.

- **The root fix (the class, not a symptom).** Every mutation routes through **one** `invalidateDerivedCaches()` that clears all three caches; `add` calls it in place of the lone `cachedIndex = null`. The method is the single place a mutation clears, so a *future* document-derived cache is added there or it regresses — the class is closed, not the one reported symptom.
- **Design choice.** The row offered "move the caches behind `Document.Index` OR one complete invalidation method"; the one-method form holds the invariant without the larger `Index` refactor (the caches stay on `Document`, cleared together on mutation).
- **Lock (both caches, JVM + JS).** `interpreter … DocumentMutationCacheInvalidationTest`: `havingRowContextCacheIsInvalidatedOnMutation` (a `Having` fill quantifier — a fresh hot row fires `HOTQ`, a stale `rowContextsCache` stays silent) and `correlationBucketCacheIsInvalidatedOnMutation` (a `$`-correlated `MoreThanOneFieldFilled` — a second same-tag row makes a fresh doc fire, a stale bucket counts one → silent). Each warms, mutates, re-queries, and asserts equality with an equivalently rebuilt fresh document — both go red on the old code, green on the fix. The full `:interpreter:check` (JVM+JS) and a star/Having adapter differential stayed green (the fix is behavior-preserving on the eval path — a mutation only ever clears empty caches during construction).

### IF143 — the compute-overlay derived-view cache: share an index-column / `$`-correlation view across a computation step's outer rows (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG60) <a id="if143"></a>

Under an ACTIVE cascade overlay, `OverlayContext.indexColumn` rebuilt `IndexColumn(starCells(...))` and `OverlayContext.correlationBuckets` rebuilt the wrapped-row hash buckets on **every read**. The raw `Document` memoizes both per `(column, scope)` at the document level (IF116's `indexColumnByScope`, the `$`-bucket `correlationBucketCache`), but the overlay deliberately bypassed those — its view legitimately changes as the cascade lands, and `by d` delegation would silently serve stale RAW cells (IF116). So a computation over `O` outer rows whose per-row evaluation read such a view (a `[F For key]` semantic-index validity gate; a `$`-equality-self-correlation quantifier in a precondition) rebuilt an `I`-row view per outer row → **O(O×I)**, quadratic when both scale. This was the last confirmed compute-side O(rows²) cliff (the IG60 disposition, carried since IF116).

- **The insight that makes caching safe.** Within ONE computation step (`comp`), the cascade writes only that step's OWN target column, and a legal computation never reads its own target (a self-reference reads the row's own *un*computed target as empty → the fill quantifier can't fire → degenerate). So within a step every OTHER column's overlaid view is **stable across the step's outer rows** — build it once, share it.
- **The fix.** A per-computation-step `OverlayViews` cache (fresh per `comp` in `ComputationEngine.computeOver`) keyed by `(column(s), scope-prefix)` — the SAME scope key the raw document-level cache uses, exposed as `EvalContext.scopePrefixOf` (the `Document.RowContext` override returns `groupScopePrefix`, and `OverlayContext` reaches it through `by d`). Two outer rows of the same scope therefore share one entry, exactly as the raw cache shares across rows → **O(O+I)**. The cache is fresh per step and never spans one, so a later step's self-write can never read a view built against a superseded overlay (the retained-space bound: the distinct views one computation references).
- **The self-target guard (do not hide a self-write behind a stale cache).** A view whose read column IS the step's target (`indexColumn(target)`, or a `$`-join/star on the target) BYPASSES the cache and rebuilds fresh every read — identical to the pre-fix always-rebuild behavior. This is defensive: the self-reference case is degenerate in outcomes (above), so the guard is unobservable, but it keeps the invariant "the cache never serves the column being written this step" explicit and preserves pre-fix behavior exactly.
- **Result + locks.** The **index-column path was the HARD cliff**: interp-only growth over N and 4N Rows went from **18.75× (pre-fix, quadratic) → 4.41× (post-fix, linear)** at 1500/6000 Rows — a clean RED→GREEN (`computeGuardedIndexCell` reads `indexColumn(Key)` per Row and that rebuild dominated). The **`$`-correlation path is a SOFT O(rows²)**: its rebuild reuses the doc-cached `rowContexts`, so the per-row constant is tiny — even at 4000/16000 Rows the pre-fix growth is only ~6.75× (the O(rows²) never clears the JIT-deflated noise into the quadratic band), and the cache is roughly NEUTRAL there. So the cache is a large win for the index path and near-nil for the correlation path, but BOTH are shared per step by the same `OverlayViews` mechanism. Gates: `adapter … PerfGateTest.computeOverlaySemanticIndexStaysSubQuadratic` (1500/6000, the strong RED→GREEN guard) / `computeOverlayCorrelationStaysSubQuadratic` (1500/6000, a soft guard that trips only on a large-constant regression, kept off the flaky-at-16000 band), and `computeOverlayAgreesWithKernel` (`runScenario` asserts the compute outcomes agree **interp == groovy == java** — the zero-unsupported + VALUE/CLEARED/ERRORED parity, a stale/mis-scoped cache would diverge). Cross-platform outcome lock: `interpreter … OverlayDerivedViewCacheTest` (JVM+JS) — a `$`-correlation over a COMPUTED column must read the cascade values (not the stale empty stored cell) consistently across the step's rows, and a semantic index under an active overlay resolves the same row for every outer row. The `Src`-not-`In` field name in the adapter scenarios dodges the kernel's `In` grammar keyword (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` in Java codegen).
- **Residual (not IG60).** Full NFR certification remains possible through IF137's six-point per-number gate, but owner policy currently promotes no candidate number; the growth gate uses the standard N/4N estimator and the parity gate shows the informative Java ratio (`ratioJava`). Multi-star equality-correlation legality and growth graduated as IF144.

### IF144 — the multi-star `$`-equality correlation hash-joins too, at the first-star scope (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG98; deeper bind-depth lock completed) <a id="if144"></a>

`starQuantTally` recognized an equality `$`-self-correlation `Having` (`[X] == [$X]` ± a `CurrentRepetition` self-exclusion) and used the O(1) hash join for a SINGLE-star operand, but routed EVERY multi-star operand (`A* / B* / F`, the star binding above the leaf group's parent — IF94) to the O(N²) `naiveStarQuantTally`, even when the same equality join was recognizable. The probe (`MultiStarOuterCorrelationDiffTest`) settled the two prerequisites IG98 demanded: the shape is **kernel-LEGAL** (the kernel MVK-accepts `MoreThanOneFieldFilled(/Perf/Lines* then Subs* then Cost Having [Cost] == [$Cost])`, a per-Sub rule whose star spans every Line's Subs) and the interpreter's naive fallback was already **correct** (it counts a Cost shared across Lines across the whole multi-star scope). A scaling measurement then showed the interp was **worse than the kernel's complexity class** — interp grew ×6.41 over 4× Lines vs kernel-Java ×2.69, the interp/kernel ratio *widening* 0.51→1.21 — so the optimization was owed, not a no-op.

- **The fix.** The recognized equality join now hash-joins the multi-star operand too: `hashJoinTally` passes `star.bindDepth` into `correlationBuckets`, which widens the bucket scope from the single-star leaf-parent (`groupScopePrefix(group, -1)`) to the **first-star bind depth** (`groupScopePrefix(group, bindDepth)`) — exactly the rows `rowContexts(group, bindDepth)` (the naive scan) already iterates, so the buckets span every A's B-rows. The `Document.correlationBucketCache` scope key already includes the prefix, so multi-star and single-star get distinct cached entries; the `OverlayContext` per-step cache (IF143) threads `bindDepth` the same way.
- **The one shape that STAYS naive — a multi-star self-exclusion.** `CurrentRepetition(G) != CurrentRepetition($G)` on a multi-star operand drops EVERY row sharing the outer's leaf index across the wider scope (multiple rows), not just the outer's own row, which the hash join's single-row self-subtraction cannot express (in the single-star scope that index is unique, so single-star self-exclusion is safe). So `starQuantTally` hash-joins a multi-star operand only when `!join.selfExcluded`; the self-excluded multi-star case stays on the correct `naiveStarQuantTally`. The WI-4 property-6 pairing (`correlationTallyBothWays`) returns null for that shape (no fast path to compare against).
- **Result + locks.** interp went from **×6.41 (worse-than-kernel, ratio 0.51→1.21) to ×2.37 (linear, ratio 0.17→0.16)** at 150/600 Lines — now **~6× faster than kernel-Java and STAYS faster** (in the kernel's complexity class). The interp-only growth gate `adapter … PerfGateTest.multiStarCorrelationStaysSubQuadratic` (N=5000/20000 Lines) confirms ×4.26 (linear; a reverted naive scan would be ~×16). Correctness: `MultiStarOuterCorrelationDiffTest` pins interp == kernel across the hash-join path (cross-Line duplicate counted across the multi-star scope, empty/zero keys and unevaluated rows drop) AND the naive-fallback path (the self-excluded case). `:interpreter:check` (JVM+JS+purity) green.
- **Deeper-scope lock.** The original shape covers a 2-level operand at bind depth 1. The follow-on `MultiStarOuterCorrelationDiffTest.threePlusLevelCorrelationLocksBindDepthZeroAndTwo` uses four repeatable levels to lock both boundary scopes the generic threading promised: bind depth 0 spans duplicates across top-level roots, while bind depth 2 stays within the bound root/region and spans the two starred levels below it. Empty and malformed join keys drop, dynamic-Groovy and static-Java agree with the interpreter, and the former §2 residual is closed.

### IF145 — one enforced prepared-model boundary: `model seed` prepares through the shared expander, and the loader refuses unprepared residue (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG91) <a id="if145"></a>

The interpreter's `ModelLoader` consumes only an **expanded, self-contained, name-path-keyed** model — it does not mount includes, inline imported typedefs, or normalize opaque ids. The runtime verbs (`model eval`/`compute`/`rule eval`) prepared their input through `InterpreterEngine.expandForEval` (`dm.core.ModelReader.expand` — mount includes, inline `TypeDefType`, normalize ids, remap included references), but **`model seed` read raw DM-JSON and called `InterpreterModelLoader.load` directly**, bypassing that boundary. Two silent-wrong-seed classes followed: an includes-bearing model was seeded from the unexpanded stub (the mount has no elements yet, so the whole mounted subtree was **absent** from the walked model and never instantiated), and an imported-typedef field flattened to `Kind.STRING` (the loader's documented v1 degrade), so the seed drew a string token where the model declares a NUMBER. The corpus differential hid this because `CorpusModelPreparer` performs its own expansion/typedef-inline/id-normalize before loading — nothing exercised the *raw* seed path.

- **The root fix has two halves — route, then guard (a route-only fix is symptom-only).** (1) `ModelSeedCommand` now prepares through the SAME `expandForEval` every runtime verb uses (it gained the `WorkspaceOption` mixin for the include resolver); a surviving unmapped included reference or an unresolved model reference **refuses loudly** (the structured `refused` envelope) rather than seed a wrong instance — seed ignores rule conditions, but keeping ONE boundary beats a divergent seed path. (2) The commonMain `ModelLoader` now **enforces** the prepared contract (`requirePrepared`): a recorded element with a non-name-path id, a `Group` still carrying `modelAlias` (unexpanded include), or a `Field` with an unresolved `TypeDefType` is **rejected with a corrective message** instead of degrading silently. This is the class-covering guard — the reported symptom was one verb, the root was "the loader trusts its caller to have prepared the model," so the fix makes *any* future bypass fail loud, not just seed.
- **Why the loader guard is safe (a documented contract flip).** The loader KDoc previously promised "a `TypeDefType` reference flattens to STRING"; no interpreter-loader test locked that degrade (the `TypeDefType` assertions all live in rulekit/modelkit/core, over their own readers), and every `ModelLoader.load` consumer feeds prepared input. `ModelLoaderParityTest` now prepares every fixture through the production `ModelReader.expand` boundary before comparing the common and JVM loaders, including both multifile storefront fixtures; raw residue remains rejected by the separate multiplatform guard.
- **Coverage placement.** The three residue classes are locked at the enforced boundary by `interpreter … ModelLoaderPreparedGuardTest` (JVM+JS: unexpanded include, unresolved typedef, opaque id each reject). The production id transform is directly locked by `ModelReaderTest.normalizesElementIdsToTheirNamePath`. The *positive* consumer boundary is locked by `cli … ModelSeedPreparationTest` for all three visible cases: an included sub-model's field is instantiated, an imported Money typedef seeds as a NUMBER rather than a STRING token, and a model with opaque element ids seeds with the same structure and constrained values as its name-path representation.
- **Current boundary (updated 2026-07-26).** The original `expandForEval`/`ModelReader` route above is the historical mechanism that exposed and fixed the seed bypass. R2.11 replaced that runtime assembler with `PortableModelPreparation`: dmtool `model expand`, `model eval`/`compute`/`seed`, and `rule eval` collect and assemble the discovered exact-id closure through public `ModelWorkspace`; runtime then crosses the strict expanded-input evaluator gate. `ModelReader` remains the published Java core's index/metadata implementation and a parity comparator, not a second dmtool runtime resolver. The real-browser evidence completes this boundary as IF224.

### IF146 — a mount is a reference-aware MOVE (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, fully graduated from IG87) <a id="if146"></a>

The first increment replaced the old depth-relative special case with an absolute prefix map, but its raw slash scanner missed legal `@From`, entity-`In`, semantic-`For`, quoted-segment, and legal `$`/`Having` positions, could rewrite path-like string literals, retained cross-group short names for the wrong post-mount context, and could preserve warnings for elements later excluded.

- **Kernel behavior and clean-room mechanism.** The relevant include-expansion path is `DocumentModelExpandService.RuleAndComputationConditionUpdaterVisitor` together with `EntityPathsRelativizer`: parsed reference spans retain their pre-mount targets as the elements move. The original Java implementation uses a reference-aware lexer/span collector, not copied kernel expression. It leaves literals and comments byte-for-byte, recognizes every legal reference slot and quoted segment, binds flag-enabled unique short names against the included model before an including-model collision can change them, preserves move-invariant same-group relatives, and remaps absolute targets across SINGLE_RG, MODEL_ROOT, and nested mounts.
- **Every fragment and the refusal lifecycle.** The one mechanism handles a rule's `errorCondition` plus each computation's `commonPrecondition`, alternative `precondition`, and `operation`. Genuine unmapped references remain loud-refused. Residuals are coupled by object identity to surviving elements, computed after exclusions and final name-path normalization, so removed nested content cannot refuse and duplicate element names report distinct full paths.
- **Proof.** `IncludeReferenceRemapTest` covers all named slots plus literal/division/relative negatives; `IncludeReferenceBindingTest` covers collision-safe short names, every computation fragment, exclusions, nested residuals, and duplicate paths; `QuotedPathSegmentParseTest` runs on JVM and Node. `IncludeResidualRefDiffTest` locks the CLI evaluation/refusal boundary. `IncludeAssemblyStrategyDiffTest` compares production preparation with kernel expansion and tri-checks SINGLE_RG/MODEL_ROOT, mount depths 0/1/n, rules, computation fragments, short names, bare-root, and legal `$` inside `Having` through dynamic-Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter. The capability matrix owns that exact include-preparation cell. IG87 is deleted; no ordinary residual remains.

### IF147 — bare-group compute fill quantifiers use an identity-preserving, formally exact, lazy declaration-order scan (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG93) <a id="if147"></a>

The compute-mode plain-field and star fill quantifiers already scan in the kernel's read order with per-operator early-stop, so a later invalid cell after the deciding cell is never read and does not poison (IF85). The **bare-group** operand (`NoFieldFilled(SomeGroup)`) was the last approximation: `scanFilled` eagerly computed the WHOLE-group `fillScope` and threw `PoisonedReadException` on **any** unknown cell — *before* yielding — so the consumer's lazy early-stop never ran. A group with a decisive early field and a later malformed field therefore over-poisoned only in the interpreter. The source comment called it "an approximation ... which no probe distinguishes yet."

- **Probe-confirmed (a must-match — both kernel strategies agree).** A transitive-read discriminator (the `ComputePoisonReadDiffTest` idiom): `FlagComp := 1 when NoFieldFilled(Details)` over `Details{ First(filled, decisive), Second(malformed) }` (First declared first), then `Flag2Comp := 1 when FieldNotFilled(Flag)`. If the group scan early-stops at First, `Second` is never read → `Flag` is a PLAIN clear → `FieldNotFilled(Flag)` reads it EMPTY → `Flag2 = 1`; if `Second` is read, the malformed value POISONS → `Flag` poisoned → `Flag2` CLEARED. The kernel **groovy-dynamic AND static-Java** both produced `Flag2 = VALUE|1` (early-stop, no poison); the interpreter produced `CLEARED`. Divergence confirmed; declaring the malformed field FIRST flips both to CLEARED (read-before-the-decisive-field), proving the ORDER is observable.
- **The completed root fix.** `Document.containedFieldCells(groupPath, declaredRange)` yields identity-preserving `(path, repetitions, raw, base state)` events one at a time from a declaration-ordered field plan cached per model/group. Each field's repetitions—including operator-relevant declared-but-uninstantiated omissions—are exhausted before the next field, so there is no lossy group-wide tail. The computation call refines each visited filled event through the same complete `FormalResultCache` used by direct reads; a valid non-scalar `Val.Unknown` such as a `DAY_OPTIONAL` date stays FILLED, while charset, custom-type, declared-constraint, and over-limit failures poison. `OverlayContext` applies VALUE/CLEARED/POISONED state by cell identity, and dependency discovery expands a bare group to every contained computed target for both whole-model ordering and `computeField` closure. Unsupported parallel aggregate contexts fail loudly instead of returning a plausible empty-group result.
- **Actual early stop.** The iterator performs lookup and formal checking only when the consumer requests the next cell; a decisive first cell does not resolve or allocate the tail. Declared omissions ride the same per-component cursor, eliminating the old eager whole-group materialization—not either composite predicate's required fresh second component scan. `BareGroupScanLazinessTest` locks first-witness constant work at N and 4N, and `BareGroupComputeExactnessTest` locks formal classification, overlay outcomes, dependency order/closure, and portable JVM/Node behavior.
- **Strategy and operator closure.** `BareGroupComputeExactnessDiffTest` authors the model, conditions, and computations through the typed modelkit/rulekit surface and tri-checks all seven legal group-fill quantifiers against Groovy-dynamic and generated Java in both decisive-before-invalid and invalid-before-decisive orders over nested repeatable descendants. `ComputationFillQuantifierScanContractTest` now locks every exact stop plus both fresh restarts and the two-pass bound on JVM+Node; `FilteredStarComputeFormalInvalidityTest` closes the same identity-preserving formal-oracle rule for kept `Having` candidates. `BareGroupComputePoisonDiffTest` remains the original discriminator. The capability matrix names the exact specialist differential. Together these locks close the IF85 residual and IG93.

### IF148 — a computation's `toleranceRangeOp` synthesizes a tolerance-band implicit validation, not strict `!=` (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG96) <a id="if148"></a>

The kernel's implicit computation-validation (every computation is also a validation rule — IF32) compares the stored value against the alternative's operation. Its codegen (`ComputationAlternativesJoiner.createComparison`) uses `compOp = toleranceRangeOp == null ? "!=" : DiffersWithToleranceRange{N}` — a `toleranceRangeOp` (the closed set `Computation.TR{1,2,5,10}`, serialized `DiffersWithToleranceRange{1,2,5,10}`) makes the check fire only when the scale-19-normalized operands differ by more than N. The interpreter dropped the field entirely: `ModelLoader` and `ComputationAlternative` kept only precondition + operation, and `ParsedModel.implicitComputationCond` always synthesized `Cond.Cmp(NE)`. So a stored value **inside** the declared band fired the implicit validation only in the interpreter — a pure over-fire (the source comment admitted "a narrow over-fire to revisit").

- **The completed data path.** `Cond.Tolerance` + `evalTolerance` were already implemented for rule conditions; IF174 later corrected their normalization order and polarity. A closed `ToleranceBand` now travels end to end: typed `Computation.Alternative` and its builder overloads → `DraftComputation.Alternative` builder → kernel read conversion before the comparison wrapper is unwrapped → validator/writer/edit replacement → CLI add/apply/read/modify → semantic diff, explanation, and model report → `ModelLoader`/`ComputationAlternative`/`ParsedAlternative` → `implicitComputationCond`. An alternative with a band builds `Cond.Tolerance(fExpr, operation, dec(band.amount))`; an absent band builds strict `Cond.Cmp(NE, …)`. Every tier retains its own band. `errorCodesToSuppress` remains separate.
- **Fail-loud boundary.** Missing or explicit JSON null means no band. Any present value must be a string equal to one of the four wire values; empty, blank, unknown, object, array, number, and Boolean values reject with a diagnostic naming `toleranceRangeOp` and the accepted set. No malformed present value can degrade to strict inequality.
- **Locks.** `ComputationToleranceValidationDiffTest` authors the computation through the typed rulekit surface and tri-checks Groovy-dynamic, one generated-Java engine, and the interpreter for every band: equal, strict interior (including decimal interior for band 1), boundary, just-outside in both directions, mutually exclusive tiers with different bands, and false common/alternative guards. `ComputationToleranceBandLoadTest` and `ComputationToleranceValidationTest` execute the boundary and synthesized validation on JVM and Node with no hidden unsupported result. `ComputationToleranceCliTest` proves all four bands through add/read, band-only modify/diff, explain, report, and persisted kernel acceptance; executable schema examples and conformance guards cover the public JSON shape. The capability matrix's computation-metadata cell names `ComputationToleranceValidationDiffTest`, not the rule-only tolerance test.

IF148 completed the metadata path; IF174 corrects the shared tolerance truth and polarity that both authored rules and these synthesized conditions execute.

### IF149 — `RepetitionNotUnique` is an ordinary per-row 3VL leaf with typed duplicate keys and peer-exact messages (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG95) <a id="if149"></a>

The kernel evaluates `RepetitionNotUnique` as a plain per-row 3VL predicate: `RuntimeController.wiederholungNichtEindeutig` builds its duplicate cache ONCE over the full scope (`isInitialised`, `ermittleWiederholungenMitDuplikaten`), **independent of the surrounding boolean context**, then per row returns `TRUE_WF` (VALUE, every key component present) / `TRUE_AF` (OMISSION, a fillable key component empty) / `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`; that result feeds the ordinary `combineUND`/`combineODER` 3VL. The static-Java codegen (`EntityListCondition.st`) emits a call to the SAME runtime method with a per-rule cache, so both strategies agree. The interpreter, by contrast, handled RNU with a *recognizer* that accepted it only standing alone or as exactly one top-level `And` conjunct (a separate message emitter); RNU under `Or` or nested elsewhere fell through to the generic walk, where `RepetitionNotUnique` was not a `PredicateOp`, and the rule was reported **unsupported**. That was an interpreter artifact, not a kernel boundary — a legal-input gap.

- **The fix (root, not a recognizer extension).** RNU is now a first-class `PredicateOp.REPETITION_NOT_UNIQUE`, an ordinary 3VL leaf. `RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator` became a **call-scoped duplicate-cache builder** (`outcomes(rule, cond, pass)`): it scans the keys' repeatable group once per pass, independently of the guard. Every composite-key cell must be relevant and formally usable before the row participates. Key atoms retain their declared type: NUMBER keys use the kernel's scale-insensitive `VkBigDecimal.equals` domain (including its scale-19 normalization), while text stays exact; stored values that could expose the scale-19 cap are already excluded by the always-on 15-total-digit formal check. Each cached row retains its VALUE/OMISSION polarity and points to one immutable duplicate-cluster pointer projection, so rich `referenced`/`fillToFix` metadata expands the RNU keys over the complete cluster while ordinary branch references stay on the current row. The grouping and pointer projection remain O(rows × key-count) to build with O(1) row lookup; the public result can still contain O(rows²) logical references when all rows duplicate, without allocating a private copy of the same cluster list per message (IF191).
- **Ordinary composition.** `rowOutcomes` builds that cache once per rule and threads a per-row polarity seam into the `Interpreter`; `evalPred`/`predType` read it, and the ordinary boolean walk composes every legal `And`/`Or` tree. The positional recognizer, its guard-scope/composition restrictions, and the standalone emitter are deleted. `testRule`/`evalCandidate` on an RNU rule also returns a verdict instead of throwing. Degrade-loud is preserved for genuinely unmodeled or invalid shapes: more than one RNU predicate in a rule, a group/asterisk key, or an iteration scope deeper than the key group.
- **Kernel model-check finding (composition legality).** A rule that iterates a repetition level may not fire on a *not-filled* repetition, so a NEGATIVE-presence branch under `Or` (`RepetitionNotUnique(key) Or FieldNotFilled(x)`) is model-**rejected** (`MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`) and never appears in a loadable model — the legal `Or` branch is always a positive-presence / value condition. The interpreter operates on kernel-valid models, so it does not re-enforce this; the differential fixtures use only positive/value branches.
- **Locks.** `adapter … RepetitionNotUniqueExactnessDiffTest` tri-checks numeric/text/composite equality, VALUE/OMISSION peer metadata, current-row ordinary references, and all-key partial relevance against Groovy-dynamic and generated Java; `interpreter … RepetitionNotUniqueExactnessTest` repeats the portable cases on JVM and Node. `RepetitionNotUniqueCompositionDiffTest` covers RNU under `Or`, nested guards, omission composition, and a now-decisive duplicate fixture. The earlier `RepetitionNotUnique{,Formal,Guarded,Nested}DiffTest`, `PartialValidationRepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest`, `RepetitionNotUniqueLawsTest`, and `RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedTest` remain green.

### IF150 — a rejected custom-field-type cell carries the validator's PROJECT-SPECIFIC error code, not a fixed constant (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG92) <a id="if150"></a>

The kernel's custom-field-type validator (`ICustomFieldValidator.validate` → `Optional<ICustomFieldTypeCheckError>`) returns a **project-specific** `getErrorKey()` that becomes the rejected cell's formal error code verbatim (`FormatDefinitionVordefiniert.validiereFormat` → `FehlerHandler.addFehler(errorKey, …)`, severity ERROR, VALUE, on the `formalePruefung` path). The interpreter's `CustomFieldType` SPI returned only `Boolean`, so `AutoChecks` emitted the constant `customFieldTypeInvalid` for every code-based rejection. Unlike the intentionally-original human message *text* (IF3, a license-safe difference), the error CODE is a **machine-observable** contract a consumer acts on — so the constant was an exactness gap, not an Option-A choice.

- **The increment.** IF150 changed the rejection representation from Boolean to `CustomTypeRejection(code, message?)`, so a code-backed field could carry its project key while a declarative `PredefinedType` retained `customFieldTypeInvalid`. `CustomFieldTypeRejectionCodeDiffTest` tri-checked accepted input and two distinct project keys. That increment did not yet carry the kernel validator context, guarantee one relevance-first invocation, reproduce message-template substitution, or make the JS boundary strict; IF152 completes those surrounding contracts.

### IF151 — legal charset definitions are validated once and combined graphemes are matched atomically in bounded linear work (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG88) <a id="if151"></a>

The kernel distinguishes three `supportedCharacters` entry kinds (`CharsetUtil.parse`): a `"X-Y"` **range**, a single-code-point **singleton**, and a legal multi-code-point **grapheme** (a combining cluster, e.g. decomposed `e`+combining acute). Singletons/ranges go into sorted range arrays; a grapheme is stored ATOMICALLY in a trie (`LEGAL_GRAPHEME_TRIE`). Runtime matching (`LegalCharTester.pruefeZeichensatz`) walks the LONGEST trie prefix from each position and consumes the whole sequence iff the stop node is terminal, else falls back to a singleton/range check on the ONE leading code point — so a configured decomposed `é` admits the full sequence but NOT the bare base, the bare combining mark, the reversed order, or a repeated component (unless those are independently allowed). The interpreter's `LegalCharset.ofSupportedCharacters` instead FLATTENED every non-range entry into independent UTF-16 units, so it wrongly admitted the base and the mark on their own.

- **The validated boundary.** The model-slot and consumer-SPI factories share one parser. It rejects empty, surrogate-bearing, over-three-unit, plain multi-character, reversed-range, and genuinely ambiguous-overlap definitions before trie construction; the root therefore cannot be terminal and every successful match advances. It preserves the kernel's legal shared-prefix and terminal-prefix shapes. The JVM definition check uses the pinned kernel's `\X` clustering; Node uses its host grapheme segmenter only at model construction, never on the value hot path.
- **The matcher.** A legal combined entry becomes an atomic code-point sequence in an original immutable trie; singletons/ranges retain the BMP bitset. `matches` performs the longest-prefix walk with singleton/range fallback. The hot trie freezes construction maps into sorted primitive-key arrays and decodes code points into packed inline values, removing the previous per-code-point `Pair` allocation and boxed `HashMap<Int,…>` lookup.
- **Locks.** `SupportedCharactersDefinitionDiffTest` matches the pinned structural accept/reject boundary. `SupportedCharactersGraphemeDiffTest` tri-checks atomicity plus legal shared and terminal prefixes against Groovy-dynamic and generated Java. `LegalCharsetDefinitionTest` and `LegalCharsetGrowthTest` repeat construction, termination, semantic preflight, and deterministic fourfold-growth checks on JVM and Node. `CharsetPerfTest` uses interleaved N/4N within-pair medians with semantic preflight under the ratified `<9×` catastrophe floor; it is an implementation backstop, not an NFR-certified target. Range/BMP locks remain green.

### IF152 — custom-field validation preserves the complete callback/result contract and runs once per relevant cell (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, completes IG92) <a id="if152"></a>

IF150 closed rejection-code identity but left four coupled gaps: `CustomFieldType.check(value)` could not observe the validation locale, declaration bounds, or stored/display mode; the loader dropped those bounds; formal emission and suppression could invoke a stateful or remote validator more than once; and a consumer-supplied message bypassed the kernel's `$<fieldName>$` substitution. Partial validation also evaluated the formal check before testing relevance, so a non-relevant custom cell could invoke the callback. The CLI bridge exposed a looser, undocumented second contract through Boolean results and `{ok, code, message}` aliases.

- **Typed declaration and context.** `FieldDef.customType` is one `CustomFieldTypeDeclaration(name, minLength?, maxLength?)`, loaded without feeding the bounds into generic `FieldConstraints`. `CustomFieldType.check(value, CustomFieldValidationContext)` receives the call locale, effective minimum/maximum (`1`/`999` when raw metadata is absent), and `isDisplayValue = false`, matching both stored-document kernel strategies. The interpreter preserves raw metadata absence but does not invent a presentation-mode call that its public document API cannot reach.
- **One complete, relevance-first formal result.** A call-local `FormalResultCache` is keyed by concrete field instance and effective raw value. It checks relevance before evaluation, performs the built-in/declarative/code-based formal work once, and retains the complete custom rejection. Formal-message emission, rule/candidate suppression, RNU key filtering, and computation poison reads all consume that result. The raw value participates in the key because a computation cascade can overlay a new value at the same path/repetitions; state never escapes the validation or computation call, preserving thread safety and snapshot correctness.
- **Unavailable is loud but not a crash.** If a registered validator cannot run, the cell is reported through the unsupported channel and retains the interpreter's deliberate lenient raw-value fallback, including index identity and its downstream collision policy. The mandatory prepass asks whether the cached result is the concrete `mandatoryField` invalid state instead of calling the rule-owned throwing accessor. One unavailable unique index key can therefore select its payload; two equal unavailable keys still produce two `uniqueIndex` messages and select neither. `CustomFieldTypeMultiplatformTest` locks both cases and one callback per cell on JVM and Node.
- **Exact rejection projection.** Formal emission and `suppressedBy` carry the same cached project code.
