A direct low-level `Interpreter` has no `ModelDef` or formal pass from which to synthesize missing required cells, so its existing valued-field-plus-`invalid` best-effort fallback is preserved rather than silently changing to an empty set; kernel-exact callback inputs are the `DmInterpreter` contract.

*Locked:* `CustomConditionInvocationDiffTest` tri-checks Groovy-dynamic, generated static Java, and the interpreter for required-empty plus one malformed repeated pointer, exact current-row error pointer, full/null versus partial relevance, clean out-of-relevance exclusion, callback-owned firing despite the supplied formal set, and blank-row custom `Or` versus guarded `And`. `CustomConditionInvocationTest` repeats the formal-address and empty-eligibility mechanisms in commonTest on JVM and Node and locks the direct low-level constructor's compatibility fallback. Existing `CustomConditionDiffTest` and `PartialValidationCustomConditionDiffTest` retain the common guarded shape and relevance controls.

### IF188 — validation tallies and computation scans are separate fill-quantifier contracts (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-20-12/13) <a id="if188"></a>

Unfiltered validation classifies the complete selected range extensionally: known-filled and known-empty cells enter separate buckets, a formally unavailable cell remains in the range but enters neither, and a declared-but-uninstantiated slot is empty only for a declared-range clause. `quantifierFires` encodes all seven exact formulas; the caller exposes only a fired VALUE/OMISSION or the kernel-visible collapsed `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`. Whole-condition empty-row eligibility and `Having` remain separate consumers. `ValidationFillQuantifierProjectionDiffTest` tri-checks the sufficient-witness separators—including VALUE at `t=2,u=1`, OMISSION at `t=1,e=1,u=1`, and unavailable-only silence—against both kernel strategies.

Computation consumes the ordered expanded cell stream instead. Every reached invalid cell poisons; each operator stops at its own decision; `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` and `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` restart their second component scan from the beginning. `ComputationFillQuantifierRestartDiffTest` tri-checks the observable `[empty, filled, invalid]` restart separator, while `ComputationFillQuantifierScanContractTest` records the exact visit trace for all seven on JVM+Node and proves the composites remain bounded by two linear passes. That guard exposed the one path still losing cell identity: a kept `Having`-filtered star candidate used only `cellState(path)`, bypassing the call's complete `FormalResultCache`, so a custom-validator/charset-style invalid cell could count as filled. The root fix carries `path + repetitions + raw + baseState` through the same `ScanCell` refinement as every other scan; `FilteredStarComputeFormalInvalidityTest` locks kept custom-validator and charset invalidity, dropped-invalid non-read, and early-stop before a later invalid candidate.

### IF189 — seed generation prepares each distinct model-static pattern once per generator (SHIPPED 2026-07-20) <a id="if189"></a>

`ValidValues.patternSample` formerly compiled a field pattern for every generated cell, so setup work scaled with patterned fields × rows × regex compilation and could dominate the evaluation whose performance the generated document was meant to measure. The generator-owned `PreparedValidValues` now prepares each distinct pattern source once, reuses the shared `PatternRegistry`, and caches both successful and unavailable samples. The cache is scoped to one `SeedGenerator`; independent generators share no mutable state.

*Locked:* `SeedGeneratorPatternPreparationTest` generates 2,000 rows across three fields with two distinct pattern sources, proves exactly two preparations and compilations, locks deterministic reuse, and proves an independent generator receives its own preparation cache on JVM and Node.

### IF190 — structural `CurrentRepetition` operands do not create computation dependencies (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if190"></a>

A synthesized depth-shaped model first exposed a false computation-cycle rejection. The dependency walk treated the group operand of `CurrentRepetition(group)` like a value reference, after which group expansion added an edge to every computed field below that group. A legal pair—one computation guarded by the current row coordinate and a second computation reading the first target—therefore became a manufactured cycle.

The dependency projection now excludes only `CurrentRepetition`'s structural group operand. Ordinary function operands remain value dependencies, and the independent scope/message reference walks still retain the group path. This matches the kernel's calculation-operand collector and fixes the dependency mechanism rather than suppressing one generated model.

*Locked:* `CurrentRepetitionComputationDependencyDiffTest` authors a small shipment model through the typed dmkits surface and tri-checks dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter; multiplatform `CurrentRepetitionComputationDependencyTest` locks the same structural guard plus real downstream dependency on JVM and Node.

### IF191 — dense RNU messages share their output-sized peer projection (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if191"></a>

The compact synthesized profile's dense `RepetitionNotUnique` branch made every one of N rows fire with N+4 referenced pointers. The logical result is necessarily O(N²), but the interpreter also rebuilt, formatted, de-duplicated, and copied the identical N-key peer list for every firing row. JFR located that avoidable physical amplification in `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes`: repeated arrays, `ArrayList`s, and `LinkedHashMap` entries dominated allocation, while the hash-grouped duplicate detector itself stayed linear.

`RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator` now formats and de-duplicates one immutable peer-pointer projection per duplicate cluster. Every row outcome shares it. `rowOutcomes` removes same-key ordinary occurrences by path and presents the remaining small current-row prefix plus the shared cluster through an immutable O(1) joined list view; no N-entry list is copied per message. Canonical pointer text is cached in the document's already mutation-invalidated derived index, so formal, auto-check, error, ordinary-reference, and RNU projection paths reuse one rendering until the document changes. Observable ordering, de-duplication, VALUE/OMISSION metadata, and complete result cardinality are unchanged.

On the exact compact synthesized profile at N=100/400 (490/1,800 firings), after 12 warmups and with identical fixed clock, custom callback, model, document, and complete tri-engine signatures, interpreter medians were 16.85/27.28 ms versus dynamic Groovy 55.25/302.17 ms and generated Java 37.98/318.26 ms. Exact growth was 1.62× versus 5.47× and 8.38×; the independent paired interpreter estimator was 3.95×. These are diagnostic same-run measurements, not promoted NFR bars.

*Locked:* `RepetitionNotUniqueProjectionReuseTest` proves one duplicate cluster's rich-message projection is shared across every pure-RNU firing on JVM and Node; `DocumentPointerFormatCacheTest` proves one canonical rendering per address and complete invalidation on mutation. `RepetitionNotUniqueExactnessTest` and the both-strategy `RepetitionNotUniqueExactnessDiffTest`, `RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedDiffTest`, and `RepetitionNotUniqueCompositionDiffTest` retain exact message semantics.

### IF192 — synthesized performance curves bind equivalent runtime work and pair all three engines (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if192"></a>

The first synthesized-model curve could configure an interpreter clock/callback independently from the kernel clock/callback, omitted custom field-type factories on the kernel side, fingerprinted neither runtime configuration, and applied IF137's paired estimator only to the interpreter. A green row could therefore compare different work or accept a worse kernel-relative curve. The external synthesized model path was also only a system-property string, so replacing its bytes at the same path could leave Gradle's test result up-to-date.

`PerfRuntimeBindings` is now the one immutable source for paired clocks, custom conditions, and custom field types. Each registration carries both interpreter and kernel implementations plus a stable contract id; it derives both engines' configuration, owns defensive copies, creates a fresh kernel `Date` per call, and contributes its descriptors to the scenario fingerprint. Comparative harness methods reject the explicitly separate interpreter-only preflight seam. Validation, computation, and custom-field rejection locks prove the bindings reach dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter with complete equal signatures.

`pairedScenarioGrowth` builds each engine and exact N/4N instance once outside timing, tri-checks both scales, then applies the interleaved median-of-within-pair estimator independently to all three engines. It fails unless the interpreter median beats both kernel strategies at both scales and its per-unit cost is no higher than either. (Corrected 2026-08-11: the second bar was originally "interpreter growth is no worse than either", which ranks engines by fixed-cost share rather than by scaling — see [IF279](#if279).) Synthesized profiles must declare their complexity and generator metadata, mark generated Java as present, emit both scale fingerprints, and register the model file itself as a Gradle input. The G52 `:adapter:synthesizedModelPerfTest` workflow now starts directly from a closed enum registry over the committed compact, breadth-shaped, and depth-shaped synthesis-profile fixtures, treats them as diagnostic stress shapes rather than fidelity-certified calibration evidence, synthesizes into ignored test output before evaluation, and separately reports model loading, interpreter construction, exact-preflighted first answer, warm evaluation, and the paired curve. An explicit synthesized-model seam remains for focused profiling.

On the compact dense-RNU profile at N=100/400 with one diagnostic pair and 12 warmups, the corrected lane measured interpreter 9.91/24.82 ms, dynamic Groovy 41.07/256.69 ms, and generated Java 30.16/235.13 ms; growth was 2.51×/6.25×/7.79×. The numbers remain diagnostic rather than promoted release bars, while the kernel-beating development assertions are mandatory.

*Locked:* `PerfRuntimeBindingsTest`, `ScenarioIdentityFingerprintTest.runtimeBindingsArePartOfTheFingerprint`, `SynthesizedProfileScenarioRegistryTest`, and the opt-in `SynthesizedModelPerfTest`; `:adapter:synthesizedModelPerfTest` declares all committed profile fixtures as inputs, while the external-model seam binds its selected model by content.

### IF193 — validation group presence preserves admitted content and erroneous state independently (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if193"></a>

The breadth synthesized profile exposed 26 interpreter-only rules whose non-repeatable group contained only a number rejected by `minFractionalDigits`. `Document.groupFilled` indexed every raw valued placement, so the interpreter called the group filled; the kernel first admits formally converted data into its group-fill cache and records rejected cells on an independent erroneous-entity path.

The fix is one call-local staged index. `AdmittedGroupContentIndex` propagates valid values, duplicate-index values admitted before uniqueness checking, and instantiated repeatable rows to ancestor group instances in O(placements × depth), reading group rows directly from the document index rather than constructing row contexts or rescanning content per model group. The formal-result cache uses that admitted-content view for requiredness, so an invalid-only parent no longer activates a `relativeToParent` sibling, and now owns the already-reached invalid-pointer set instead of triggering a second declared-field expansion. `GroupPresenceIndex` overlays content, error, and NONE/PARTIAL/FULL relevance dimensions. Positive presence is knowable from any relevant descendant; negative absence requires full relevance and no formal error. Group-list quantifiers use the resulting true/false buckets, while the plain multi-group `NumberOfFilledGroups` becomes unavailable if any operand group is erroneous or not fully relevant. A live tri-engine discriminator refuted the review-time source prediction that an over-limit repeat row is absent from ancestor presence: creating the row still fills its non-repeatable ancestor, while its structural diagnostic and ordinary-row suppression remain separate. The raw document content index remains unchanged for rule-row eligibility and compute mode.

**Ancestor-star composition defect (FIXED 2026-07-30; SPEC-2026-07-26-02).** `groupFillStates` treated every `Expr.Star` group operand as a terminal repeatable-row stream and skipped any selected row whose terminal group was not definitely filled. For `Sections*/Items*/Details` with non-repeatable `Details`, that discarded the existing FALSE/UNKNOWN group product; malformed-only content therefore became an empty stream and made `NoGroupFilled` fire. The internal `GroupListEnvironmentProvider` now reuses the document's first-star row topology at the deepest repeatable ancestor environment when the terminal is non-repeatable, and the same `GroupPresenceState` owner yields TRUE/FALSE/UNKNOWN for the terminal in that exact context; generic `rowContexts` remains unchanged for aggregates and `Having`. A genuinely repeatable terminal remains its own environment and its instantiated empty row remains structurally TRUE. The five requested accounts plus that neighboring control are tri-locked by `NonRepeatableGroupBelowStarDiffTest` and kept on JVM + Node by `NonRepeatableGroupBelowStarTest`; no second topology, terminal state owner, or row-count special case was added.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest` tri-checks empty, invalid-only, valid-only, valid-plus-invalid, all five group-list quantifiers, the plain count, and relative-requiredness. `GroupPresenceAdmissionDiffTest` tri-checks positive-vs-negative partial relevance, complete descendant/group relevance, and the live over-limit-row behavior. Both compare dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter.

### IF194 — multi-star declared-tail polarity is hierarchical from the first star (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if194"></a>

The breadth profile's `G19*/G20*/G21*/F149` rule exposed a value/omission type split: the interpreter compared the flattened leaf-row count only with `G21`'s per-parent repeatability. One hundred leaf rows therefore looked complete against a cap of two even though the outer `G19` declared 961 rows and actual branches were missing. The same shortcut loses a missing middle branch.

`Document.RowContext.starOmission` now follows the parser's existing first-star bind depth and checks each reopened repeatable level per actual parent. Missing capacity at the outer, middle, or leaf level makes the aggregate fillable; levels above the first star stay bound. `DocumentConfig` now retains the model's complete repeatable-group set, so an unbounded group with no declared cap correctly retains an open tail instead of disappearing from this proof. The proof walks only actual indexed rows, never materializes the declared Cartesian domain, and caches the complete Boolean per `(path, bindDepth, scope)` in the document's mutation-invalidated index. This is O(actual rows × star depth) once per scope and O(1) for every subsequent rule-row read.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `MultiStarDeclaredTailPolarityDiffTest` tri-checks missing outer, middle, and leaf capacity, full saturation, and a bound-outer control against both kernel strategies. The existing document-cache invalidation boundary owns the new memo.

### IF195 — valid number seeding honors the complete fractional-digit interval (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if195"></a>

The breadth synthesized profile exposed a formally invalid number produced by the generator itself. `ValidValues.number` treated `maxFractionalDigits` as a range from zero, ignored `minFractionalDigits`, and returned a selected `minValue`/`maxValue` bound verbatim. A fixed two-decimal field could therefore receive an integer or one-decimal sample and suppress the rule whose performance the document was intended to exercise.

NUMBER seeding now validates the declared `[minFractionalDigits, maxFractionalDigits]` interval before producing a value. An unbounded value with a positive minimum chooses its emitted scale from that inclusive interval; a selected numeric bound retains its value and is padded to the declared minimum. The `minFractionalDigits = 0` branch retains the former random calls and output sequence exactly, so existing seeded fixtures remain deterministic. An inconsistent minimum above the maximum fails loudly instead of emitting a known-invalid value.

*Locked:* multiplatform `ValidValuesFractionDigitsTest` covers fixed and variable unbounded scales, lower-only/upper-only/two-bound padding, an inconsistent interval, and exact legacy random-sequence preservation on JVM and Node. `ValidValuesConstraintTest` retains the range and unconstrained-number controls.

### IF196 — breadth validation prepares model paths once and reads structural rows directly (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if196"></a>

The exact breadth-shaped synthesized curve exposed a repeatable growth loss: at 100→400 selected leaf rows the interpreter grew 1.73–1.82× while dynamic Groovy grew 1.59–1.69× in the failing runs. An interpreter-only JFR attributed the largest avoidable clusters to two sibling validation passes rebuilding model-static path structure. `AutoChecks.overLimitLevel` split every valued-field path and joined each prefix once during formal checking and again during structural over-repetition emission; the group branch also called `contextsFor` merely to recover row repetitions, which needlessly built content-aware evaluation contexts. IF193's admitted-content pass independently split and rejoined the same path for every admitted cell while propagating it to ancestor groups.

`ModelPathIndex` now prepares each declared entity's group prefixes and ordered repetition bounds once per `DmInterpreter`. Formal checking, over-repetition emission, and admitted-content propagation share those immutable entries. The structural group scan reads `Document.instantiatedRows` directly because it needs row identity, not an evaluated `EvalContext`; this removes the sibling content-index work without changing which rows or errors are observed. The call-local content/error indexes and all public results remain unchanged.

On the exact profile-derived breadth model, fixed clock/callback, exact N/4N documents, complete tri-engine signature preflight, three interleaved pairs, four warmups, and two timed iterations, the corrected curve measured 23.04→35.90 ms for the interpreter (1.43×), 127.15→200.31 ms for dynamic Groovy (1.53×), and 53.90→109.59 ms for generated Java (1.84×). The interpreter remained faster at both scales and now grew better than both kernel strategies. These numbers are diagnostic development evidence, not promoted NFR bars.

*Locked:* multiplatform `ModelPathIndexTest` checks prepared ancestor/group-prefix lookup and outermost-bound selection; `OverRepetitionDiffTest`, `GroupPresenceAdmissionDiffTest`, and `GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest` retain exact dynamic-Groovy/generated-Java/interpreter semantics. The opt-in `SynthesizedModelPerfTest` is the decisive complete-result, paired tri-engine breadth guard.

### IF197 — performance compute comparisons project report-all results to the kernel's delta boundary (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if197"></a>

The first depth-shaped COMPUTE preflight produced one interpreter-only `VALUE|V1` for a computed STRING whose input seed was already `V1`. This was a harness artifact, not an evaluator divergence: the interpreter's public compute API deliberately reports every computed instance, while both kernel strategies report only changes from the input. Corpus comparison paths already applied `kernelComputeGranularity`; `InterpreterPerfEngine` had compared its report-all signatures directly.

The interpreter performance instance captures the exact source placements while it materializes the document, outside every clock, and applies the shared kernel-delta projection while materializing each timed COMPUTE result. Validation is unchanged. IF199 subsequently split the input-static pointer lookup from the result-side projection: only the former prepares with the document, while computation, complete signature materialization, filtering, and sorting remain inside every clock. Failed paired-growth assertions also retain both N/4N rows' exact interpreter/Groovy/Java medians, so a curve cannot hide its absolute comparison when the growth gate fires.

*Locked:* `PerfComputeGranularityTest` authors an unchanged STRING computation through the typed dmkits surface and proves that the interpreter engine presents the empty kernel-delta signature set before any cross-engine comparison. The corrected depth-shaped `SynthesizedModelPerfTest` preflight agrees completely across the interpreter, dynamic Groovy, and generated Java at N=100 and N=400.

### IF198 — empty-string ingestion materializes an empty cell, never a filled value (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if198"></a>

The regenerated compact profile exposed five interpreter-only firings (C101, C103, C105, C108, and C113) before performance timing could start. Their shared operand was a STRING cell stored as `""`: dynamic Groovy and generated Java treated it as present-but-unfilled, while `Document.field` retained `value = ""`, so direct `FieldFilled`, requiredness, and the document content index all treated it as filled. `cellStateAt` happened to report `PresentEmpty` because it independently checked `raw.isNullOrEmpty()`, leaving the public stored-state read correct while evaluation consumers disagreed with it.

The root fix is at the one document-ingestion boundary. A field placement always retains its original raw text, but its evaluation value is normalized from `""` to `null` before it enters the document index. The placement therefore remains materialized as `PresentEmpty`; it is not collapsed to `Absent`. Every supported ingress route—the public builder, `PlacementSink`/seed generation, and computation-applied copies—funnels through that boundary, so requiredness, positive and negative field presence, non-repeatable group presence, row-content eligibility, stars, scan states, and formal-value enumeration now share the kernel's `DocumentUtils.valueExists` notion. Non-empty values and the separate CRLF normalization contract are unchanged.

The exact compact N=100/400 tri-engine semantic preflight now passes and the five false firings disappear before timing. *Locked:* typed own-domain `EmptyStringIngestionDiffTest` tri-checks dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter for positive/negative field presence, non-repeatable group presence, requiredness, exact polarity, and indexed pointers; multiplatform `EmptyStringIngestionTest` locks the retained `PresentEmpty` placement plus unfilled evaluation on JVM and Node.

### IF199 — compute comparison prepares immutable seed state once per reusable instance (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if199"></a>

The compact COMPUTE curve exposed an adapter-side false bottleneck. IF197 correctly made interpreter report-all results comparable with the kernel's delta-only results, but the one-shot `kernelComputeGranularity` helper rebuilt a pointer→seed map by scanning every input placement inside every timed call. The profiled computation produced one result at every scale, so this O(document placements) projection preparation—not evaluator computation—made interpreter growth lose to dynamic Groovy even while the interpreter was much faster absolutely.

`KernelComputeProjection.prepare` now indexes each filled input placement once when the reusable document instance is materialized. Each timed call still performs the actual computation, creates every complete signature, filters it against the prepared lookup, and sorts the comparable output. The compatibility `kernelComputeGranularity` function remains one-shot and delegates to the same primitive. A sibling sweep found no matching adapter-side rebuild in either kernel engine: their document instances are already materialized outside timing and each call performs only kernel computation plus result signature construction, so neither was changed.

The exact five-pair, 12-warmup, 20-iteration compact curve at N=100→400 changed from interpreter 0.151→0.188 ms with 1.71× paired growth to 0.03→0.05 ms with 0.99× growth; dynamic Groovy measured 2.82→4.33 ms (1.52×) and generated Java 0.78→2.26 ms (2.80×). The larger N=1,000→4,000 confirmation held interpreter medians at 0.03→0.03 ms with 1.37× paired growth versus Groovy 6.29→18.99 ms (3.02×) and Java 4.46→17.32 ms (3.88×). Exact tri-engine semantic preflight passed at every scale. These are diagnostic same-run measurements, not promoted NFR bars.

*Locked:* multiplatform `ComputeGranularityTest` proves preparation reads each placement exactly once, repeated projections never revisit them, and an explicitly stored empty string remains unfilled for delta projection. `PerfComputeGranularityTest` retains the adapter integration and tri-checks unchanged-value plus present-empty clear behavior. The property-driven `SynthesizedModelPerfTest` enforces absolute and growth wins for each invoked curve; the two compact curves above are recorded executions of that opt-in guard.

### IF200 — numeric aggregates preserve staged order and declaration-specific fillability (SHIPPED 2026-07-21; completes SPEC-2026-07-20-15 and SPEC-2026-07-21-02) <a id="if200"></a>

The arithmetic fold was already correct: `Aggregates.sum` starts at zero, visits values in encounter order, and each `Dec.plus` applies precision-50 `HALF_UP`. Two adjacent resolution mechanisms were not exact. Group-star expansion sorted field paths lexically instead of retaining model declaration order, and the polarity walk summarized only a single star's global signedness (falling back to symmetric omission for multi-field operands). The latter made an all-empty unsigned `Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue` grow-only instead of exposing the kernel's both-directionally fillable zero identity, and it could not distinguish which declaration was actually missing after another contributed a value.

`Document.containedFields` now preserves the insertion order prepared by `ModelDef.fields`, and both aggregate evaluation and compute scans reuse that one cached declaration plan. `numericAggregateFill` summarizes each legal numeric contributor as present, missing, and missing-signed; group stars expand through the same plan. With no missing declaration the result is fixed; with missing declarations and no present value the zero identity moves both ways; after a present value, `Sum` grows and shrinks exactly when a missing declaration is signed, while `MaxValue` is grow-only and `MinValue` shrink-only. `Having`, Date aggregates, exhausted stars, unavailable reads, and unsupported shapes retain their established paths.

*Locked:* multiplatform `NumericAggregateSemanticsTest` distinguishes encounter order, per-addition rounding, model-order group expansion, the unsigned all-empty identity, and declaration-specific signedness. Typed own-domain `NumericAggregatePolarityDiffTest` tri-checks all three operators' unsigned empty identity, a present unsigned tail, and the two multi-declaration signedness directions against dynamic Groovy and generated Java. Existing aggregate, Date, Having, poison, and row-permutation suites retain the surrounding contract.

### IF201 — validation messages use a raw display channel and an explicit name-policy cascade (SHIPPED 2026-07-21; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-21-01) <a id="if201"></a>

The interpreter previously rendered `$field.value$` from the normalized evaluation cache and treated a missing value as a hardcoded kind default. That conflated two kernel channels: evaluation sees admitted CRLF normalized to LF, while validation-message interpolation asks the caller-facing data route for its original display string. A present empty display is also not rendered verbatim; like absence, it selects the field format's active default. The verified Number discriminator uses its declared minimum fractional digits (`0`, `0.00`, …), while the retained scale-zero Number and String cases remain `0` and empty. Live probes corrected one premise in the incoming specification: the current modern `DocumentV2` route still emits `0.00` for `Locale.GERMANY` and for `modelConfig.decimalSeparator=","`; the model-config-derived `0,00` expectation is false. The interpreter therefore implements the exact admitted dot-default profile and does not invent a locale or model-config conversion layer.

`Document` now retains the original placement text and exposes it only through `EvalContext.messageDisplayValue`; ordinary field, formal, rule, and computation reads remain on the normalized index. The renderer decodes an authored `$$` to one literal `$` in the same single token pass as `$field$` and `$field.value$`, so substituted bytes are never parsed again. `$field$` now has an explicit `MessageLabelProvider`: a non-null consumer result wins verbatim, including empty; null falls through to a nonempty localized model label and then the resolved path plus repetition coordinates. The default provider preserves dmtool's established label-or-name profile, while passing no provider exposes the kernel's debug fallback.

*Locked:* `MessageValueDisplayPolicyDiffTest` compares exact Number-default, CRLF, and literal-dollar/opaque-insertion text through dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter. Multiplatform `MessageDisplayPolicyTest` locks provider-empty versus provider-absent, model-label/debug fallbacks, missing versus explicit-empty values, scale-zero/String controls, raw CRLF, `$$` decoding, and opaque replacement bytes on JVM and Node. `DisplayNameDiffTest`, `LocalizedMessageDiffTest`, and `UnspecifiedValueInterpolationDiffTest` retain the existing default-profile and localization controls.

### IF202 — partial validation skips every `Having` rule before evaluation (SHIPPED 2026-07-21) <a id="if202"></a>

CI fuzz seed 33 was not a special numeric or correlation case. It was the first generated document/relevance combination where a filtered self-correlation was live under full validation while partial validation exposed a missing rule-level gate: the interpreter evaluated the filtered rule, but both kernel strategies returned no message.

The root mechanism is source-backed. The Java, Groovy, and JavaScript-VK `CodeGenRuleDefinition.st` templates all emit a method-entry return when `t.filterConditions` meets partial-with-3VL mode, before iteration setup and condition code. Generated Java and Groovy confirm the emitted branch, and the kernel BA appendix independently lists rules with filter conditions among checks partial validation may skip. Thus relevance does not refine a filtered rule during partial validation: even wildcarding its error field and every referenced instance does not run it; full validation is unchanged.

`PreparedRulePlan` now records the presence of a filtered star once while preparing the model. `rowOutcomes` checks that flag once per rule and returns before iteration, RNU, row contexts, or correlation caches in a partial pass. The fix adds no per-row work, makes partial filtered rules strictly cheaper, and preserves the existing hash/ordered correlation paths for full validation.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `PartialValidationFilterSkipDiffTest` keeps equality and ordered-correlation rules live under full validation and absent under all-repetitions-relevant partial validation against dynamic Groovy and the interpreter; `PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` retains the original seed-33 randomized class guard.

### IF203 — runtime-invalid integral power poisons a Number computation target, exactly like division (SHIPPED 2026-07-22; completes SPEC-2026-07-21-05) <a id="if203"></a>

The named question IF177 deferred — invalid-power computation-side classification — is now closed with **no new mechanism**: it reuses IF177's `Val.DomainFailure` path. `VkBigDecimal.power` returns the same `INVALID_NUMBER` sentinel as zero division for the two runtime-invalid integral regions — `0` to a negative integral exponent, and an integral exponent outside inclusive `[-1000, 1000]` — and `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert` routes it through the same generic target-invalidity handler. `ExprEval.power` therefore returns `Val.DomainFailure` (not `Val.Unknown`) for both regions; the numeric wrappers (`Abs`, operand-list `Min`/`Max`, rounding) preserve it at the shared gates, a rule comparison still projects it to UNKNOWN/no fire, and a legal `RoundAccounting(0 ^ -1, 2)` / `RoundAccounting(2 ^ 1001, 2)` computation reaches the store boundary invalid: no fresh value, a stale value reported CLEARED, `berechnungsWertFehler` at the target, and a poisoned/transitively-cleared dependent read.

The distinction from the two *authoring* rejections is deliberate and preserved: a **non-whole exponent** (`MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`, IF-audited authoring gate) is never a legal runtime value, so `power` keeps a defensive `Val.Unknown` there rather than promoting it to a domain failure; the two runtime-invalid regions above *are* legal models whose value is invalid at runtime. Valid boundary powers stay values: `0 ^ 0 = 1`, and exponent `±1000` executes.

No new public-API surface beyond IF177's already-classified `Val.DomainFailure` subtype (that compatibility note stands unchanged). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.PowerDomainFailureComputationDiffTest` tri-checks (dynamic Groovy + generated Java + interpreter) both invalid-region witnesses as fresh-absent/stale-CLEARED with `berechnungsWertFehler` and dependent poison, the `Abs`/`Min`/`Max` wrapper preservation, and the `0 ^ 0` / `±1000` value boundary; `DivPowDiffTest` continues to lock the unchanged rule-side quiet projection.

### IF204 — operand-list `Min`/`Max` over TIME folds by time-of-day; DATE/DATETIME by instant (SHIPPED 2026-07-22; completes SPEC-2026-07-21-07 runtime side) <a id="if204"></a>

Star `MinValue`/`MaxValue` already folded all three date-like kinds via `Aggregates.min`/`ValCompare.ORDER`, but the operand-list `Min`/`Max` path routes through the instant-centric `evalTemporal`/`aggregateTemporal` (so a composed DATETIME operand keeps its exact-instant identity across a DST overlap — [IF128 family]). That path was DATE/DATETIME-only: a bare TIME has no model-zone instant, so `temporalLeaf` resolved `calendarOperandInstant("HH:mm:ss", …)` to null and returned `NON_RELEVANT` — the whole operand-list `Min([t1],[t2])` collapsed to no value and the referencing comparison silently did not fire, while both kernel strategies folded the times and fired.

The kernel folds a pure time by its **decoded time-of-day coordinate** and a dated value by its **resolved instant** (SPEC-2026-07-21-07). `temporalLeaf` now materializes a time-only `TemporalValue` (text, no instant), and `aggregateTemporal` keys the fold on `timeOfDaySeconds(text)` for a time-only value and on `instant(timeZone)` for any dated value. Authored operands are homogeneous (the kernel's comparability gate — SPEC-2026-07-22-01), so one key kind decides the whole fold. The star path is unchanged. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.TemporalExtremaDiffTest` tri-checks the TIME one-second separator, DATETIME exact-instant, all-empty no-value, symmetric omission polarity, malformed-cell suppression, the operand-list scalar forms, the neighboring DATE control, and the equal-looking `Europe/Berlin` fall-back overlap selecting by instant.

### IF205 — the depth-shape "growth-ratio loss" is a fixed-cost-dilution artifact; the interpreter is the lowest-per-row engine (RESOLVED 2026-07-22; graduated from IG101) <a id="if205"></a>

IG101 recorded that the deepest nested repeatable shape (3–4 levels) made the interpreter's **paired N→4N growth ratio** exceed both kernel strategies', treating it as a possible per-row defect after two representation attempts (a cached-hash list wrapper, a primitive-coordinate/key prototype) failed to move it. A clean full-warmup measurement to N=16,000 disproves the defect framing: **it is a metric artifact, not a defect.**

Decompose `time = fixed + perRow·N`. Clean depth/validate (warmup 12, one run, N=4,000→16,000): per-row is **interpreter 0.047 ms/row < generated Java 0.078 < dynamic Groovy 0.084** — the interpreter has the *lowest* per-row cost of all three engines, so it is the best-*scaling* engine per row, not merely the leanest in fixed cost. The consequences:

- **No crossover at any N.** Measured directly to 16,000 rows the interpreter is still 2.13× faster than Java and 2.67× than Groovy; the interp-vs-Java speedup asymptotes to ~0.078/0.047 ≈ **1.67×** (>1×) and the *absolute* gap widens with N. Neither kernel strategy ever overtakes.
- **The growth-ratio loss is the kernel's fixed cost diluting its per-row cost.** The kernel pays a huge per-call fixed cost (hundreds of ms — e.g. Java ≈ 583 ms at N=4,000) versus the interpreter's ~12 ms, so its N→4N *ratio* looks flat (1.4×) while the interpreter's tiny fixed cost makes its (smaller) per-row work a larger *fraction*, inflating its ratio. Comparing a lean engine's amortization curve against a heavy one's is what the ratio metric mismeasures.
- **The interpreter is strictly linear** — per-row flat at ~0.04–0.05 ms/row across 100→16,000; no super-linear term.

So the two prior attempts failed for the right reason: **there is no genuine per-row inefficiency to remove** — deep repetition-coordinate construction/hash/equality is already cheaper per row than compiled Java on this shape. IG101's re-open trigger ("evidence the absolute lead is eroding") is **not** met; the lead widens. The only remaining lever would *widen* the already-winning ~1.67× asymptotic margin over Java — optional, low-payoff, and the failed attempts show it is hard — so IG101 is closed as an artifact rather than deferred. Evidence (diagnostic, not an IF137 promotion): [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) "Checkpoint 2026-07-22 — IG101 resolved: depth per-row favours the interpreter (no crossover)".

### IF206 — `Now` carries exact milliseconds behind whole-second rendering (SHIPPED 2026-07-22; completes SPEC-2026-07-22-02) <a id="if206"></a>

The interpreter previously used the same whole-second string as both `Now`'s display value and runtime coordinate. That made a stored `12:00:00` spuriously equal to an injected `12:00:00.999`, lost strict ordering within the rendered second, and made sub-day difference boundaries depend on premature truncation. The kernel instead keeps the validation clock's `Date` unchanged and performs comparison and sub-day arithmetic on its epoch-millisecond identity; only authored/stored syntax is whole-second.

`EvalClock.now` now accepts the existing whole-second ISO form or the same form with an exact three-digit millisecond fraction. `TemporalValue` stores epoch milliseconds; ordinary fields and constructed values enter with a zero remainder, while `Now` retains the injected remainder through sub-day shifts and temporal `Min`/`Max`. Sub-day differences divide the exact millisecond delta by the selected unit in milliseconds using truncation toward zero. Rendering remains unchanged. UTC comparisons retain their lexicographic fast path whenever the whole-second strings differ and resolve exact instants only for a rendered tie; non-UTC comparisons already use the exact-instant path for DST identity. The correction therefore adds no expression-tree scan or general per-row comparison cost.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `NowMillisecondIdentityDiffTest` pins one `Date` into dynamic Groovy and generated Java and compares both with the interpreter for same-rendered-second equality/order, ±999 ms truncation, and a remainder-preserving one-second shift. Multiplatform `NowMillisecondIdentityTest` locks the same mechanism on JVM and Node; existing whole-second temporal and DST suites remain the controls.

### IF207 — context-free `BaseYear` follows the kernel's numeric default; temporal consumers opt into the date role (SHIPPED 2026-07-22; completes SPEC-2026-07-22-03) <a id="if207"></a>

The interpreter already resolved `BaseYear` correctly in direct comparisons, but `evalConst` defaulted it to `YYYY-01-01`. Arithmetic and Number computations therefore received text instead of the kernel's scale-0 decimal. The kernel parse tree defaults the simple operation to NUMBER, and explicitly date-typed consumers perform the date conversion.

`evalConst(BaseYear)` now returns the configured year through the ordinary decimal path. Comparison evaluation still selects the numeric or date form from the other operand, while `evalTemporal` explicitly supplies the base-year-start date. The typed authoring surface keeps the public zero-component `BaseYear` record stable; `Computation.Builder` admits its existing `baseYearNumber()` expression for a NUMBER target as the one kernel-defined polymorphic kind case. All other kind mismatches retain the general gate, and a missing configured year still reaches the kernel's `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` authoring rejection.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `BaseYearArithmeticDiffTest` tri-checks direct `BaseYear`, `BaseYear + 1`, and `Input + BaseYear` Number computations across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter. Multiplatform `BaseYearComputationTest` locks JVM/Node evaluation, `BaseYearArithmeticLawsTest` locks missing configuration through the real kernel, and `BaseYearDiffTest` retains the date and comparison roles.

### IF209 — numeric-text conversion selects one exact domain and reuses ordinary wrapper semantics (SHIPPED 2026-07-23; completes SPEC-2026-07-22-13 and SPEC-2026-07-23-01) <a id="if209"></a>

`FieldValueAsNumber` used to accept only a direct field expression and handed its stored text to the platform decimal parser. That erased the kernel-legal `Enumeration -> Category` selection and made JavaScript's ASCII parser reject a host decimal digit that the kernel admits. The correction stays inside the existing expression node and evaluator: its sealed source is a direct field/enum or one category projection, category lookup reuses ordinary `Expr.EnumCategory`, and one small parser validates the kernel grammar while normalizing each host decimal digit to ASCII before the shared `Dec` constructor. Missing remains numeric zero with symmetric fill, a present value is fixed, and a formally invalid reached computation read uses the existing poison path.

The direct `RoundDown`/`RoundUp`/`RoundAccounting` and `Abs` follow-up needs no new evaluator branch. The existing rounding path replaces scale at authoring, rounds the produced `Dec`, and preserves `fill(arg)`; the existing absolute-value path retains scale and applies `ArithFill.abs`, so symmetric missing zero becomes grow-only. Both wrappers naturally keep formal poison because they map the conversion result rather than intercepting its read. This reuse is the reason the focused wrapper controls passed without production changes after the conversion fix.

*Locked:* `FieldValueAsNumberDomainDiffTest` and `FieldValueAsNumberWrapperDiffTest` agree across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter for selected domain, Unicode digits, exact values, missing polarity, wrapper scale, and poison. `FieldValueAsNumberDomainTest` and `FieldValueAsNumberWrapperTest` pin the same mechanisms on JVM and Node; `FieldValueAsNumberDomainLawsTest` owns static source-domain admission.

### IF210 — numeric wrappers compose directly with `RangeAsNumber` (VERIFIED 2026-07-23; completes SPEC-2026-07-23-02) <a id="if210"></a>

No production gap remained: parser and authoring already expose `RangeAsNumber` as the ordinary Number-valued `Expr.Func`, rounding already maps any numeric child and preserves its fill, and `Abs` already maps the value through `ArithFill.abs`. The existing `RangeAsNumber` evaluator supplies the decisive source facts: a selected digit run is a fixed nonnegative scale-0 number, a filled nonnumeric selection is fixed zero, a missing source is grow-only zero, and a formally unavailable computation read is poison. Consequently rounding assigns its authored scale, while `Abs` retains scale 0 and is value-identical. Serialized integral results are canonical numeric text (`125`, not a promised `125.00`); authored scale is checked by the kernel's result-type gate rather than inferred from display text.

*Locked:* `RangeAsNumberWrapperDiffTest` agrees across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter for direct admission, default/authored rounding, selection/fallback/missing polarity, exact values, and poison including a dependent read. `RangeAsNumberWrapperTest` pins the same mechanisms on JVM and Node. Existing `RangeCoercionDiffTest`, `FunctionFillPolarityDiffTest`, and `FunctionFillTest` retain the standalone mechanisms.

### IF211 — `Abs` maps the completed numeric aggregate and its fill (VERIFIED 2026-07-23; completes SPEC-2026-07-23-03) <a id="if211"></a>

No production gap remained. The parser already represents `Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/numeric `NumberOfDifferentValues` as ordinary Number-valued `Expr.Func` children, the aggregate evaluator owns their one ordered fold, and the existing `Abs` branch maps the resulting `Dec` exactly once. The same composition reaches `funcFill`: the aggregate first derives its result directions, then `ArithFill.abs` transforms those directions from the folded sign. Consequently a negative Sum is folded before sign removal, all-empty Sum/extremum zero changes from bidirectional to grow-only, and the already-grow-only distinct-count zero stays grow-only. Aggregate scale passes through unchanged, while a formal-invalid member follows the existing aggregate unknown/poison route through both the wrapper and dependent reads.

*Locked:* `AggregateAbsDiffTest` agrees across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter for all four aggregate families, fold-before-`Abs`, present and all-empty polarity, derived-scale computation, and poison. `AggregateAbsTest` pins those mechanisms on JVM and Node. Existing aggregate and `Abs` tests retain the standalone owners.

### IF212 — temporal numeric wrappers reuse the source projection; direct numeric `BaseYear` is legal (FIXED 2026-07-23; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-04) <a id="if212"></a>

The runtime composition already matched the kernel: date/time component extraction and Date-only month/year difference return the established scale-0 `Val.Num`, rounding and `Abs` consume that value through their ordinary branches, `funcFill` preserves or transforms the source directions, and computation poison passes through. The focused cross-product confirms stored DATE, TIME, and DATETIME components; fixed `YearFromDate(BaseYear)`; signed difference before `Abs`; symmetric-empty rounding versus grow-only empty magnitude; authored scale; and formal poison across both kernel strategies and JVM/Node.

The entry's direct-`BaseYear` rejection claim was wrong. Kernel `CheckOpUtils.isConstant` recognizes the `konstante` grammar member only; `BaseYear` uses `konstanteReferenzjahr`, and the wrapper's number-like gate explicitly admits `BASE_YEAR`. Dynamic Groovy and generated Java accept and evaluate both `RoundDown(BaseYear)` and `Abs(BaseYear)` in validation and computation. The interpreter already used its context-free numeric default (IF207). The actual gap was typed authoring: `Dsl.baseYearNumber()` selects the numeric role, but `OpListMath` re-read the underlying polymorphic `BaseYear.kind()` as its primary DATE role and rejected `Abs` before kernel validation. Its existing `Abs` gate now recognizes that one polymorphic numeric role, matching the already-established `Computation` exception without adding an AST node or widening ordinary DATE operands.

*Locked:* `TemporalNumericWrapperDiffTest` agrees across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter including direct and derived Base-Year forms; `TemporalNumericWrapperTest` pins JVM/Node; `BaseYearArithmeticLawsTest` locks configured direct/derived admission and missing-config rejection. Existing standalone temporal, rounding, `Abs`, and IF207 controls remain green.

### IF213 — semantic-index Number keys compare by normalized numeric value (FIXED 2026-07-23; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-08) <a id="if213"></a>

Semantic-index key identity is owned by the declared index-field kind (kernel `IndexFieldCache.normalizeValue`): a **Number** index field normalizes both the admitted stored key and the requested literal-or-field-valued key numerically, so `5` and `5.00` select the same row; every non-Number kind keeps exact stored text. The interpreter's document-owned lookup map (`Document.RowContext.indexedCell`) had keyed on raw placement text on both sides, so a Number row stored as `5.00` missed a requested `5` — and, symmetrically, a stored `5` missed a requested `5.00` (the build side and the query side both failed to normalize, which is why the fix is a single shared key projection rather than a one-sided patch).

The fix is at that shared root: one `indexKey(value, numeric)` projection applied identically on construction AND query. A Number index field's key is the peer's established canonical numeric form (`Dec.display`, trailing zeros stripped), with a raw-text fallback for a non-parsing token (mirroring the kernel's raw fallback); every other kind stays exact text. No second evaluator, lookup cache, raw-value framework, or normalization pass was added. The must-not-change owners are untouched: index-column invalidity poisoning/suppression, duplicate-key unavailability, clean no-match, matched-empty, selected-target conversion, and phase precedence all still flow through the existing `IndexColumn`/compute-guard machinery — and because `IndexColumn`'s cells are `Val.Num` whose equality is scale-insensitive, a numerically-duplicate Number column (`5` + `5.00`) is already flagged unavailable, so the normalized key never selects one duplicate participant.

*Locked:* the Number literal-key and field-valued-key cases in `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest` (tri-engine: dynamic Groovy == generated Java == interpreter, both spellings and the reverse build/query asymmetry); the exact-String separator in `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexDiffTest` (the same `5`/`5.00` pair stays distinct, and would go red under an over-normalizing all-kinds key); JVM/Node host parity by the interpreter's multiplatform `SemanticIndexNumberKeyTest` (both spelling directions, literal + field-valued keys, and the String separator, on both hosts). Existing semantic-index validation, presence, compute-no-match, and matched-invalid-cell controls remain green.

### IF214 — the filtered field-list traversal prefetches one kept successor (FIXED 2026-07-23; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-10 and SPEC-2026-07-23-11) <a id="if214"></a>

The kernel's filtered entity iterator (`EbenenIterator.InnerIterator`) advances to the NEXT kept candidate before returning the current one, so computation-phase filter and target reads interleave by exactly one kept-successor lookahead (KERNEL-FINDINGS §9/§11 "prefetches one kept successor"). The interpreter's two filtered field-list consumers each read in a different, wrong order: `firstFilledScan` consumed a kept row's target immediately after that row's own filter (under-reading the successor filter — a filled first target won where the kernel poisons during the successor search), and `starOperandVals` filtered every row eagerly before mapping targets (over-reading later filters). The fix is one shared lazy `keptRowsWithSuccessorLookahead` sequence at the traversal's existing owner in `ExprEval`: it yields a kept row only after the successor search has completed, serving both consumers without changing their distinct target consumption — `firstFilledScan` keeps its prefix stop (stopping the pull leaves filters beyond the prefetched successor unread), `starOperandVals` still drains every kept row into the aggregate fold. Validation is order-insensitive here (filter reads are 3VL row-drops, never aborts), so the same traversal serves both modes; no second filter AST, target evaluator, scheduler, iterator framework, or capture route was added.

One observability boundary was established while locking: a compute poison is CAUSE-BLIND on both sides (the kernel's generated catch discards `CalculationFieldException.getInternalId()`; the interpreter's `ComputationEngine` catch equally drops `PoisonedReadException.path`), and the draining aggregates read the same total cell set under any complete order — so SPEC-2026-07-23-11's "later filter poison where the kernel reports an earlier target poison" divergence does not manifest in any observable outcome, and the pre-fix eager order was already outcome-equivalent for `Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues` (all seven aggregate separators were green before the fix; `build/lock-11-red.log`). The read order is outcome-observable exactly through `FirstFilledValue`'s prefix stop, which went red→green (`build/lock-10-red.log`: a filled current target vs the successor-filter poison, directly and through a false candidate).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueKeptSuccessorDiffTest` (successor-filter poison over a filled current target; filter-beyond-successor unread; poison through a false candidate; earlier-terminal-operand suffix invisibility; empty-kept-target continuation — tri-engine, dynamic Groovy == generated Java == interpreter) + `adapter.laws.AggregateKeptSuccessorDiffTest` (the five aggregate separators including the clean-fold no-first-value-stop control, plus MinValue/NumberOfDifferentValues family pins) + multiplatform `FirstFilledValueKeptSuccessorTest`/`AggregateKeptSuccessorTest` (JVM + Node). The kernel fact lives in KERNEL-FINDINGS §9/§11 and the canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9/§11 prose.

### IF215 — `NumberOfValueInFields`: the value route now expands starred operands, and its filtered fill rides the `containsFilteredValue` value gate (FIXED 2026-07-23; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-12) <a id="if215"></a>

The interpreter supported only scalar-field operands of `NumberOfValueInFields`. A starred `Group* / Field` operand — filtered or bare — reached `evalExpr(Expr.Star)`'s "a starred path must sit inside an aggregate" throw, so `validateFull` reported the whole rule UNSUPPORTED and raised (`build/lock-12-red.log`: `IllegalStateException` at the interpreter call, while the same probe's groovy-dynamic == java-static tri-check agreed the kernel *accepts and evaluates* the shape — the interpreter was the sole diverging party). One class, two fronts:

**(1) The value route couldn't expand a starred operand.** `numberOfValueInFields` now iterates the operand's row contexts exactly as `filledFieldsValue` does — a `Having` filter drops a row FIRST (a dropped row's malformed cell never suppresses), then a kept row is counted iff it is filled and its value equals the constant; a kept malformed cell makes the count non-evaluable (`Val.Unknown` in validation, `PoisonedReadException` on the compute path). The scalar arm is unchanged. This reuses the existing filtered traversal — no second count/filter mechanism.

**(2) The polarity had no filter separator.** For a filtered star, `NumberOfValueInFields` was blanketed both-directional by the value-combiner rule (`hasHavingStar → Fill.of(true, true)`), which overstates omission repair for a selected non-match. It now joins `FILTER_SENSITIVE_COUNTS` (so `fill`'s combiner blanket exempts it) and rides `countFill` with a value-match `qualifies` gate: a filter present ⇒ growable, but shrinkable only when a MATCHED cell came through the filter. So a filtered current match is both-directional (OMISSION either side) while a filtered selected non-match is a grow-only zero (VALUE stored below, OMISSION stored above). `NumberOfDifferentValues` stays under the combiner blanket (kernel source).

**Second instance (predicted from the root, reproduced).** The same scalar-only assumption made a *bare* (unfiltered) star equally unsupported. The (1) fix covers it — a bare star iterates every row (no filter drop), counts matches, and is grow-only via its omitted tail (never shrinkable). Locked alongside the filtered separators.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.NumberOfValueInFieldsPolarityDiffTest` (the two separators + unfiltered-star both directions + filter-drops-then-matches + no-rows tail + exact token identity + first-formal-cause, tri-checked groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter) + scale-normalized Number equality in `adapter.laws.NumberOfValueInFieldsDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … NumberOfValueInFieldsPolarityTest` (JVM + Node). Fix in `commonMain` (`ExprEval.numberOfValueInFields`, `PolarityWalk.countFill`/`FILTER_SENSITIVE_COUNTS`) → JVM + JS. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS "NumberOfValueInFields (family 6)" §12 supplement.

### IF216 — the fixed group-list tally classifies each operand by kind, and partial-mode presence reads honor relevance in both directions (FIXED 2026-07-23; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-09) <a id="if216"></a>

`quantTally`'s `countsGroups` branch called `groupPresence` on EVERY operand, so a kernel-legal FIELD operand of a fixed group-list quantifier (`AllGroupsFilled(PolicyNumber, Vehicle)`, field-only lists, the `NoGroupFilled(F)`/`AtLeastOneGroupFilled(F)` singletons) was classified as a contentless group — a filled field read "definitely empty" (`build/red-entity-operand-diff.log`: 11 of 18 tri-check scenarios diverged on the interpreter alone; groovy-dynamic == java-static throughout). The fix dispatches per operand on the model fact `declaredKind(path) != null` (the same discriminator `hasFillScopeOperand` uses in the inverse direction): a FIELD operand contributes the shared `presenceTri` three-way state — REUSING the field-presence classifier — while a GROUP operand keeps the IF193 product state. `quantifierFires`, the polarity walk, and the starred single-group route (IF68) are untouched; no second family or fold.

**Second instance (predicted from the root, reproduced).** The kernel makes a non-relevant operand unavailable in BOTH directions during partial validation, but `filledTri` ordered `!filled → FALSE` before the `invalid` gate, so an EMPTY non-relevant field still supplied an empty witness — visible in the new mixed lists AND pre-existing for the plain field-list quantifiers and scalar presence forms. `filledTri` now checks `cellRelevant` (row-correct, via the context's own reps) before the fill read; the hook is null in full validation and compute, so only partial-mode presence reads change.

*Locked:* tri-engine `adapter.laws.GroupListEntityOperandDiffTest` (mixed/field-only fill states, malformed + non-relevant field operands, decisive witnesses beside unavailable siblings, fixed-singleton ≡ scalar presence; groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter, full + partial) + multiplatform `GroupListFieldOperandTest` (JVM + Node) + round-trip `adapter.GroupListEntityOperandReadTest` + F4 `validate.laws.GroupListEntityOperandLawsTest`. Fix in `commonMain` (`Interpreter.quantTally`, `Interpreter.filledTri`) → JVM + JS. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §1 "the FIXED group-list quantifiers consume one common field-or-group entity list".

### IF217 — the index-field stored default: full/compute STAGE the S-value, partial marks the cell silently unavailable, and the compute path gained the preliminary index poison (FIXED 2026-07-23; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-14 and SPEC-2026-07-23-15) <a id="if217"></a>

The model surfaces did not represent the closed Enumeration's **S-value** (DM-JSON `sValue` — the kernel's model-owned index default) at all, so the whole phase-specific staging semantics was missing: full validation flagged an eligible clean-empty index `mandatoryField` where the kernel stages the declared token (`build/idx14-red3.log`: 7 of 15 tri-check scenarios diverged on the interpreter alone; groovy-dynamic == java-static throughout, incl. the per-parent scoping witness). The representation landed at each surface's natural owner — modelkit `FieldTypeSpec.EnumSpec.sValue` + `EnumFieldBuilder.sValue(...)` (authoring validates the token against the declared values; emit + authored-state read-back + the CLI field-spec `enum.sValue` round-trip incl. `field-add`/`typedef-add` schemas), and `FieldDef.enumSValue` → `ModelDef.indexDefaults` in the interpreter model (`ModelLoader` parses the key). The behavior then reuses the existing owners: `AutoChecks.eligibleIndexDefaults` computes the kernel's per-document eligibility (exactly one instantiated row per parent, clean-empty cell — absent or present-empty; a formally invalid explicit value is filled, hence never eligible); a FULL call (`validateWith`/`evalRule`) stages the cells through the existing `Document.withComputed` call-local view (no document mutation, no second document); a PARTIAL call feeds the same cells to `FormalResultCache` as silently **unavailable** (`INDEX_DEFAULT_SUPPRESSED`, a message-less generated failure seeded into `invalidPointers` eagerly) — the generated checks stay silent, dependent reads turn UNKNOWN, and the group-presence index sees the error state, so a `NoGroupFilled` cannot prove absence.

**Second front (SPEC-2026-07-23-15, predicted from the shared root and reproduced).** Computation applies the SAME full-call staging (`ComputationEngine.computeOver` stages before evaluating), and the compute path was missing the kernel's **preliminary index invalidation** entirely: a computation directly reading a selected EMPTY index computed `Length("") = 0` where the kernel poisons the read (prepass `mandatoryField` → `markFieldAsInvalidForValidation` → the reading instance clears), and a DUPLICATED index read the duplicated token where the kernel's `uniqueIndex` prepass poisons both readers — two sibling instances of one missing mechanism. The fix gives the compute call the same index knowledge validation has: `duplicateIndexCells` now feeds the compute `FormalResultCache`, and `AutoChecks.preliminaryEmptyIndexCells` (post-staging, over-limit rows keep their over-repetition precedence) poisons empty-index reads through `operandNotCheckRelevant` and the `computeScanState` seam — while an ordinary required-empty operand stays empty-as-0 (IF76 unchanged). The eager `formalErrorsInOperands` envelope channel remains kernel-only; the report-all interpreter documents the poisoned reader as CLEARED, which `kernelComputeGranularity` projects to the kernel's silence for a never-source-filled target.

*Locked:* tri-engine `adapter.laws.IndexDefaultStagingDiffTest` (absent/present-empty staging with dependent-rule visibility, admitted/invalid-explicit + multi-row + mandatory + non-defaulted-duplicate controls, per-parent scoping, no-phantom-row, and the partial silent-suppression arms incl. group-error retention; groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter, full + `validatePart`) + the SPEC-15 arms of `adapter.laws.ComputationEagerPrepassLawsTest` (selected-vs-unused, iteration/group-ref/semantic-index/parallel-iteration/filter induction, staging value parity, empty/duplicate poison projections; dual-kernel-route) + multiplatform `IndexDefaultStagingMultiplatformTest` and `ComputationIndexDefaultMultiplatformTest` (JVM + Node) + authoring `modelkit.EnumSValueTest`. Fix in `commonMain` (`Model.kt`, `ModelLoader`, `AutoChecks`, `FormalResultCache`, `DmInterpreter`, `ComputationEngine`) → JVM + JS. Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §1/§12 supplement (SPEC-2026-07-23-14) + §11 supplement (SPEC-2026-07-23-15).

### IF218 — the value-list quantifiers preserve operator-specific pass structure: encounter order + witness-specific filter polarity (FIXED 2026-07-24; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-23-16) <a id="if218"></a>

`ValueListQuantEval` collapsed each side of a shape-B quantifier into one global summary (`VlqSide.anyUnknown`/`anyEmpty`/`hasHaving`), so multi-operand encounter and filter cases lost the kernel's per-operator traversal: a fired `AtLeastOne`/`NotAll` was typed OMISSION off an earlier filtered NONwitness (`build/lock-16-red.log`: both kernel routes `VALUE_ERROR`, interpreter `OMISSION_ERROR` — the S2 twins), an `AtLeastOne` values-side filter that selected nothing present escalated the same way (S4, incl. the dropped-everything second instance predicted from the root), and `No`'s verdict went UNKNOWN off a fields-side UNKNOWN even when an earlier match had already decided non-firing (S1 — kernel-message-unobservable since `ValidierungsErgebnis` merges FALSE/UNKNOWN and the DSL has no negation, but mechanism-divergent per `keinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern`'s early return).

**The fix, at the traversal root.** The one expansion became per-OPERAND and encounter-ordered (`VlqOperand`: cells in authored order — `null`=empty / `Val.Unknown`=malformed / present value — plus `filtered`/`omittedTail`/`extentUnknown`), and the single-pass `vlqRes` computes truth + fired polarity in the operator's OWN pass — one evaluator, three pass structures as its dispatch, no second quantifier AST/side model/filter evaluator: `AtLeastOne` = complete values (a filtered operand marks polarity only on a PRESENT selection) then fields to the deciding match (polarity = the decider's filter); `No` = poison-sensitive values (UNKNOWN member suppresses; empty/tail/reached filter → sticky omission) then the ordered fields scan (match decides FALSE before every later UNKNOWN/filter/tail; reached UNKNOWN first suppresses; the partial extent gate sits at the operand's scan position); `NotAll` = fields-presence prepass (no present cell → FALSE, values never consumed) then values (No-style) then the restarted fields scan (polarity = the witness's filter ∨ values omission). The typed walks consume `vlqRes` directly (one expansion where truth+type previously took two). Per-cell classification is untouched — type admission, category projection (SPEC-2026-07-22-05), scale-19 `ValCompare.equal`, index-local UNKNOWN (IF60), the `Having` keep-law (IF74), and the partial-star extent arms (SPEC-2026-07-22-09) all preserved; the EXPANSION stays eager per side, so the read set — and the compute-mode poison surface, IF219's adjacent territory — is byte-identical.

**Supersedes the IF74/IG48-era polarity generalization** "a Having-bearing operand forces OMISSION on fire, unconditionally" — over-drawn from single-operand evidence (with one operand the filtered operand always IS the decider/reached, so every prior lock stays green; `ValueListQuantifierHavingDiffTest` unchanged and passing).

*Locked:* tri-engine `adapter.laws.ValueListQuantifierOrderDiffTest` (the six SPEC-2026-07-23-16 separator families S1–S6: both No orders + fires-control, filtered-nonwitness vs filtered-decider for `AtLeastOne`/`NotAll`, values-side present/empty/dropped selections, `No`'s reached filtered absence, `NotAll`'s prepass-before-poison; groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter on codes AND polarity — the java-static polarity via `validationSignaturesSorted`) + multiplatform `interpreter … ValueListQuantifierOrderEvalTest` (JVM + Node: the polarity twins plus the decided-vs-suppressed internal-verdict pins — `Tri.FALSE` for match-before-UNKNOWN, `Tri.UNKNOWN` for the reverse, eager values poison, prepass-before-poison). Fix in `commonMain` (`ValueListQuantEval.kt`, `PolarityWalk.kt` dispatch) → JVM + JS. Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §8 "Runtime semantics of the shape-B QUANTIFIERS" (the three pass structures + witness-specific filter polarity); canonical prose KERNEL-SEMANTICS §8 + the §12 non-escalating-family note.

### IF219 — the group-list quantifiers read the entity list in ORDER on the compute path (short-circuit poison) and admit a starred group scope MIXED with plain operands (FIXED 2026-07-24; graduated from IG102, reconciles the SPEC-2026-07-23-09 compute supplement) <a id="if219"></a>

Two divergences surfaced by the IG102 probe of the FIXED group-list quantifiers used as a COMPUTATION precondition — both rooted in ONE stale assumption. The compute dispatch (`Interpreter.evalPred`) routed only the FIELD-fill family through the kernel's ordered poison-on-read scan (`computeMode && !op.countsGroups → computeFillQuantifier`); the GROUP-fill family kept the eager `quantCounts` tally on the comment's premise that "group presence reads the fill caches, not cell values" — false since IF216 made FIELD operands representable in these forms. So (1) the eager tally READ EVERY operand, poisoning on a formally-invalid field even when an earlier operand had already decided the quantifier — where the kernel's `…EntityAngegeben` read STOPS at the deciding operand (`build/ig102-probe4.log`: `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(filledField, malformedField)` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(filledGroup, malformedField)` COMPUTE on both kernel routes but CLEARED on the interpreter); and (2) a STARRED group scope MIXED with other operands (`AtLeastOneGroupFilled(field, Coverages*)`) threw `UnsupportedConstructException: unsupported operand Star` in `quantTally`/`pathOf` — in validation AND compute — where the kernel evaluates it (`build/ig102-probe5.log`).

**The fix, at the shared read root.** A single lazy per-operand state stream `Interpreter.groupFillStates(args): Sequence<Tri>` is the one source for both phases: a checked FIELD operand yields `presenceTri` (poison-capable in compute via `filledTri`), a bare GROUP operand yields its IF193 product state (structural in compute — `erroneous`=false, so a malformed-only group is FILLED, never poison), and a starred repeatable GROUP scope yields one `Tri.TRUE` per instantiated (Having-kept) row (the IF68 count, so `n` is the yielded count, not `args.size`). Validation folds it to the `(t, f, n)` tally `quantCounts` already fed `quantifierFires` (the single-star `starGroupTally` and the eager group branch of `quantTally` are subsumed — identical tally, plus mixed stars); compute consumes it lazily in `computeGroupFillQuantifier`, whose per-operator short-circuit (`any`/`none`/`all`, the composite two passes) STOPS at the deciding operand exactly as the kernel does — a malformed field after the decision is never read. Mode-dependence lives entirely in `presenceTri`/`filledTri`, so the one stream serves both. The polarity walk was already routed through `quantCounts` (unchanged). Kernel truth: a FIELD read poisons (a plain absent field is empty-as-0, IF77), a GROUP read is structural, a mixed starred scope counts instantiated rows.

*Locked:* tri-engine `adapter.laws.GroupListEntityOperandComputeDiffTest` (field/group clean·invalid·absent poison-vs-clear-vs-value, read-order stop for a decisive field/group before a malformed field, mixed starred scope instantiated/empty — discriminated by a dependent `Audit = [Payout]`; groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter) + mixed-star VALIDATION arms in `adapter.laws.StarredGroupQuantifierDiffTest` (a field beside `Waived*`, fires + polarity) + multiplatform `interpreter.GroupListFieldOperandComputeTest` (JVM + Node). Fix in `commonMain` (`Interpreter.groupFillStates` / `quantCounts` / `computeGroupFillQuantifier`, the `evalPred` compute dispatch) → JVM + JS. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §1 supplement "In a COMPUTATION precondition the read is ORDER-observable".

### IF220 — an over-limit row is outside the parallel-iteration join (empty-as-0, never its poisoned cell) (FIXED 2026-07-24; graduated from IG103) <a id="if220"></a>

`d961c44d`/IF27 excluded over-limit rows from the star-aggregate/quantifier/filter domain (the kernel's cache-admission gate), but the index-keyed **parallel-iteration** outer join (`Document.parallelContexts`) still read its rows unfiltered — `indexMap` (the per-group index-value → row map), `parentRowsOf` (the common-parent rows), and the frame-row expansion all consulted `rowsWithPrefix`/`rowsOf` directly. So an over-limit row joined: its index value became a join key, and a reference to it read its **over-limit cell**. In VALIDATION this was unobservable (the cell reads unavailable, so a cross-group compare is suppressed either way — the interpreter already agreed), but in COMPUTE it POISONED the reader where the kernel — which never inserts an over-limit coordinate into its `IndexFieldCache` — reads the unmatched side **empty-as-0** (`build/ig103-probe1.log`: `Total[Extra] = VALUE|0` on both kernel routes, `CLEARED` on the interpreter, when `Total[Extra]`'s only Demand match was an over-limit `Demand[3]=Extra` row).

**The fix, at the shared `overLimitRows` root.** The same `overLimitRows(group)` decision `starRows`/`rowContextsScoped` use (one document-owned `declaredReps` fact, no second walker) now filters all three parallel-join reads: `indexMap` skips over-limit rows so they are not join keys, `parentRowsOf` drops an over-limit common-parent row, and the frame-row expansion drops an over-limit frame row (`/Plan/X(frame)/A ⋈ B`). An excluded row's index value is absent from the join, so the reference reads unmatched (empty-as-0), exactly as the kernel does; the physical row still emits `zuGrosseZeile` and stays `GroupFilled`-visible (those readers keep consulting `rowsOf` directly, IF27). Per-row rule suppression via the unavailable cell (also IF27) is unchanged, so validation stays byte-identical.

*Locked:* tri-engine `adapter.laws.OverLimitParallelIterationDiffTest` (plain join validation + compute, framed `/Plan/X/A ⋈ B` validation over an over-limit frame row and an over-limit framed-group row, and framed compute over an over-limit shared-side row; dynamic-Groovy == java-static == interpreter) + multiplatform `interpreter.OverLimitParallelIterationTest` (JVM + Node). Fix in `commonMain` (`Document.indexMap` / `parentRowsOf` / `parallelContexts` frame-row expansion) → JVM + JS. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 over-repetition supplement "The same exclusion holds in the index-keyed PARALLEL ITERATION".

### IF222 — index invalidity on the compute parallel join is PARENT-COORDINATE scoped, not global: detection pools per parent and marking keys on the shared prefix (FIXED 2026-07-25; found while answering a12-kernel-lean EXP-2026-07-25-03) <a id="if222"></a>

`ComputationEngine`'s parallel-join guard was coordinate-blind in **both** of its steps: `invalidIndexGroup` pooled every row of an index group across *all* parents (`doc.contextsFor(group)`) before testing empty/duplicate, and the marking loop then cleared **every** instance of the computation. The kernel is scoped at both steps — `IndexFieldCache.getInvalidIndexGroupParentIndices` records invalidity **per parent coordinate** of the index group, and `CalculationCache.markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated` marks only instances whose own indices, **truncated to the path prefix shared with that index group's parent** (`numSharedLevels` + non-repeatable padding), match one of those coordinates.

**One root, two symptoms — the second predicted from the root and reproduced before the fix** (found while answering a12-kernel-lean's EXP-2026-07-25-03):
- **over-marking.** With a malformed key in the computed field's **own on-path** index group, the shared prefix reaches *below* the sibling level, so the kernel spares a sibling parent row's target (`VALUE|200`) — the interpreter cleared it.
- **false duplicates.** Index uniqueness holds within one parent's row set, so the same key value under two different parent rows is not a duplicate — the pooled check saw one and cleared both targets where the kernel computes both.

**The fix, at the shared root:** `invalidIndexParentCoords` groups a group's contexts by their parent coordinate and returns only the invalid ones; `markingKey` computes each target instance's test coordinate as its indices truncated to the prefix shared with the index group's parent, padded to that parent's depth. A malformed key that lies outside every target's shared prefix now marks nothing, and surviving instances still compute (the guard only short-circuits when all were marked). This makes the blast radius **locus-dependent by construction**, which is the kernel behavior: off-path groups truncate at the common ancestor and still clear everything beneath it (deliberate over-marking the kernel's own comment concedes), on-path groups distinguish siblings.

**Scope note on [IF96](#if96):** its "an invalid index anywhere clears every instance" is correct for the fixture it was verified on — A/B/C/Target as mutual *siblings*, where every group is off every other's path, so the shared prefix is the common ancestor and clearing everything is right. It over-generalized to the on-path locus, which that fixture could not reach. IF96's locks all stay green under this fix.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.ParallelIterationIndexMarkingDiffTest` — four cases, all three engines, on-path / off-path / both-parents-same-key / clean control. Kernel truth: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "the parallel-iteration computation route".)*

### IF221 — the two corners of the enum index S-value staging: a `$field.value$` message reads the ORIGINAL cell (staged→empty), and an out-of-domain S-value is rejected on load (FIXED 2026-07-24; graduated from IG104) <a id="if221"></a>

IF217 landed the phase-specific staging on the `code@pointer` message set, leaving two corners the SPEC-2026-07-23-14/-15 gate flagged (IG104). Both are now probed and locked.

**(a) A fired message's `$field.value$` interpolation over a STAGED index cell renders the cell EMPTY, not the staged token — a real interpreter DIVERGENCE, now fixed.** The §13 interpolation estate never saw a staged cell. The kernel splits the two reads (KERNEL-FINDINGS §4/§12 two-corner supplement): the staged S-value lives only in the transient evaluation cache (`ValidationCache.datenCache`, which the CONDITION consults — so the rule FIRES on the token), while `$field.value$` substitution reads `getReplacementForParam` → `validationCache.getDaten().getValueForDisplay(id)` — the ORIGINAL caller document, which never sees that cache — then falls to the enum's empty format default (`build/ig104a.log`: both kernel routes render `service level []`; the interpreter rendered `service level [STD]` because it read its staged call-local view). **The fix, at the message-rendering root:** a `$field.value$` interpolation now resolves against the ORIGINAL caller document, not the staged eval view — `renderMessage` gained a `messageValueOf` seam that `DmInterpreter` feeds `pass.callerDoc.messageDisplayValueAt(path, ctx.repsOf(path))` (the pre-staging document threaded through `ValidationPass.callerDoc`); the condition/typing walk still reads the staged `call`. This is the interpreter's mirror of `datenCache` (eval) vs `getDaten()` (display). The sibling site was `evalRule` (`testRule`/`evalCandidate`), fixed at the same root; when nothing is staged `callerDoc === call`, so every other message is byte-identical.

**(b) An out-of-domain enum `sValue` is rejected LOUD on load — a foreign model the typed builders refuse to author.** The typed surface fail-fasts (`FieldTypeSpec.EnumSpec` validates the token against the declared values), so a well-formed dmkit model can never carry it; but a raw DM-JSON foreign model could. The kernel's stance is unambiguous (`build/ig104b-probe.log`): an out-of-domain S-value is `MVK_S_VALUE_INVALID` at `checkConsistency`, and BOTH strategies REFUSE to generate validation code (there is NO runtime staged-formal-check path).
