**The fix** drops the pattern grouping and takes the cover over the union of every addressing entity: each becomes a box over the iterated levels (a concrete index pins that level, `ALL` — or a level deeper than the entity's own path — spans it), and `boxesCoverEveryTuple` asks whether the boxes cover the full cross-product. Recursing on the wildcarding boxes first keeps it linear in the entity count on the enumerate-every-row shape the perf guard measures; the descent is bounded by the concrete indices actually present, never by the declared repetition counts.

**The union is right here and wrong one gate over.** The wildcard-only gate is deliberately *not* a union: two entities that each pin a different outer row and wildcard the inner one cover every cell between them, and the kernel is still silent (IF259's third reading). Completeness composes across entities; wildcard-ness does not. The two gates now differ in exactly that, which is the distinction the shared helper had blurred.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialNestedAggregateDiffTest` (`:adapter`), whose previously "mixed" case turned out to share one pattern and is relabelled; its comparison is now a firing count rather than a set of codes.

### IF261 — a bounded reuse cache is not a fix for a sharing invariant: two interleaved clusters defeated it completely (2026-08-08) <a id="if261"></a>

[IF256](#if256) stopped the façade copying a pointer list the engine shares, using a one-slot-per-channel identity cache justified by "the sharing is per cluster and the two channels alternate".

**Discriminator:** 40 rows alternating between two duplicate keys, so findings alternate between two source lists. Every call misses the single slot: **40 distinct instances across 40 findings** — the full O(N²) allocation back, at the boundary IF256 was written to protect.

Nothing bounds how many duplicate clusters a document may interleave, so no slot count is defensible; picking one is the same "bound asserted rather than measured" this whole sequence is about, and enlarging it would only move the discriminator.

**The fix removes the copy instead of caching it.** The engine builds its shared cluster projection as an `immutableListSnapshot`, and the boundary publishes an already-immutable list as-is (`isImmutableListSnapshot`). No cache, no scale at which it degrades; a list from anywhere else is still copied, so the published list is immutable either way. The invariant is now a property of the value rather than of a cache's hit rate.

*Locked by:* `perf/FacadeProjectionSharingTest` (`:adapter` — the dense single cluster and the alternating pair, both asserted by list identity, no clock).

### IF262: the partial fuzz could not generate its gate's deciding shapes, and adding them exposed an "any wildcard wins" defect (FIXED 2026-08-08, graduated from IG112) <a id="if262"></a>

The partial-validation fuzz previously varied document values and relevant subsets, but every generated relevant pointer had concrete coordinates. Its 180 cases could not enter either starred-extent gate's wildcard branches, and its nested model carried neither a nested single-star aggregate nor a multi-star operand. This was a structural hole, not a shortage of seeds.

The generator now has three deterministic relevant-set shape classes: the original concrete-only class plus two wildcard-placement classes. A coverage meter fails unless the exact deciding axes occur: an explicit wildcard, mixed wildcard/concrete coordinates within one identifier, and multiple wildcard patterns at one path. The fuzz catalog adds a row-bound `SumOfProducts(Parts*/Weight, Parts*/Weight)` and a legal `Sum(Lines*/Parts*/Weight)`. Against the previous interpreter, that extension went red on **54 of 180** document/relevance combinations, all interpreter-only divergences; after the root fix, all 180 agree across interpreter, Groovy-dynamic kernel, and static-Java kernel.

**Observed discriminator.** A `NumberOfDifferentValues(Lines*/Price)` rule whose error field is `Price` received a direct field wildcard together with concrete relevant `Lines` rows. The kernel was silent and the interpreter fired. The required second instance used `Sum(Lines*/Price)` with a separate `Total` error field and reproduced the same split, proving this was not error-field rule gating or one operator's implementation. A complete field wildcard alone fired in both engines, as did a wildcard broad enough to encompass the concrete siblings. `SumOfProducts` also fired under complete wildcard field extents in both engines, proving its separate completeness gate must not be folded into this fix.

**Source-discriminated mechanism.** The kernel does not ask whether any caller pointer wildcards the star. `ValidationCommand.initialisiereRelevanteFelder` first expands relevant groups into descendant field identifiers; `compressRelevantFieldsForValidationCall` stores them per field name; `ValidationCache.reduceRedundancies` removes an identifier only when another wildcard identifier encompasses it. `EntityIterator.certainlyNotInSameSubtree` then filters to the current iteration subtree, and `existiertEbeneMitSternUndTeilvalidierungUngleich` rejects the aggregate extent if any identifier still present is concrete at a starred iterator level. The rival "any wildcard wins" mechanism predicts firing for the mixed discriminator and is falsified by both kernel differentials.

The interpreter's `starRelevantOf` gate now mirrors that sequence: expand relevant ancestors to the operand path, remove identifiers encompassed by broader wildcard identifiers, filter to the current row, then require every remaining identifier to wildcard every iterated star level. Reduction is cached per operand path, so the row hot path does not repeatedly perform the pairwise pattern work. `SumOfProducts` retains `starFullyEnumeratedOf`.

**Amended 2026-08-08 — this gate serves the four all-rows aggregates ONLY.** The original text ended "the same shared gate serves all-rows aggregates and starred value-list quantifiers", which was an inference from one implementation serving two call sites and is **falsified**: the kernel fires a starred `No` and suppresses `Sum` on the same relevant set. The value-list site is existential and now has its own `starExtentKnownOf` ([IF264](#if264)).

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest` for the exact reduction matrix, the independent `Sum` reproduction, and the `SumOfProducts` boundary; `laws/PartialNestedWildcardScopeDiffTest`, `PartialMultiStarScopeDiffTest`, `PartialValidationValueListDiffTest`, and `PartialNestedAggregateDiffTest` for sibling gates; `perf/PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` for the structurally metered 180-case tri-differential.

### IF263: a coverage claim beside its timing guard has two owners and can drift (FIXED 2026-08-08, graduated from IG111) <a id="if263"></a>

`InterpreterPerfCoverageTest` derived the eight public `DmInterpreter` evaluation signatures, but coverage came from a hand-written map from each signature to a test class and method name. A second check proved the target still existed and carried `@Test`. That could not prevent the map pointing at an unrelated test, and a test rename required an otherwise meaningless synchronized edit. The root defect was duplicated ownership: the timing test contained the measurement while another file owned the claim that it was the measurement.

The side map is gone. Each timing method now owns a `@TimesInterpreterEntryPoint` declaration with the method name and typed parameter classes. `InterpreterPerfCoverageTest` independently discovers the adapter's live JUnit plan, accepts declarations only on `@Test` methods a performance lane selects, resolves each declaration against the real `DmInterpreter` method, and compares that derived signature set with the independently reflected entry-point set. A renamed test carries its declaration with it; a removed test removes its declaration; a new overload changes the reflected denominator; a wrong method name or parameter list fails resolution. *(Amended 2026-08-13: the accepted set was literally `@Tag("perf")`. When the performance lanes split into a routine `perf` tier and a checkpoint `perf-bench` tier, that rejected `PerfBenchTest`'s real `compute` declaration — the check now accepts either lane tag, and the closed pair is itself guarded by `PerfTestClassificationTest`. An **untagged** claim is still rejected, which is the invariant: a claim no performance lane selects could be satisfied by a test that never times anything.)*

The exact scope matters. This removes the stale class/method pointer and co-locates the coverage declaration with the code that performs the timing. It does not bytecode-instrument a test body to prove that an honest annotation's text matches every call it makes. The behavioral timing assertions and kernel preflights remain the oracle for the guard's work; this denominator proves ownership and complete declared coverage.

*Locked by:* `InterpreterPerfCoverageTest` (`:adapter`, ordinary lane). Red phase: after deleting the map and before adding declarations, the derived guard set was empty against all eight reflected entry points; adding the declarations made the exact-set check green.

### IF264: the starred value-list extent gate is EXISTENTIAL where the all-rows aggregate gate is universal (FIXED 2026-08-08) <a id="if264"></a>

[IF262](#if262) established that the four all-rows aggregates take their wildcard gate **universally** over a reduced identifier set, and the interpreter applied that predicate at both starred-extent call sites — the aggregates and the value-list quantifiers — on the strength of one implementation serving both. The two gates do not agree. Seven relevant-set shapes, one model, one document, a starred `No` beside a starred `Sum`, read per engine:

| relevant set | starred `No` | starred `Sum` |
|---|---|---|
| field wildcard alone | fires | fires |
| **field wildcard + concrete group rows (semantic root)** | **fires** | **silent** |
| field wildcard + a concrete identifier for the SAME field | fires | fires |
| concrete field identifier + wildcard group | fires | fires |
| all-semantic field wildcard + concrete group rows | fires | fires |
| **field wildcard + concrete group rows (physical root)** | **fires** | **silent** |
| concrete group rows only | silent | silent |

Dynamic-Groovy and static-Java agree on every cell, so this is not an [IF136](#if136) strategy split. Rows 2 and 6 are the discriminator and rows 1/7 are the positive controls that keep them from reading as "the value-list gate is absent": with no wildcard at all the value-list entry is UNKNOWN too.

**Row 3 is what makes the reading precise, and it is why this is not a retraction of IF262.** A wildcard identifier beside a *concrete identifier for its own field* fires on **both** sides — IF262's reduction removes the encompassed sibling exactly as measured. What the aggregate gate does and the value-list gate does not is carry the **expanded group rows** into the same universal test. So the split is about which identifiers reach the gate, not about wildcards as such, and the aggregate mechanism in IF262 stands unchanged.

**Consequence and fix.** Folding the value-list site into IF262's reduction made the interpreter suppress a `No`/`NotAll`-values entry the kernel fires — a divergence shipped in `3ce95a05` and live until this finding. `EvalConfig` now carries `starExtentKnown` beside `starRelevant`; `DmInterpreter.starExtentKnownOf` is the existential predicate (the pre-IF262 form), keeping the star anchor of [IF259](#if259) and the per-iteration-row scope of [IF257](#if257) — only the reduction/universal layer is aggregate-only. `ValueListQuantEval` reads the new seam.

**Why the standing nets missed it.** The 180-case partial fuzz agrees before and after: its value-list rules never receive a field wildcard beside concrete group rows, which is [IG112](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md)'s shape gap one level deeper — the generator now emits wildcards, but not the *combination* that separates two gates answering the same structural question. The finding came from asking for a discriminating case for a claim that had been carried by shared implementation, which is the [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) "match the oracle to the CLAIM KIND" rule applied to a mechanism claim.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialExtentGateShapeDiffTest` (the seven-shape table, both kernel strategies, both operators side by side) and `laws/PartialValidationValueListDiffTest.oneWildcardEstablishesTheExtentEvenBesideConcreteRows` (with its wildcard-removed positive control).

### IF265: the relevance reduction was quadratic in DISTINCT wildcard shapes, which no timing guard could see (FIXED 2026-08-08) <a id="if265"></a>

[IF262](#if262)'s dominance reduction costs `identifiers x distinctWildcardMasks x depth`. The mask count is bounded by `min(identifiers, 2^depth)`, which reads as "linear for a fixed path depth" — and that reading is what let the cost ship unmeasured. It is wrong wherever `2^depth` exceeds the relevant-set size, because the mask count then grows *with* the set and the reduction is quadratic.

**Measured, on a 14-level model with the identifiers' masks forming an ANTICHAIN** (every mask wildcards exactly 7 of the 14 levels, so no identifier removes any other and every probe is wasted work):

| distinct wildcard masks | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| 1500 | 337 ms | 10 ms |
| 3000 | 1306 ms | 17 ms |
| growth on doubling | **3.86x** — quadratic | **1.69x** |

The antichain is the shape that matters and the reason the first attempt at this measurement read a reassuring 1.26x: the dominance scan **short-circuits** on its first hit, so a set whose masks do dominate each other exits after a few probes however many masks it holds. A guard for this has to construct the incomparable case deliberately.

**The fix is a strictly-larger-mask precondition, not a cache.** A candidate is removable only by a tuple whose wildcard mask is a **strict superset** of its own, so masks are grouped by how many levels they wildcard and a candidate skips every group that cannot exceed it — in the antichain, all of them, without touching a single mask. Masks are `Long` bitmaps, so the surviving comparisons are two integer ops instead of building and hashing a fresh list. A path deeper than 63 segments falls back to the original pairwise scan, which is equivalent and unreachable in practice.

**What this does NOT claim.** The residual worst case — a set mixing many different wildcard counts, where each low-breadth candidate really must consult the higher-breadth masks — is still `identifiers x masks`, now with a cheap constant. Subset-query indexing would be needed to remove that, and nothing observed motivates it.

**Provenance.** Found by an owner review of a review: the coverage claim was that `validatePart` is timed, and that is true — `PartialSumOfProductsPerfTest` times it. But it grows the relevant set with identifiers of one shape and exercises the `SumOfProducts` gate, so it could not see this. The `p <= 2^depth` bound in the original analysis was correct and the conclusion drawn from it was not.

*Locked by:* `StarRelevanceReductionGrowthTest` (`:interpreter` jvmTest, kernel-free, current-thread CPU time per the algorithmic-work exception) — the antichain builder carries a `check` that the requested mask count was actually available, so it cannot silently measure a smaller set than it claims.

### IF266: two sources for "which index cells duplicate", and the evaluation sites read the relevance-blind one (FIXED 2026-08-08, graduated from IG113) <a id="if266"></a>

Under partial validation the kernel builds its index cache from the RELEVANT fields only ([IF39](#if39)), so a duplicated index cell compromises the rule it feeds **only when its duplicate PARTNER is also relevant**. `AutoChecks.duplicateIndexCells` takes the relevance predicate and honours that. `IndexColumn` derived `duplicated` from every cell in the column with no relevance filter — and `IndexColumn` is what the *evaluation* sites read. So the fact had two sources and the consumers all consulted the blind one: `ValueListQuantEval`'s compromised-index path, `ExprEval` at four sites (`anyEmptyOrDuplicated`, the two indexed reads, `anyNotCheckRelevant`), and `Interpreter`'s index-aware fill tally. Not an `AtLeastOne` bug — a class, of which `AtLeastOne` was merely the operator the fuzz happened to reach.

**Measured** on a four-row index column carrying `A, A, B, ∅`, both kernel strategies agreeing on all seven relevant-set shapes: the rule is suppressed exactly on `{the non-matching row, the matching row's group}` and on the all-wildcard set — the two shapes where both `A` cells are relevant. A relevant cell whose partner is outside the set fires.

**Fix, corrected 2026-08-09 after the full lifecycle measurement.** `Interpreter.indexColumnOf` is the shared call boundary. Relevance is checked first; a non-relevant cell participates in neither projection nor collision and enters the call-local column as unavailable, so it compromises an unmatched read without invoking a custom validator. A relevant cell then passes the same built-in/custom formal admission gate as the other identity consumers. `IndexColumn` resolves only admitted typed values and applies the one shared collision policy. The earlier sentence that empty/malformed cells marked the column irrespective of relevance was unmeasured and is withdrawn; the direct semantic partial differential and zero-callback host control now establish the complete ordering.

**The constraint that shaped the threading.** `IndexColumn` is memoized per (column, star-scope prefix), because a per-lookup rebuild is O(rows) and a per-row indexed rule then O(rows²) (`PerfGateTest.semanticIndexStaysSubQuadratic`). The document owns the raw model-derived column. A relevance/custom-admission-scoped column may not enter that cache because it depends on the call configuration, so `Interpreter.indexColumnOf` owns a call-local memo. Validation shares it for the pass; computation shares it for one computation step and bypasses it only for the step's own changing target overlay.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialAtLeastOneScanDiffTest` (the seven-shape duplicate table), `laws/NumericIndexKeyDiffTest.aNonRelevantSemanticIndexCellIsUnknownRatherThanSelectedOrAbsent` (direct semantic presence/absence 3VL), and `CustomFieldTypeMultiplatformTest.aNonRelevantCustomIndexCellIsNotAdmittedOrValidated` (relevance-first callback discriminator).

### IF267: a starred value-list entry must not read a cell outside the relevant set (FIXED 2026-08-08, graduated from IG113) <a id="if267"></a>

The extent gate ([IF264](#if264)) answers *"do I know every row?"*. It does not answer *"may I read THIS row?"*, and the interpreter asked only the first: `vlqOperands` classified every star cell regardless of relevance. `AtLeastOne` never consults the extent at all, so for it the second question was the only gate there was — and it had none.

**Measured** with cells `A, B, C` and the value list `In "A"`: with only row 2 relevant, the kernel is SILENT — the sole matching cell sits in a row the caller did not select. With row 1 relevant, or the path wildcarded, it fires. Both kernel strategies agree. A non-relevant cell contributes nothing to the scan.

**What is deliberately NOT settled.** Whether the kernel *skips* such a cell or classifies it UNKNOWN is not discriminated by any case that could be constructed: the two differ only for the `No`/`NotAll` family, and there the extent gate fires first on every subset shape that excludes a cell — a relevant set narrow enough to exclude one cell is never wildcard enough to establish the extent. The implementation skips. A case that separates them would need an operator that reads cells without reading the extent *and* distinguishes an absent cell from an unknown one; none exists in the catalog today. `PartialValueListCellRelevanceDiffTest`'s second block measures the masking rather than asserting the equivalence.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialValueListCellRelevanceDiffTest`.

### IF268: the rule-run gate compares only the levels the rule's ITERATION bound (FIXED 2026-08-08, graduated from IG113) <a id="if268"></a>

The kernel runs a rule only where its error field is relevant — the interpreter's long-standing gate, and correct. What it got wrong is WHICH repetition it compares. The comparison is over the levels the rule's iteration actually BOUND. Below them no coordinate was ever chosen: the kernel defaults it to the first repetition for the message POINTER, and does not require the relevant set to name that repetition. The interpreter compared the full resolved instance, so a rule whose error field lives in a repeatable group *deeper* than its own iteration was silently skipped.

**Corrected 2026-08-08, before this finding had shipped a day:** the first fix read the bound level off the rule's DECLARED group (`RuleDef.group`). That is wrong by this module's own law — rule iteration is **reference-driven** and the declared group drives nothing (`IterationScopes.prepare`, `IterationLawsTest`). The two blocks below could not see it: the first uses a star-only rule, whose iteration anchor *happens* to equal its declared group, and the second declares the rule on the group it iterates. An external review supplied the case that separates them — declared on `/Order`, non-starred references reaching `/Order/Items`, so the plan iterates `Items` — where the kernel gates per row and the declared-group reading fired all three rows at row 1. The bound count now comes from the **row context** (`EvalContext.boundLevelsOf`), which is the only thing that knows what it actually bound; `Paths.boundLevels`, the path-algebra version, is deleted rather than left as a second answer to the same question.

**Measured, two blocks that only settle the law together** (both kernel strategies, `PartialRuleGateScopeDiffTest`):

| declared on | iterates | error field | relevant | kernel |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/Order` (star-only refs) | `/Order` | `../Items/Tag` | `Items[2]/Tag` only | fires at **`Items[1]/Tag`** |
| `/Order` (star-only refs) | `/Order` | `../Items/Tag` | `Items[2]` (the group) | fires at `Items[1]/Tag` |
| `/Order` (star-only refs) | `/Order` | `../Items/Tag` | the sibling `Ack` only | silent |
| `/Order/Items` | `/Order/Items` | `../Tag` | `Items[2]/Tag` **+** `Items[3]/Note` | fires at `Items[2]/Tag` **only** |
| `/Order/Items` | `/Order/Items` | `../Tag` | `Items[2]/Note` only | silent |
| **`/Order` (refs into `Items`)** | **`/Order/Items`** | `../Items/Tag` | `Items[2]/Tag` only | fires at **`Items[2]/Tag`** only |
| `/Order` (refs into `Items`) | `/Order/Items` | `../Items/Tag` | every `Note`, no `Tag` | silent |

Row 1 falsifies the exact-instance reading. Row 4 falsifies the two coarser rivals in one shot: "the error PATH is relevant somewhere" would fire on every row, and "the rule's ELEMENT is relevant" would admit row 3, whose `Note` makes that element relevant. **Row 6 falsifies the declared-group reading** — same declared group as row 1, same error field, opposite answer, because the references moved the iteration. Rows 3, 5 and 7 keep the gate from collapsing into "always run": the error path must still be relevant *somewhere* under the bound levels.

**Fix.** `ValidationPass.runsForRow` replaces the direct `isRelevant(errorPath, ctx.repsOf(errorPath))` call; the bound count is `EvalContext.boundLevelsOf` (a `Document.Ctx` row reports its shared ancestry with the group it iterates, capped by the coordinates it carries; a `ParallelRowContext` reports each joined group's own depth); and `EffectiveRelevanceIndex.isRelevantWithin` answers the query off the same index the ordinary predicate uses — tuples and wildcard patterns projected onto the bound levels, memoized per (path, bound), which a rule fixes. Where the error field sits inside the iterated element — every per-row rule, so the overwhelming majority — the bound count covers every level and the query is byte-identical to the old one.

*Corrected 2026-08-09:* this paragraph used to end "The `ParallelRowContext` arm is reasoned from `repsOf`'s own binding, not measured; no probe in the estate reaches a parallel-iteration rule reporting below a joined group." **Both halves are now false.** `laws/PartialParallelJoinKeyDiffTest` runs partial validation over `Demand ⊥ Supply` with the rule at `/Staffing` reporting into `../Demand/Headcount` — below a joined group, including the `Demand[-5]` unmatched arm — tri-engine. That the arm is genuinely *load-bearing* there, rather than merely present, is a mutation result, not a reading: replacing the `ParallelRowContext` bound with the path's own length turns `aParallelRowExistsOnlyWhereItsIndexCellIsRelevant` red while `PartialRuleGateScopeDiffTest` stays entirely green — so the parallel arm is covered by that test and by nothing else.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialRuleGateScopeDiffTest`; the parallel arm additionally by `laws/PartialParallelJoinKeyDiffTest`.

### IF269: a parallel-iteration row exists only where its INDEX cell is relevant (FIXED 2026-08-08) <a id="if269"></a>

Parallel iteration is an outer join keyed by the groups' shared **index field**, and under partial validation the kernel builds that key cache from the RELEVANT cells only ([IF39](#if39)). So a relevant set that names the payload field but not the index produces **no join keys and therefore no iterations at all** — the rule never runs, whatever else is relevant. The interpreter built the join from every cell and over-fired.

**Measured**, `Demand ⊥ Supply` under `/Staffing` sharing `Department`; rule on `/Staffing`, error field `../Demand/Headcount`; both kernel strategies agreeing on all fourteen shapes:

| relevant set | kernel |
|---|---|
| `Demand[1]/Headcount` (payload only) | **silent** |
| payload only — other row, wildcarded, or both rows | **silent** |
| `Demand[1]` (the whole group) | fires at `Demand[1]` |
| `Demand[1]/Department` (index only, no payload) | silent |
| `Demand[1]/{Department, Headcount}` | fires at `Demand[1]` |
| `Demand[2]/{Department, Headcount}` | fires at `Demand[2]` |
| both rows' index + payload, or both wildcarded | fires at both |
| the far side only | silent |
| `Supply[1]/{Department, Headcount}` + `Demand[1]/Headcount` | fires at **`Demand[-5]`** |
| `Supply[2]=Ops` + `Demand[1]/Headcount` | fires at **`Demand[-5]`** |

Two independent gates have to hold, and the table separates them: **the index cell decides whether the ITERATION exists**, the error field decides whether the rule RUNS in it ([IF268]). `Demand[1]/Department alone` is the row that proves the second gate is still there; the group row is the one that shows why selecting a whole row works — a relevant group covers its own index cell.

**The unmatched side is the sharper half.** Leaving one group's index cell out of an otherwise relevant row does **not** remove the iteration — the far side still supplies the key, and this group becomes the outer join's "not specified" side, reporting at the kernel's `IIdentifier.UNKNOWN` repetition. That forced the second half of the fix: `ParallelRowContext.boundLevelsOf` must treat an unmatched group's own level as **unbound**, because nothing chose it. This is [IF268]'s principle one step further — what the iteration did not choose is not compared, whether because the level sits below the iteration or because the join found no row.

**Fix.** `Document.parallelContexts` takes the call's cell-relevance predicate and `indexMap` skips a row whose index cell is outside the set; `IterationScopes.iterationContexts` threads it; `DmInterpreter` supplies `config.cellRelevant`. Null everywhere else, so full validation and every compute consumer are unchanged.

*Corrected 2026-08-08:* skipping the row was only **half** the rule. A non-relevant index cell also **invalidates the column**, so the group's unmatched references read 3VL UNKNOWN rather than a definite empty — [IF271](#if271), found by the partial parallel fuzz. Every shape in the table above uses `FieldFilled(…) Or FieldFilled(…)`, which is TRUE whenever the far side is filled and therefore reads identically under both rules; the fourteen rows pinned the POINTER and left the VALIDITY unmeasured. The lesson is narrower than "add more shapes": a table of shapes measured through ONE operator can only settle what that operator's truth function can distinguish.

**Provenance.** Found while probing the ONE arm of [IF268]'s fix that had been reasoned rather than measured — whether a parallel context binds its joined groups' own levels. That reasoning was correct, and this defect was sitting next to it. The standing lesson is the cheaper one: the probe cost minutes and the arm it was written to check turned out fine, which is not an argument against having run it.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialParallelJoinKeyDiffTest` (fourteen shapes, both kernel strategies plus the interpreter; both halves of the fix independently mutant-checked).

### IF270: a DUPLICATED index value is never a selectable parallel-join key (FIXED 2026-08-08) <a id="if270"></a>

**Claim.** In the index-keyed parallel join a duplicated index value keys **nothing**: every colliding row leaves the group's `value → row` map. The group is separately marked invalid there, so a reference to that value is unmatched-into-an-invalid-group and reads 3VL UNKNOWN — which is what suppresses a negative operator like `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` over it. A *clean* value in the same compromised group still joins normally.

**The defect.** `Document.indexMap` did `byValue.put(v, row)` and only recorded invalidity on collision, so the **last** colliding row stayed selectable. It then matched definitely, and a rule fired one message the kernel never emits, pointed at that last row. The interpreter was faithfully implementing [KERNEL-FINDINGS](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md) §9's supplement, which asserted "a duplicate value still sits in the index cache (last write wins) and the union" — false, and contradicted by [KERNEL-SEMANTICS](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md) §10's own statement about the same `IndexFieldCache`. Root cause is therefore a **doc conflict the code inherited**, not a coding slip; the false supplement is corrected in place.

**Discriminators** (all three engines; `Demand ⊥ Supply` over `models/staffing.dm.json`):

| document | kernel | pre-fix interpreter |
|---|---|---|
| unique keys (control) | fires at `Demand[2]` and `Demand[-5]` | identical — so this is not a pointer/off-by-one defect |
| `{Dup, Dup}`, far side clean | silent | spurious fire at `Demand[2]` |
| `{Dup, Dup, Eng}` | fires at `Demand[3]` only | also fires at `Demand[2]` |
| far side ALSO carries `Dup` | silent | spurious fire at `Demand[2]` |
| the same key three times | silent | fires at `Demand[3]` |

Row 3 kills "an invalid index suppresses the whole group" (the clean value fires). Row 4 kills "the value simply leaves the union" (it is in the union from the far side, and the near side is *still* silent) — together they leave exactly one reading. Row 5 is the positive control for the collision set: a fix that only removes on the second occurrence re-inserts on the third.

An **empty** index cell was already exact — it never entered the map — which is what left the duplicate branch as the lone offender, and it is the sibling site the same sweep checked. The semantic-index column ([IF213](#if213)) also already excluded duplicates; the parallel join was the one index-keyed lookup that resolved a duplicate by row order.

**Provenance.** Not found by review or by reasoning: found by *de-duplicating* Harness G. `SiblingGroupFuzz.parallelCase` varies only on `caseIndex % 2` and its document depends solely on the doc seed, so the former `MODELS = 40 × DOCS = 5` loop ran 200 times over 10 unique comparisons and just **5 distinct documents**. Rebalancing to `PARALLEL_CASES × 40` documents — fewer total runs — reached doc seed 11 and turned it red immediately. The redundancy had been hiding the defect for as long as the harness existed.

*Locked by:* `laws/ParallelJoinDuplicateKeyDiffTest` (seven documents, both kernel strategies plus the interpreter; both the removal and the collision set independently mutant-checked, the latter detected by the triple-key row alone).

### IF271: a non-relevant index cell INVALIDATES the parallel-join column, not just its key (FIXED 2026-08-08) <a id="if271"></a>

**Claim.** Under partial validation, a row whose index cell is outside the relevant set is "not given" to the relevance-scoped `IndexFieldCache` — so it contributes no join key ([IF269]) **and** marks the group's index invalid for that parent, exactly as an EMPTY index cell does ([KERNEL-FINDINGS](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md) §9). One such cell compromises the whole column. Every unmatched reference into that group then reads 3VL **UNKNOWN** instead of a definite empty, which suppresses a negative operator over it.

**Why it hid.** [IF269] implemented only the key half, and its fourteen-shape law test could not see the difference: it measures `FieldFilled(Demand/Headcount) Or FieldFilled(Supply/Headcount)`, a positive operator that is TRUE whenever the far side is filled — identical whether the near side is UNKNOWN or definitely empty. The blind spot is the operator's truth function, not the number of shapes.

**Discriminators** (`Demand ⊥ Supply`, `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled(Demand/Headcount, Supply/Headcount)`; both kernel strategies agree):

| relevant set | kernel | pre-fix interpreter |
|---|---|---|
| every index cell relevant (control) | fires at `Demand[2]` and `Demand[-5]` | identical |
| this side's index cells ALL non-relevant | silent | fires at `Demand[-5]` twice |
| this side: only ONE row's index cell relevant | silent | fires at `Demand[-5]` |
| the FAR side: only one row's index cell relevant | silent | fires at `Demand[2]` |

The control is what makes the rest meaningful — a fully relevant column still fires exactly as under full validation, so this is not "partial relevance silences negative rules". Rows 3 and 4 settle the *scope* of the invalidity: it is per column and symmetric across the join, not "only when the group has no keys at all".

**Fix.** One branch in `Document.indexMap`: a non-relevant index cell now takes the same path as an empty one — `invalidOut.add(group)` before the `continue`.

**Provenance.** Found by the partial parallel-iteration fuzz on its first run (`SiblingGroupFuzzDiffTest.parallelIterationAgreesWithKernelUnderPartialRelevance`), which is the cell no other net reached: the only other partial net's model is a nested chain, and `IterationScopes.prepare` excludes an ancestor group from the parallel candidates, so that topology can never be planned as a parallel iteration at any seed count. Two of 240 combinations diverged; `FuzzDiagnostics.shrinkPartial` — the relevant-set twin of `shrink`, added for this — reduced both to the same three-entity set, after which the hand-built discriminators above were straightforward. Hand isolation had failed first for a reason worth recording: every attempt included the ROOT group entity, which expands onto every descendant, so five "different" relevant sets were silently the same set.

*Locked by:* `laws/PartialParallelJoinKeyDiffTest.aNonRelevantIndexCellInvalidatesTheColumnNotJustItsKey` (mutant-checked: reverting the fix fails ONLY this method, while the fourteen positive shapes stay green — the blind spot, demonstrated rather than asserted) and the fuzz regime above as the class-level net.

### IF272: every index consumer follows one admission, identity, ambiguity, and selection lifecycle (FIXED 2026-08-09; corrected in place after the follow-up review) <a id="if272"></a>

**Claim.** An index cell does not become a key merely because it has stored text. The kernel-observed lifecycle is `cache membership/relevance -> formal admission -> canonical key projection -> collision/ambiguity -> row selection or parallel join -> three-valued result -> message pointer`. An over-limit cell is outside the evaluation cache entirely: it contributes no key or collision and does not compromise a clean no-match. An in-cache cell that is non-relevant for the call, empty, built-in-invalid, or rejected by a registered custom type contributes no key and cannot collide with or be selected instead of an admitted peer; it does compromise the column where the kernel reports an unmatched read as UNKNOWN. Among admitted cells, a duplicated key selects no row. On a Number index field the legal spellings `5`, `5.0`, and `5.00` are one runtime key; every other measured kind remains exact stored text. `indexKeyOf` retains the kernel projection's raw fallback for a non-parsing Number token as a total pure function, but formal admission prevents such a stored token from entering an identity relation.

**Correction to the first version of this finding.** The first implementation unified only canonical key projection and claimed four complete sites. That was too narrow in two ways. First, identity was still formed from raw placements before formal admission, so malformed and custom-invalid cells could create collisions, joins, semantic matches, presence results, and compute invalidity. Second, the observable denominator is not four implementation sites: it is seven consumers, including scalar semantic read, semantic presence, the contained-field semantic scan, parallel join, `uniqueIndex`, starred `IndexColumn`, and computation. `RepetitionNotUnique` is not a `ModelDef.indexFields` consumer at all; it is a separate user-authored relation over arbitrary key fields, already measured by SPEC-2026-07-22-10.

**Discriminators.** Both kernel strategies agree on every row below. `5`/`5` is the textual control for every `5`/`5.00` collision, and every silence equivalence class has a firing peer on the same model and rule.

| input | kernel and fixed interpreter | pre-fix rival ruled out |
|---|---|---|
| admitted Number keys `5`, `5.00` in one group | `uniqueIndex` on both; the key selects nothing | raw-text equality treated them as distinct |
| `Offered: 5` vs `Quoted: 5.00` | one joined row | duplicate-only reasoning cannot explain a cross-group match with no collision |
| invalid `05 -> 10`, then admitted `5 -> 20`, lookup `5` | selects row 2; row 1 keeps `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen` | first raw placement selected row 1 or fabricated a collision |
| invalid `05`, admitted `5`, admitted `5.00` | admitted peers collide; key unavailable | skipping the invalid row but skipping collision policy selected one admitted peer |
| two malformed `x` cells | two format errors, no `uniqueIndex` | malformed raw text participated in uniqueness |
| registered custom index `bad`, payload present | project rejection only; measured semantic presence, starred fill/count aggregate, and entity-valued starred value-list stay silent; callback once per call | typed/raw starred consumers bypassed custom admission or repeated validation |
| registered custom-index validator unavailable | CELL unsupported; its raw key follows the deliberate lenient identity fallback, including duplicate removal; no document-level crash | the mandatory prepass rethrew a cached unsupported status before rules ran |
| partial semantic lookup with matching index excluded | `FieldFilled` and `FieldNotFilled` both silent | excluded key was selected or treated as a definite absence; the callback-first rival is killed by the zero-invocation host control |
| semantic key on row 3 beyond `declaredReps=2` | no selection; `FieldNotFilled` fires at `[-5]`, proving clean absence rather than UNKNOWN | instantiated structure was confused with index-cache membership |
| empty field-valued query beside a filled index cell; beside an empty index cell | clean column: `FieldNotFilled` and a fillable `FirstFilledValue(Group For key)` control fire; compromised column: scalar presence and contained-field group scan are UNKNOWN/silent | separate null-query short circuits treated both consumers as definite absence and bypassed column invalidity |
| one authored key expression targeting the repeated error field selects row 2; clean no-match | message pointer follows row 2; clean no-match uses `[-5]`; semantic refs to sibling payload fields do not add pointer targets | evaluation value and error context were resolved independently, or every semantic payload ref was counted |
| two distinct authored key expressions targeting the repeated error field, including `"5"` vs `"5.00"` and two field refs whose values select one row | ordinary row-1 error iteration | runtime-row or canonical-key equality incorrectly merged pointer targets |
| semantic target `/Offered/Details/Premium For "5"`, where `Offered` owns the index and `Details` repeats twice | the key selects `Offered[2]`; both `Details[1]` and `Details[2]` remain independently iterable and carry their own pointers | immediate-parent-only index lookup declined the rule; binding only the selected row lost the descendant coordinate |
| group target `/IndexedParents/Children For "0"`, where only `Children[2]` is filled; control target `/IndexedParents For "0"` over the same document | the descendant target points at `Children[2]`; the ancestor target defaults the error descendant to `Children[1]` | the selected indexed row alone supplied too few coordinates, while following the arbitrary first-filled payload would make both targets point at row 2 |
| one exact authored key target appears through both the ancestor group and a deeper group or exact error field, in both operand orders | both orders point at the deepest error-containing selector's `Children[2]` | first-AST-reference selection made message attachment operand-order dependent |
| repeated error field is referenced ordinarily, directly or through a containing group, beside a matching semantic group selector; direct-field control selector cleanly misses | every rule retains its physical error-field rows; the semantic group remains only a value operand | the star-free field walk missed ordinary group operands, while semantic pointer reanchoring selected one row or `[-5]` and suppressed ordinary iteration |
| invalid source or target index on a parallel computation | affected target instances CLEARED | compute used a different admission/collision relation |
| a semantic computation reads a scalar/group payload below an indexed ancestor or a field-valued query key produced by an earlier computation | selection and payload use the current overlay, including a nonrepeatable descendant group, not the raw absent cells | selection was overlay-aware but selected payload, descendant context, or query projection bypassed the cascade |

**Fix.** `ValueAdmissionCache` evaluates the value-owned gate once per concrete cell and is shared by formal reporting and identity construction. `IndexIdentityCandidate` plus `resolveIndexIdentity` is the one collision/ambiguity implementation: inadmissible/non-relevant/empty cells compromise without participating; admitted duplicates are absent from the unique map and retain all duplicate row pointers. `indexKeyOf` is the one raw-text projection and remains Number-only because that is the measured kernel basis. `nearestIndexFieldOf` is the single structural resolver for scalar/group semantic targets below an indexed ancestor. `Document.indexedContainedFieldCells` composes the selected indexed prefix with the current coordinates through the authored target group, rather than constructing a descendant context from the shorter index-field coordinates. `Document.indexedCellReps`, `Document.indexMap`, `AutoChecks.duplicateIndexCells`, `ComputationEngine.invalidIndexParentCoords`, and the typed `IndexColumn` all feed the same lifecycle. Starred value-list expansion consumes the admission-aware `IndexColumn`, not raw `starCells`. Validation combines relevance with admission at the call boundary, relevance first, so an excluded custom cell is neither validated nor admitted. The model-static pointer plan leaves physical iteration authoritative whenever the complete ordinary message-reference inventory contains the repeated error field, directly or through an expanded group operand; the star-free reference view remains a second guard for RNU-excluded keys. Otherwise it groups semantic refs by exact authored key target, chooses the deepest selector that contains the repeated error field independently of operand order, preserves coordinates through that selector's authored group path, and defaults only levels below it. Runtime key normalization remains a separate stage. Computation memoizes each stable admission-aware column once per step, bypasses the memo only for its own changing target overlay, and resolves both a field-valued query and the selected scalar/group payload through that overlay.

The public interpreter's custom-unavailable fallback remains deliberately tolerant: an unregistered value, or a registered validator that cannot run, participates exactly as before while the ordinary formal pass reports the cell unsupported. `CustomFieldTypeMultiplatformTest` locks the unique and duplicate cases on JVM and JS. This is project-owned degrade behavior, not a claim about what a kernel call without its required custom validator does.

**Performance and scope.** The resolver is linear in the candidate count. Document-level raw identity inventories and call-level typed columns are memoized by exact field and scope; the compute step shares the latter across outer rows. Numeric parsing/rendering is a per-cell constant and is now measured directly on validation and computation against both kernel strategies; [IF275](#if275) records the gate and closes the String-only evidence hole that existed when this finding first shipped.

*Locked by:* `laws/NumericIndexKeyDiffTest` for the seven-consumer tri-engine matrix, pointer/3VL controls, and nearest-indexed-ancestor plus descendant-iteration rows; `laws/FirstFilledValueEntitySpecDiffTest` for descendant group selection, exact pointers, deepest-selector precedence, both operand orders, and direct-field plus containing-group precedence over matching and no-match semantic selectors; `laws/IndexIdentityCustomTypeDiffTest` for registered custom admission, formal error visibility, starred fill/count/value-list consumers, and once-per-call invocation; `laws/SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest` for selected scalar/group payloads below an indexed ancestor and a field-valued query computed earlier in the cascade; `property/IndexIdentityStagePropertyTest` for exhaustive sequences of up to three classified candidates; `CustomFieldTypeMultiplatformTest` for relevance-first and unsupported-custom host behavior. Deliberately raw-keying the join/auto/compute sites, admitting malformed semantic rows, retaining a duplicate in the unique map, bypassing `IndexColumn` in the value-list expansion, admitting before relevance, merging exact pointer targets canonically, retaining the first equivalent pointer selector, allowing semantic reanchoring to override an ordinary error-field or containing-group reference, restoring immediate-parent-only semantic selection, dropping descendant iteration, or bypassing any compute overlay input makes its owning focused lock red.

### IF273: a coverage meter's relevance input must be the production query, while its classifier stays independent (FIXED 2026-08-09, graduated from IG115) <a id="if273"></a>

Harness G classified generated rows through an adapter-side mirror of the interpreter's private relevance query. Direct tests could lock the mirror and the interpreter's differential could run production relevance, but nothing compared the two. A drift in the over-reporting direction could therefore let the meter claim a runtime shape that no validation call actually reached. This exposed the denominator claim, not the differential result itself.

`EffectiveRelevanceIndex` is now the one implementation used by both `DmInterpreter.validatePart` and `FuzzDiagnostics.RelevantView`. It lives in an engine package that the build deliberately excludes from the frozen ABI contract, so the test instrument gains an exact production seam without publishing a supported user-facing relevance API. Its concrete-cell and bound-level queries share the same tuple/wildcard index; malformed coordinate arities remain inert and global fields remain unconditionally relevant.

The other half remains independent by design. Harness G does not ask production index identity code whether a generated key survives admission and collisions, because that would make a production defect self-certifying. `SiblingGroupFuzz.parallelRelevanceShapes` implements only the constrained String-key envelope the generator can produce, and its hand-built classifier law includes duplicate, empty, absent-placement, disjoint-key, complete-match, and compromised-column cases.

*Locked by:* `EffectiveRelevanceIndexTest` on JVM and JS for exact, ancestor, wildcard, multi-pattern, malformed-arity, global, and bound-level projection; `ParallelRelevanceShapeTest` for the independent classifier; `SiblingGroupFuzzDiffTest` for the tri-engine runtime result. Removing the ancestor/wildcard expansion makes the shared host law red; restoring collided-key survival or empty-string admission makes the classifier law red.

### IF274: a fuzz denominator needs one firing control per promised rule/shape class (FIXED 2026-08-09, graduated from IG117) <a id="if274"></a>

Harness G's partial differential used one `kernel ever fired` flag per rule shape. That proved each rule could fire somewhere, but it let one relevance shape vouch for every sibling shape. The coverage meter could therefore reach a promised `(rule, relevance shape)` class only on silent documents while an unrelated input supplied the positive flag. Random agreement would stay green even if that class exercised no observable rule result.

The denominator is now the finite cross-product of three parallel rule shapes and five promised relevance shapes. Every one of the 15 classes has a silent witness and a paired kernel-firing control on the same model, rule, and generated document; only the relevant set changes. Thirteen pairs reuse observations already in the 360-case deterministic matrix. The positive value-comparison rule needed two additional silent relevance seeds on document 6, and those share one all-relevant partial control, so the complete regime performs 363 distinct tri-engine observations rather than rerunning the Groovy oracle for control assertions.

The control table is itself exact-set checked against `PARALLEL_CASES × ParallelRelevanceShape.entries`, including duplicate-owner rejection. Each silence seed is reclassified by the live meter, must remain silent in the normative dynamic kernel, and must agree across dynamic Groovy, static Java, and the interpreter; its paired input must fire the same rule and likewise agree. `allRelevantPartialObservation` still calls the partial-validation entry point on all three engines, so a full-validation result is never used as a proxy.

*Locked by:* `SiblingGroupFuzzDiffTest.parallelIterationAgreesWithKernelUnderPartialRelevance`. Pointing one control at its own silent seed makes the focused law red. Adding a rule or promised relevance shape without a control fails the exact-set denominator before behavioral assertions run.

### IF275: the routine performance gate measures the Number-index lifecycle on validation and computation (FIXED 2026-08-09, graduated from IG116) <a id="if275"></a>

Every prior performance scenario used String index fields, where `indexKeyOf` returns after one kind comparison. That estate could guard the join's complexity class but could not measure the Number path added by [IF272](#if272), which parses and canonically renders every admitted index cell. A green String gate was therefore no evidence about the changed constant, and validation alone could not reach compute-side invalid-index detection.

The routine gate now has two typed Number-index scenarios over sibling repeatable groups. Validation reaches the automatic uniqueness pass and the parallel join: `A` starts with admitted keys `1` and `1.00`, while later A/B rows use value-equal, text-different spellings. Computation copies `A/Value` to `B/Value`; the same source collision compromises the Number index and makes every stale B target visibly `CLEARED`, so semantic agreement proves `ComputationEngine.invalidIndexParentCoords` actually ran. `ScenarioInventoryTest.routineGateCoversNumberIndexValidationAndComputation` locks both operations, both index fields, Number kind, and the required rule/computation so the scenarios cannot silently drift back to the String fast path.

At 500 and 2,000 rows in the complete isolated `scripts/perf.sh gate` run on 2026-08-09, validation measured interpreter **3.05/13.24 ms**, dynamic Groovy **21.33/124.67 ms**, and generated Java **17.96/117.57 ms**. Computation measured interpreter **1.75/7.57 ms**, dynamic Groovy **14.48/98.58 ms**, and generated Java **13.39/93.38 ms**. The fixed-size oracle is strict: exact tri-engine outcomes first, then interpreter faster than both kernel strategies at both sizes and below the 5-second catastrophe ceiling. The 4× curve is secondary evidence against a rescan: interpreter growth was 4.34× validation and 4.33× computation. (Corrected 2026-08-11: this run also recorded those factors as "no worse than either kernel strategy", which held only because this machine's kernel readings happened to measure *above* 4.34×; on a hosted runner the same code measured the kernel *below* the interpreter and the comparison failed. The cross-engine bar is now per-unit cost — [IF279](#if279).) The complete gate passed in **3m59s**.

*Locked by:* `PerfGateTest.numberIndexValidationBeatsBothKernelStrategiesAndCostsLessPerRow`, `PerfGateTest.numberIndexComputationBeatsBothKernelStrategiesAndCostsLessPerRow` (renamed 2026-08-11 — the old `…AndScalesNoWorse` named the retired cross-engine growth-ratio bar, [IF279](#if279)), and the cheap `ScenarioInventoryTest` envelope guard. Replacing Number canonicalization with raw text makes validation fail semantic agreement; removing compute invalid-index detection makes computation fail semantic agreement; changing either declared index field to String makes the ordinary structural guard red.

### IF276: the placement seam materializes the V2-effective ancestor and predecessor rows in linear work (FIXED 2026-08-09, graduated from IG114) <a id="if276"></a>

The original IG114 observation was right about evaluation and wrong about the builder mechanism. Kernel V1 `addEntityInstance` stores only the exact sparse placement. Before validation or computation, every runtime entry point converts V1 through `DocumentV1V2ConverterImpl.ofDocumentV1`; V2 repetition insertion pads positions before `n` with empty group instances and creates missing ancestors. The observable owner contract is therefore the V2-effective tree the runtime evaluates, not the mutable builder's immediate entity list.

Two tri-engine rows separate that conversion from ordinary row content. An explicit `Lines[5]` group makes the per-row `Qty < 5` rule fire OMISSION on rows 1 through 5. A field-only `Lines[5]/Qty = 7` placement makes it fire on rows 1 through 4. The first addressed row is itself empty content; the second is filled and does not satisfy the rule; both share the same four padded predecessors. Pre-fix, the interpreter instantiated only row 5 and missed the predecessor results. A structural nested lock establishes the source-backed scope rule: addressing `Outer[3]/Inner[4]` materializes `Outer[1..3]` and `Inner[1..4]` only beneath `Outer[3]`, not below the synthetic outer predecessors.

`DenseGroupTreeMaterializer` is the shared root for `Document.group`, `field`, `emptyField`, rendered JSON fields, copy/computation application, `asSink`, seeding, conformance, fuzz, and performance inputs. It keeps one high-water mark per `(group path, concrete parent repetitions)`, so sequential construction emits each required group row once rather than rescanning `1..n` for every placement. The exact work guard places two fields in each of 10,000 rows and observes precisely 10,001 group emissions: one root plus 10,000 line rows, all distinct. This is an algorithmic assertion, not a timing threshold. Malformed coordinate refusal remains at the existing `Document.build` boundary; the materializer does not invent a broader client-side check.

Canonical Document JSON remains unchanged. Its array already carries every position, including `{}` for an empty row, and ingress already reconstructs all of them. The fix changes the public placement/asSink seam and its in-repo consumers to match the runtime tree those same placements produce in A12.

*Locked by:* `laws/RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.sparseGroupAndFieldPlacementsMaterializeV2PredecessorRows` across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter; `DocumentPlacementsSnapshotTest` on JVM and Node for ordered transport state, nested parent scoping, and exact linear work. Removing predecessor materialization makes both sparse differential rows red; replacing the high-water suffix with a repeated `1..n` scan violates the exact work count.

### IF277: nested validation iteration projects implicit child row 1 without changing stored structure (FIXED 2026-08-09; graduated from IG118) <a id="if277"></a>

Validation rule iteration has a distinct row domain. For every existing repeatable parent iteration row, a nested repeatable scope with no concrete child contributes one implicit child at coordinate 1. The projection composes recursively across any nesting depth; an absent outermost repeatable group still contributes no row. On `/Root/Outer[3]/Middle[2]/Inner[4]`, the kernel's `Qty < 5` rule fires at implicit `Outer[1]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]`, `Outer[2]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]`, and `Outer[3]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]`, plus concrete `Outer[3]/Middle[2]/Inner[1..4]`. Before the fix, the interpreter evaluated only the four concrete Inner rows.

The projection belongs only to validation iteration. It does not mutate the V2-effective tree from [IF276](#if276). `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)`, starred group/index operands, mandatory and `uniqueIndex` auto-checks, parallel identity, and computation all remain bounded to stored rows. `RepetitionNotUnique` deliberately consumes validation iteration rows because it is itself a validation relation: equal filled ancestor keys plus optional-empty implicit child keys duplicate and emit OMISSION at the implicit pointers. A three-level consumer matrix separates every domain; a computation assigning a constant to `Inner/Qty` produces only the four concrete target outcomes.

`Document.iterationRowsByGroup` is the recursive validation projection. It groups concrete child rows by parent prefix once, fills only missing branches with all-1 lower coordinates, and memoizes the result per group. `validationContextsByGroup` and `storedContextsByGroup` keep the two domains explicit. The anchored caller inventory is finite: `IterationScopes` and `RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator` use validation contexts; `AutoChecks` and `ComputationEngine` use stored contexts; star/group and parallel consumers use their existing stored-row indexes directly. This prevents a future convenience merge from silently materializing identities or computed targets.

The same sweep corrected a stale owner claim: earlier `ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeDiffTest` controls used positive child guards and inferred “zero evaluations” from silence. An implicit empty row makes those guards false too, so the controls never discriminated the mechanism. Their live claim is now deepest-scope/per-parent resolution and positive-guard silence; the empty-as-zero row is the mechanism discriminator.

*Locked by:* `laws/RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.missingNestedBranchesContributeOneImplicitChildRowPerRepeatableLevel`, `.implicitRowsAreValidationAndRnuContextsButNotStoredGroupOrAutoCheckRows`, and `.computationsRemainBoundedToConcreteNestedRows`, all tri-engine; multiplatform `ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeTest.anImplicitChildIsEvaluatedAtCoordinateOne`; the corrected `ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeDiffTest` positive-guard controls. Replacing validation rows with stored rows loses the implicit fire/RNU outputs; routing stored consumers through validation rows invents mandatory/computation/group results and makes the same exact-set law red.

### IF278: a same-path composite `RepetitionNotUnique` key has an order-independent row domain (FIXED 2026-08-09; graduated from IG119) <a id="if278"></a>

The kernel admits composite RNU keys at different depths on one group hierarchy path. Authored order does not choose the row domain, with or without an ancestor `@From` scope. On `/Root/Outer[]/Middle[]/Inner[]`, the ancestor-first tuple `(Outer/Marker, Inner/Optional)` and its descendant-first reversal both emit OMISSION duplicates at the implicit `Outer[1..2]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]/Optional` rows when those two outer rows share `Marker=D`; adding `@From Outer` preserves that result, while changing the second marker to `E` makes all four rules silent. Dynamic Groovy and generated Java agree exactly. Before the fix, the interpreter executed only the descendant-first spelling and reported the ancestor-first rule unsupported because its plan used the first key's immediate parent as the key group.

`RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator.keyRowGroup` now derives one order-independent owner: the deepest immediate key parent after verifying that every key parent lies on the same ancestor chain. The default reference-scope prefix is derived from that owner too, so no remaining plan decision reads `keys.first()`. This is deliberately narrower than choosing the deepest group from arbitrary inputs: keys in parallel branches remain kernel-rejected (`MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH`) and a hand-built interpreter model degrades loudly at the same topology boundary.

*Locked by:* `laws/RepetitionNotUniqueKeyOrderDiffTest.samePathCompositeKeyOrderDoesNotChangeTheDuplicateRelation` across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter, with both authored orders and the distinct-marker non-firing control; `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest.repetitionNotUniqueKeysMustLieOnOnePath` for kernel rejection of parallel key paths; multiplatform `PreparedPlanSupportTest` for the interpreter's loud parallel-path boundary. Restoring either `keys.first()` use makes the ancestor-first differential row red; removing the ancestry check makes the prepared-plan control red.

### IF279: a cross-engine N/4N growth factor ranks engines by fixed-cost share, so the leanest engine loses it deterministically (FIXED 2026-08-11) <a id="if279"></a>

`pairedScenarioGrowth` accepted a run only when the interpreter's median within-pair N/4N growth was no worse than both dynamic Groovy's and generated Java's. The 2026-08-11 nightly gate failed `Number index compute` on that bar with `interpreter growth 3.94x must be no worse than Groovy 3.59x and Java 3.86x` while the interpreter was **9.6× cheaper per row** than generated Java and pulling further ahead with scale (ratioJava 0.25 at N=500 → 0.16 at N=2,000). The bar, not the engine, was wrong.

Decompose `time = fixed + perUnit·N`. At a 4× step the measured factor is exactly `4 - 3f`, where `f` is the fixed cost's share of the small-scale reading. That model reproduces all three of the failing run's factors from its own medians:

| engine | N=500 | N=2,000 | derived fixed cost | `f` | `4 - 3f` | measured |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dynamic Groovy | 36.414 ms | 126.815 ms | 6.28 ms | 17.2 % | 3.48 | 3.59 |
| generated Java | 30.460 ms | 119.347 ms | 0.83 ms | 2.7 % | 3.92 | 3.86 |
| interpreter | 3.148 ms | 12.396 ms | 0.065 ms | 2.1 % | 3.94 | 3.94 |

**What that table is and is not** (sharpened 2026-08-11 after review): substituting the derived `F = (4·T_small − T_large)/3` back into `4 − 3f` yields `T_large/T_small` identically, so the column is the ratio-of-medians rewritten, not an independent fit — it *demonstrates* the decomposition rather than corroborating it, and its residual against the "measured" column is simply median-of-within-pair-ratios versus ratio-of-medians. The mechanism also holds only where every engine is linear over the step: on Apple Silicon the kernel strategies measure 6.35×/6.60×, which the model cannot represent at all (`f` would be negative). The per-unit repair is right regardless of that scope, and the discriminating evidence for it is the per-row decomposition, not this table.

So when all three engines are linear over the step, "interpreter factor ≤ both kernel factors" reduces to "**the interpreter must carry the largest fixed-cost share of the three**". It inverts the goal (the only way to satisfy it is to get fatter), it is unreachable by scaling better, and passing it was never evidence: on Apple Silicon the same commit measures the kernel strategies *super*-linearly (Groovy 6.35×, Java 6.60× against the interpreter's 3.85×), which masks the inversion entirely. That is why [IF275](#if275)'s 2026-08-09 gate recorded the factors as "no worse than either kernel strategy" and the identical code failed on a hosted runner two days later. [IF205](#if205) had already established the dilution mechanism on the synthesized depth lane and explicitly parked the guard change; this is that change, and it lands for both callers at once because both share the harness.

The comparison is now **per-unit cost** — the slope `(largeMs - smallMs) / (large - small)`, which is scale-free and fixed-cost-free, so it compares across engines. `PairedAcceptance` refuses a run where the interpreter's slope exceeds any kernel strategy's, and refuses a non-positive or non-finite slope as a degenerate measurement rather than treating a flat curve as a fast engine. Because the interpreter holds ~10× of slope headroom here, that comparison cannot see an algorithmic blowup, so the catastrophe bar is kept as a separate, **self-referential** check: the interpreter's own N/4N growth against `SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX`, measured on the **CPU clock** (the 2026-08-10 contention correction) and re-measured before failing. The kernel strategies' own factors stay in the returned curve as reported log values, gated by nothing.

*Locked by:* `PairedAcceptanceTest` on scripted curves — `theLeanestEngineMayHaveTheHighestGrowthRatio` replays the failing run's exact medians and asserts both that the interpreter's *factor* is the worst of the three and that acceptance passes, so reinstating a factor comparison turns it red; `anInterpreterThatCostsMorePerUnitThanJavaAloneIsRefused` and `losingToBothStrategiesNamesBoth` prove the new bar fires and names what it lost to; `aQuadraticInterpreterKeepsTheLowestSlopeSoTheFloorIsASeparateCheck` pins the slope rule's blind spot and that the floor covers it; the degenerate-curve cases prove a flat engine is refused. `GrowthFloorRetryTest` continues to own the floor's median-of-re-measurements acceptance rule.

### IF280: a committed per-runner-class anchor is the only bar that sees a uniform slowdown (SHIPPED 2026-08-11; graduated from IG120) <a id="if280"></a>

The 2026-08-11 blind-band audit measured what this estate can actually detect: every bar was a catastrophe floor with 2.4×–3800× of slack, and the 18 interpreter-only growth lanes were **asymptotic-only** — a growth ratio is unchanged by a uniform `k×` slowdown at every `k`, so an interpreter that got twice as slow everywhere passed the entire nightly gate. The tightest bar that would have caught 2.1× (`MIN_KERNEL_ADVANTAGE`) never runs in CI.

A portable per-scenario budget cannot close that. Comparing the same 17 gate observations on Apple Silicon and a hosted x64 runner, **absolute** CPU medians drift `0.91×–2.45×`; **normalizing by another interpreter scenario in the same JVM** (`B1@5000`) leaves `0.78×–2.10×`, so it buys nothing — constructs respond differently, `AddDays` being 2.45× slower on the runner while `complexRealWorld` was 0.91×; and the **kernel-advantage** normalizer, the best of the three at 2.07×, carries 1.57× of single-machine run-to-run noise inside that figure. Any budget honestly derived from those numbers trips at ~3×, which the existing `beats both` bar already does.

The drift is *between* runner classes, so the fix is to stop crossing them. `PerfAnchors` compares each accepted median with a number committed for **this** class in `adapter/src/test/resources/perf/anchors.json`. The tolerance is `1.8× + 1 ms`, and its basis is the **envelope of every anchored observation across every available run**: replaying a third isolated full run against the committed block gives `0.92×–1.36×` over all 36 observations, the widest being `NDV@20000` at 45.82 → 62.30 ms. *(Corrected 2026-08-11, same day: this first said `1.01×–1.16×` from two runs of `B1@5000` and set the factor to 1.5. That was falsified by the third run's own log, where 1.5 would have left `NDV@20000` 10 % of margin — a healthy gate one contended nightly from red. The absolute term still keeps `wideGroups@50`, anchored at 1.13 ms, from flapping.)* The set includes one **wall-clock** lane, `COLD first-call (startup)`, whose widest observation is 1.26×; the small wall-clock *fixed-size comparison rows* move up to 1.54× and are deliberately not anchored.

Four properties make it a guard rather than a report:

- **It costs nothing when it passes.** `assertGrowthBelowCatastropheFloor` now returns the estimate acceptance was decided on, so the anchor reads those exact medians instead of taking a fresh sample.
- **A breach re-measures with the observation's own estimator** — a median over `GROWTH_PAIRS` samples, not one sample — and is accepted on the median of the attempts. The ratified growth-floor discipline, so one contended slice cannot fail a healthy lane while a real slowdown reproduces; taking a median across samples of different variance would not be that rule.
- **It asserts only where the measurement is valid.** An absolute-time bar under parallel forks is a coin flip, so it is armed by `-Dperf.anchor.lane`, which only the dedicated single-fork `perfGateTest` sets; a broad `:adapter:test -PrunPerf` records its observations and announces that it asserted nothing.
- **The two ways of being unanchored are told apart.** A runner class with no block at all is bootstrap: reported, never failed. A key missing *inside* an anchored class fails under `-Dperf.anchor.required=true`, which the isolated `perfGateTest` lane sets **unconditionally** — a new lane, or a scenario whose fingerprint changed and took its anchor with it, is exactly how a bar silently disappears. *(Amended 2026-08-11, same day: this was `CI != null || -PperfAnchorRequired`, which put the failure on the one runner nobody watches interactively. A fingerprint change keeps the observation **count** intact, so `PerfAnchorCoverageGuardTest` cannot see it either, and the local gate printing a note while the nightly went red was a defect rather than a courtesy. Verified harmless on the current tree without a gate run: no lane label and no scenario-content source changed since the block was authored at `4ea0bf3a`, and the last filtered local run's six keys are all in it.)*
- **The anchor is committed, not rolling, and cannot be partially deleted.** A cache-backed baseline would let `K` successive within-tolerance degradations compound into an unbounded one; a committed number only moves when someone moves it, and `scripts/perf-anchor.sh` makes that a reviewable diff belonging in the same commit as the change that made the cost legitimately higher. That script **refuses** a refresh whose input does not measure every lane **the anchor file** carries: a lane whose growth floor breached contributes no observation, and the nightly uploads its record precisely when the run breached, so the documented recovery path is the one most likely to be fed a partial run — and a wholesale block replacement from one would silently drop the rest. *(Amended 2026-08-11, same day: the rule first compared only against the keys **that block** already had, which is off by exactly one case — a **new** runner class, where there is no previous block to compare with. Rehearsing the IG120 step against a 34-of-36 input duly wrote an under-covered block with no complaint. Keys name the lane, scenario fingerprint, scale, engine and clock and never the machine, so the other anchored classes' key sets are the right denominator, and they cover both cases: the previous block's keys are a subset of them.)* `PerfAnchorResourceTest` additionally guards the committed file's shape on every push, because a renamed block reads as bootstrap, a renamed `observations` member reads as an anchored-but-empty class, and a non-numeric value degrades to a 1 ms ceiling.

**What it costs in detection.** A uniform regression below the factor on a large lane still passes, and more on the small lanes where the slack dominates: at the tightened `1.65` the current blocks span `1.65×`–`3.54×` of the anchor. This is a bar where none existed, not a 10–30 % sensitivity. *(Amended 2026-08-11: this paragraph also said the missing-key failure was unreachable in the nightly because CI had no committed block. The hosted `linux-amd64-ci` block was authored the same day from three sequential dispatched runs, so both classes are anchored and both carry the missing-key refusal.)*

**A block belongs to a MACHINE, and that is now a check rather than a convention (2026-08-12).** The environment key names an os/arch family while an absolute time is a property of the hardware, so the rule "one anchoring machine per runner class" was prose nothing could enforce; `processors` narrowed the family without closing it, since two machines of the same core count and different generations still collided. `PerfEnvironment.machine` probes the machine from the operating system — `sysctl hw.model` plus the CPU brand string, `/proc/cpuinfo`'s `model name`, `PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER` — because the JVM exposes no CPU identity at all, and every run records it. **The rule is not uniform across enforcement levels, deliberately:** where a breach fails the build an identity is *required*, so a mismatch, a block that names no machine, and a run that cannot name its own all read as bootstrap; where a breach is only flagged the runner is a VM the provider chooses per job, and demanding an identity there would leave the nightly with no absolute bar on any night the provider handed out different hardware — trading a possibly-noisy signal for none at all. So the hosted bar stays armed and states the machine it ran on, which is what lets a flag be attributed later. **Re-open trigger:** if the recorded hosted models show the runner class spans several machines, decide whether that block is a bar or only a trend line.

*Amended 2026-08-13, closing the two loose ends a cold review left in this mechanism — and they do **not** get the same disposition, because the two consumers have different jobs.* **The authoring path had a hole a truthiness test cannot see:** `perf-anchor.sh` refused a header with no `machine`, but the probe emits the literal `"unknown"` (`MachineIdentity.UNKNOWN_MODEL`) when the OS exposes no model string — reachable on **linux-aarch64**, whose `/proc/cpuinfo` carries `CPU implementer`/`CPU part` and no `model name` — and `not header.get("machine")` passed that straight through. The block it would author is **worse than one with no machine at all**: `MachineIdentity.check` falls to its *mismatch* arm, so the lane never asserts *and* reports "captured on unknown ×N and this run measured on …", which reads as a machine swap rather than as a block that was authored wrong. It is refused now, with its own corrective, since re-measuring reproduces a sentinel where it fixes an absence. **The rendering path gets disclosure instead of refusal:** `perf-history.sh` grouped by environment alone and omitted `machine` from its header, so the one surface where a human compares absolute numbers across runs rested on the assumption this finding disproved — but copying the refusal would be wrong. Splitting the table on `machine` cuts the darwin series in two, because **17 of the 28 committed runs predate the field**, destroying the continuity a trend table exists for to guard against a machine change that probably never happened; the 28-run window shows the 2026-08-12 optimizations as an unbroken `−16 %`/`−17 %` on `CO-correlation`, which a split would have halved. So the header names the machine, a table whose runs name *different* machines says so once, and an **unrecorded** machine is disclosed as `?` and never counted as a difference — treating absence as an identity would print the warning on every invocation over the real record, which is how a warning stops being read. **The third caveat is deliberately NOT fixed:** the hosted block's `jdk` reads `21.0.11,21.0.12` because the script joins differing JDKs rather than refusing them, and making that an identity field like `machine` and `commit` would be an invented refusal — `nightly-performance.yml` pins `java-version: 21`, which resolves to whatever Temurin patch is current at dispatch time, so the fold is the runner's choice, and no measurement here says a patch bump moves an absolute time. A refusal would make the hosted block unauthorable on any night a patch lands mid-window, in exchange for nothing measured. The join is honest disclosure of a fold the script cannot avoid, and it self-clears whenever three runs land on one patch. **Owner ruling, 2026-08-13: the 21.0.11-vs-21.0.12 difference is irrelevant** — so this is settled rather than merely unmeasured, and `jdk` stays a recorded condition, never an identity field. Re-open only if a JDK *major* is ever folded into one block, which is a different quantity.

**The band has a LOWER edge too, because an anchor stops describing the code and nothing noticed (2026-08-12).** An observation below `0.6 × anchor − slack` now reports `[perf][STALE]`, naming how far off the anchor is and how to refresh it. This is the one failure mode a fingerprint cannot catch — the scenario did not change, the code under it got faster — and it had already happened twice: the 2026-08-12 refresh found `PF-framed` at `0.36×` of its recorded number and the cache-key change below moved 34 of 36 lanes, seven below `0.6×`. A ceiling at `2.8×` the real cost is a bar that cannot fail. It reports and never fails, because the usual cause is a legitimate optimization and a red until someone refreshes would make green mean "nobody sped anything up lately".

**The tightening this finding's own tolerance paragraph promised: `1.8 → 1.65` (2026-08-12), and the quantity that decides it is not the one to reach for.** Both blocks are now a median of three, which was the named prerequisite. A breach is `observed > anchor × factor + slack`, so what a factor must clear is `observed/anchor − slack/anchor`, not `observed/anchor` — and that term is 3.7× the anchor on the smallest lane.
